Pulse rate and systolic and diastolic blood pressure had to be measured after the patient had been in a sitting position for at least 5 min.

(k) 12-lead ECG (single recording) was to be recorded at Visit 1 (screening) and Visit 4 (at 120 h -6/+54 h after the first study drug administration), after the patient had been in a supine position for at least 5 min, and was to be assessed in a central laboratory.

(l) A centralized laboratory was to be used for hematology, blood chemistry, and urinalysis.

(m) Patients were to receive dexamethasone on Days 1 to 4. The first Day 2 dexamethasone dose ("morning" dose) was to be administered between 24 and 26 h after the Day 1 oral dexamethasone administration. Dexamethasone for Day 2 (evening dose, within 8-12 h after morning dose), Day 3, and Day 4 were to be provided to the patient by the Investigator at Visit 3.

(n) Patients were to be asked to report date and time of onset of emetic episodes (episodes of retching or vomiting), as well as, date and time of intake of rescue medication on their diary.

(o) Adverse events were to be collected from informed consent signature up to the follow-up visit or last patient contact.
L303: (Source – PALO 15-17 Study Report Page 42)
L304: ## Dietary Restrictions/ Instructions
L305: No specific dietary restrictions/ instructions were included in the study protocol. This was not an issue from a clinical perspective.
L306: ## Concurrent Medications
L307: With respect to concurrent medications, the only medications that were specifically excluded from the study were drugs that had specific antiemetic activity. This list of medications included but was not limited to 5-HT3 receptor antagonists, NK1 receptor antagonists, benzamides, phenothiazines, benzodiazepines, butyrophenones, anticholinergics, antihistamines, domperidone, mirtazapine, olanzapine, prescribed cannabinoids, and over-the-counter antiemetics, cold, or allergy medications, and the exclusion criteria specified that these medications could not be taken within 24 hours prior to the start of the reference HEC administration on Day 1.
L308: ## Treatment Compliance
L309: A patient was compliant with therapy if he/she took all study drugs (including dexamethasone) provided in the clinical study medication kit.
L310: ## Rescue Medication
L311: Rescue medication was defined as any antiemetic medication taken for treatment of emesis (vomiting, retching) and nausea in the 0-120 hours from start of reference chemotherapy administration. It was permitted on an as-needed basis to alleviate established, refractory, or persistent nausea or vomiting. Rescue medication was not allowed for prevention of emesis, nor to increase the expected anti-emetic effects of the study medication. The Investigator was to be provided with enough rescue medication, e.g., metoclopramide tablets, to be distributed to the patient on an as-needed basis. However, the Investigator was authorized to use an alternative rescue medication, based on his/her judgment. The Investigator was to ensure that rescue medication was made available to patients whenever needed. NK1 receptor antagonists were not allowed and 5-HT3 receptor antagonists were not to be used as rescue medications. Commercial palonosetron (Aloxi®, Onicit®, Paloxi®) was not allowed.
L312: ## Subject Completion, Discontinuation, or Withdrawal
L313: **Completion:** A patient will be defined as having “completed” the study if he/she completes Visit 5 (either on-site or phone contact).
L314: **Discontinuation:** Termination of the study before Visit 5 (or before the relative phone contact) will be considered as a premature discontinuation.
L315: **Replacement:** Patients who discontinued after randomization were not to be replaced.
L316: **Follow-up Procedures and/or Assessments:** If a subject discontinued from the study after treatment with the investigational product, the Investigator must try his/her best to contact the subject within 20(±2) days after the first treatment on Day 1 (i.e., at approximately the same date when Visit 5 was to be scheduled), in view of completing the safety assessment by collecting information that may be suggestive of AEs.
L317: ## Missing Data
L318: Any patients who do not provide data about occurrence of emetic episodes and rescue medication intake or do not provide a full set of these data throughout the 24 hours after HEC administration will be considered as treatment failures (i.e., non-responders) for the primary efficacy analysis.
L319: ## Study Endpoints
L320: For a discussion of how the primary endpoint for PALO 15-17 was determined, please refer to the regulatory history discussion in Section 3.2.
L321: ## Primary Endpoint
L322: The primary endpoint for this study was:
L323: &gt; the proportion of patients with a complete response (CR) in the acute phase of CINV.
L324: CR in the acute phase is defined as:
L325: &gt; **no emetic episode and no rescue medication** during the **0-24 hours** after the start of HEC administration on Day 1.
L326: ## Secondary Endpoints
L327: The secondary endpoints were the proportion of patients with CR during the delayed and overall phases, the proportion of patients with no emetic episodes during the acute, delayed, and overall phases, and the proportion of patients with no rescue medications during the acute, delayed, and overall phases.
L328: ## Definition of Emetic Episode
L329: An emetic episode was defined as one or more continuous vomits (expulsion of stomach contents through the mouth) or retches (an attempt to vomit that is not productive of stomach contents). Emetic episodes were considered distinct if separated by absence of vomiting and retching for at least 1 minute. All patients were asked to document in patient diaries (one for the acute phase and one for the delayed phase) all details about their emetic episodes during the 120 hours after the start of administration of the reference HEC (0-24 hours in the diary for acute phase and &gt;24 to 120 hours in the one for delayed phase). These details included each episode of retching or vomiting, date of each retching or vomiting episode, and exact time of onset of each retching or vomiting episode. Each emetic episode, as well as date and time of occurrence, was then documented on the eCRF (electronic case report forms) in accordance with the patient diary records.
L330: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L331: ## Definitions of Analysis Populations
L332: The following definitions of analysis populations were used:
L333: - &gt; “All Randomized Patients”: “All Randomized Patients” includes all patients who have been randomized to treatment.
- &gt; Full Analysis Set (FAS): The Full Analysis Set (FAS) includes all patients who have been randomized to treatment and received HEC regimen and active study drug (including partial infusion). Following the intent to treat principle, patients were assigned to the study treatment group per the treatment to which they have been randomized. FAS was to be used for demography, other baseline characteristics, for primary efficacy analysis, and all efficacy analyses.
- &gt; Per-Protocol (PP) Population: The Per-Protocol (PP) population included all patients from the FAS, who had completed the 0-24 hour study period and with no major protocol violations (i.e., those affecting the primary efficacy endpoint). All the protocol violations (e.g., wrong inclusion, poor compliance, missing diaries, forbidden
L334: concomitant medications, mis-randomizations) will be reviewed and discussed case by case during the blind data review meeting (BDRM) and decisions described in the blind data review document/minutes which will be finalized prior to database lock. Patients will be assigned to study treatment groups per the actual treatment received. PP population was also used for demography, other baseline characteristics, and for the primary efficacy analysis.
L335: ## Primary Efficacy Analysis and NI Margin
L336: To test the null hypothesis, the lower limit of the two-sided 99% CI for the difference in proportion of patients with CR in the acute phase between palonosetron IV 30-minute infusion and palonosetron IV 30-second bolus will be compared to the **pre-set noninferiority margin set at -15%**.
L337: The null hypothesis can be written as: H_0: \pi_T - \pi_C \le -\Delta
H_0 will be tested against the alternative hypothesis: H_1: \pi_T - \pi_C &gt; -\Delta
L338: Where \pi_T is the proportion of patients with CR in the test group (PALO IV 30-minute infusion), \pi_C is the proportion of patients with CR in the control group (PALO IV 30-second bolus) and \Delta is the non-inferiority margin which is fixed at 15%.
L339: ***Reviewer's Comment:*** *Per the regulatory history, the Sponsor and FDA agreed upon a -15 % non-inferiority margin with two-sided 99% confidence interval. This is based on the previous precedent of FDA's acceptance of the 15% noninferiority (NI) margin in the PALO-10-01 study from the oral Akynzeo development program. See additional discussion in the Statistical Analysis Plan – Basis for Selection of NI Margin section below.*
L340: ## Missing Data
L341: With respect to missing data, any patients who did not provide data about the occurrence of emetic episodes and rescue medication intake or that did not provide a full set of these data throughout the 24 hours after HEC administration were considered as treatment failures (i.e., non-responders) for the primary efficacy analysis. For consistency, the same approach for handling missing data as described for the primary efficacy analysis was also applied to emetic episodes and rescue medication intake.
L342: ## Adjustment for Multiplicity
L343: No adjustment for multiplicity was made in the study. All secondary efficacy endpoints were intended to provide supportive evidence related to the primary objective or additional clinical characterization of treatment effect, the results on these were interpreted in only a descriptive manner.
L344: ## Basis for Selection of NI Margin
L345: 21 CFR 314.126(a)(2)(iv) states the following:
L346: "If the intent of the trial is to show similarity of the test and control drugs, the report of the study should assess the ability of the study to have detected a difference between treatments. Similarity of test drug and active control can mean either that both drugs were effective or that neither was effective. The analysis of the study should explain why the drugs should be considered effective in the study, for example, by reference to results in previous placebo-controlled studies of the active control drug."
L347: The Guidance "Non-Inferiority Clinical Trials to Establish Effectiveness" states the following:11 "The goal of the study is to show that the effect of the test drug (T) is not inferior to the effect of the active control (C) by a specified amount, called the NI margin, or M."
L348: For anti-emetics, the Applicant notes the following:

"Ideally, the margin should be identified based on past experience in placebo-controlled studies. However, for many years, the use of placebo antiemetics has not been considered ethical in patients undergoing HEC. Consequently, there are no CINV studies directly comparing IV palonosetron and placebo."
L349: The Applicant notes that in Study PALO-99-05 (trial for the Aloxi IV program; NDA 21372), a "modeled historical placebo" (i.e., literature-based meta-analysis (PAL0-01-23)) was used. This Reviewer notes that this approach was deemed acceptable based on the Clinical Review of that NDA.12 The Clinical Review under NDA 21372 stated the following regarding PALO-01-23:

"Since the use of placebo is not ethically acceptable in the CINV subject population, a literature-based meta-analysis (PAL0-01-23) was performed to provide historical placebo control data. Since dolasetron performed similarly to the modeled historical dolasetron and far better than in the modeled historical placebo, the applicant demonstrated validity of the trial for the 24 hour end-point."
L350: The Applicant states the following regarding the "modeled historical placebo" (PALO-01-23) (***emphasis added***):

"Data were collected on potential important covariates (e.g., treatment, age, sex, naivety, coadministration of corticosteroids, country/region of study sites location, single versus multicenter, type practice setting, analysis population, dosing route, distribution of cancer treated, chemotherapy emetogenicity, specific chemotherapeutic agents used) which might have predicted CR. The meta-analysis database was comprised of 78 treatment arms from published trials and included data from 7,274 patients (the above-mentioned studies using placebo were part of the database as well). Using this database, a logistic regression was performed to identify which covariates were predictive of CR. Using the relevant covariates from the meta-analysis, an equation was produced to determine the predicted CR rate for any given patient population (i.e., any given combination of relevant covariates). Relevant covariates from PALO-99-05 were entered into this equation (model) to produce the predicted historical placebo CR in the acute phase for the patient population of that study. The effect of the ***modeled historical placebo estimated by this equation indicated a proportion of patients with CR in the acute phase of 11.8% (95% CI: 8.7% to 15.8%).***"
L351: 11 https://www.fda.gov/downloads/Drugs/Guidances/UCM202140.pdf (accessed December 11, 2017)
L352: 12 https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2003/21-372_Alox.cfm (accessed December 10, 2017)
L353: The Applicant states the following regarding the choice for M1 (*emphasis added*):
L354: The entire effect of the active control drug assumed to be present in this non-inferiority trial (M1) is estimated by the difference between the lower limit of the 95% CI for the IV palonosetron 0.25 mg (i.e., 52.4%) and the upper limit of the 95% CI for the modeled historical placebo (i.e., 15.8%). Therefore, the non-inferiority margin for the current study should not exceed 36.6% (M1).
L355: The Applicant states the following regarding the choice for M2 (*emphasis added*):
L356: Based also on clinical judgment, it was considered that a substantial portion of M1 (at least 50%) should be preserved, leading to a value of about 18%. To be as conservative as possible, a further additional adjustment is applied to reflect uncertainties and the non-inferiority margin is reduced to 15% (M2, the largest clinically acceptable difference of the test drug compared to the control drug).
L357: In addition to the precedent of Trial PALO-99-05 (trial conducted for Aloxi IV with a 15 % NI margin and a 97.5 % confidence interval) (see Clinical Review of NDA 21372),13 this Reviewer notes that the NI Margin of 15% (99% confidence interval) was used in Trial PALO-10-01 (efficacy of oral palonosetron 0.50 mg in the HEC setting using palonosetron 0.25 mg IV bolus as active control drug) for Akynzeo capsules (see Clinical Review of NDA 205718). The Applicant also cited other precedents where the 15% NI Margin was used, which included: (1) Dolasetron study MCPR0031 (versus ondansetron, 95 % CI) and study 73147-3-S-093 (versus granisetron, 95 % CI); (2) Palonosetron MEC studies 99-03 and 99-04 (both with 97.5 % CI; and (3) trial for Sancuso granisetron transdermal patch (95% CI).
L358: ***Reviewer's Comment:*** *This Clinical Reviewer agrees with the 15% NI Margin with the use of a two-sided 99% confidence interval and the basis for selection of this NI Margin using the "modeled historical placebo" (PALO-01-23) given the precedents described above.*
L359: ## Interim Analysis
L360: No interim analysis was performed for this study.
L361: ## Planned Subgroup Analyses
L362: It was felt that sex and the country in which the study was being performed would affect the primary endpoint. As such, the subgroups that were explored included:
L363: - Sex- (Male, Female)
- Country- (Belarus, Bosnia and Herzegovina, Bulgaria, Georgia, Greece, Hungary, Lithuania, Romania, Russia)
L364: Please refer to the statistical review for a more complete discussion involving the statistical analysis plan.
L365: 13 https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/nda/2003/21-372_Alox.cfm (accessed December 10, 2017)
L366: ## Protocol Amendments
L367: The Sponsor submitted two protocol amendments. The first, dated October 19, 2015, was submitted to correct minor inconsistencies between the synopsis and the rest of the protocol. None of these revisions changed the essential aspects of the protocol. The second amendment, dated January 25, 2016 was implemented to update general information to current status, to specify in detail time windows for study drug, additional non-investigational study drug, and reference chemotherapy administration, vital signs and 12-lead ECG measurements, to align the wording related to the study drug storage conditions with the text reported on study drug label, to unequivocally clarify the meaning of the wording “first study drug”/“post-dose” in some sections, and to correct minor typing errors. Here again, these revisions did not change the essential aspects of the protocol.
L368: ## Data Quality and Integrity: Sponsor's Assurance
L369: The study was performed per the CRO's and Sponsor's SOPs. These included study conduct and the archiving of essential documents in accordance with ICH-GCP guidelines.
L370: ## Monitoring
L371: The study was monitored by the CRO's adequately qualified and trained clinical monitors on a regular basis throughout the study period, to ensure the proper conduct of the clinical trial. The purposes of the clinical trial monitoring were to verify that the rights and well-being of study patients were protected, that the reported trial data were accurate, complete, and verifiable against the source documents, and that the study was conducted in accordance with the current protocol, GCP guidelines, and applicable regulatory requirements. During the monitoring visits, monitors were to verify the following information (including but not limited to): patient informed consent, patient's eligibility, safety data and reporting, quality of source documents, and eCRF data against patient's medical records. If inconsistencies were identified, the corresponding corrections to the eCRF data were to be made by the Investigator. Monitors were to also check patient compliance, accrual, drug handling, including dispensing procedures, and accountability logs, delegation of responsibilities within the Investigator's team, relevant communications with family doctors, if any, ancillary equipment and facilities, including refrigerators, local labs, etc. The Investigator and other site staff involved in the study had to allocate enough time to the monitor during these visits.
L372: A total of 16 on-site audits were performed by the CRO (9 audits) as well as by the Sponsor (7 audits) to ensure that the study followed GCP, applicable regulatory requirements, and the study protocol. Additional audit activities may also have been conducted after study completion.
L373: In addition, Regulatory Authorities could conduct on-site inspections (during the study or after its completion). If a Regulatory Authority notified the Investigator of an inspection or visits the site unannounced for purposes of conducting an inspection, the Investigator had to inform the study Sponsor and CRO immediately. The Investigator was to make all efforts to facilitate the conduct of the audits and inspections, giving access to all necessary facilities, data and documents.
L374: ## Laboratory Accreditation
L375: All samples were sent to a qualified central laboratory for analyses.
L376: ## Data Reporting and Management
L377: Electronic case report forms (eCRFs) were to be used for recording patient's study data using a validated web-based Electronic Data Capture (EDC) system. It was the responsibility of the Investigator to ensure that the eCRFs were properly and filled in. The eCRFs had to be completed for all patients who were included in the study.
L378: Each authorized site personnel were assigned a login and password to enter the EDC system via a secure network.
L379: Data Clarification Forms (DCFs) were to be used to validate/clarify data entered on an eCRF page.
L380: Discrepancies raised were to be reviewed online to determine the corrective action needed. Changes were to be made directly by authorized site staff.
L381: Patients' source documentation (i.e., hospital charts) were to be maintained at the clinic. Source data to be recorded directly onto the eCRFs were to be communicated in advance.
L382: Paper patient diaries covering the acute and delayed phases of CINV were to be used to collect emetic episodes (i.e., retching and vomiting episodes), and rescue medication intake.
L383: The medical (hospital) files of trial patients were to be retained in accordance with national legislation (and in accordance with the maximum period of time permitted by the hospital (or institution/private practice)).
L384: ## 6.1.2. Study Results
L385: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L386: Palo 15-17 was conducted in compliance with good clinical practices with the CFR regarding protection of human subjects, Institutional Review Boards, and obligations of clinical investigators.
L387: This study was also in accordance with the European Union (EU) clinical Trial Directive 2001/20/EC, the United States (US) Code of Federal Regulations 313.32, and the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) E6 Guideline for Good Clinical Practice (GCP) and ICH E2A Guidance on Clinical Safety Management.
L388: ## Financial Disclosure
L389: A review of this study revealed no financial conflicts of interest. Please see financial disclosure
L390: forms in Section 13.2. The applicant provided adequate documentation to show that there were no financial conflicts of interest between themselves and the 274 Investigators that participated in this study.
L391: ## Patient Disposition
L392: A total of 441 patients were randomized.
L393: The 441 patients randomized to the study were distributed between treatment groups as follows: 225 patients randomized to palonosetron 0.25 mg as a 30-minute IV infusion (“infusion group”) and 216 patients randomized to 0.25 mg as a 30-second IV bolus (“bolus group”).
L394: Four-hundred forty (440) (99.8%) patients were treated (225 [100%] patients in the infusion group, and 215 [99.5%] in the bolus group); and 424 (96.1%) patients completed the study (218 [96.9%] patients in the infusion group, and 206 [95.4%] in the bolus group).
L395: Of the 17 (3.9%) patients who withdrew from the study prematurely, 13 (2.9%) patients died (6 [2.7%] patients in the infusion group, and 7 [3.2%] in the bolus group); 2 (0.5%) patients were lost to follow-up (one patient in each treatment group); and 2 (0.5%) patients chose to withdraw (both patients in the bolus group).

L400: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L401: Overall, 15 (3.4%) patients in the FAS had major protocol deviations: 11 (4.9%) in the infusion group and 4 (1.9%) in the bolus group. The most common (affecting 9 [2.0%] patients) major protocol deviation referred to “dexamethasone not administered within the time window on the morning of Day 2”. All remaining major protocol deviations either referred to a HEC dosage
L402: outside of the reference range (3 [0.7%] patients) or to the use of medication with potential anti-emetic effects within 24 h after starting the reference HEC (3 [0.7%] patients). Please see the table below.

L411: ## Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)
L412: At study entry, primary cancer location was similar between treatment groups, with the most frequent primary cancer location being lung and respiratory tract (51.1%), followed by “other” (19.8%), and head and neck (17.3%). Median time since diagnosis was 42 day for both treatment groups.
L413: The extent of cancer at study entry was categorized as either primary, metastatic, or locally recurrent, with no notable differences between the treatment groups in the proportions of patients assigned to each category. The majority of patients had primary (52.0%) or metastatic (43.4%) disease. For metastatic cancer, the most commonly reported sites for metastases were lymph nodes (23.9%), “other” (13.2%), and lung (12.0%). A small proportion of patients (4.5%) had locally recurrent cancer. L434: ## Prior Medications
L435: Considering the Safety Population, use of prior medications was recorded for 42.7% of patients in the study.
L436: The most commonly reported classes of medications (ATC level 4 headings) used prior to study entry were heparin group (7.0%), other opioids (6.1%), and beta blocking agents, selective (5.5%).
L437: ## Concomitant Medications
L438: Use of concomitant medications was recorded for 81.8% of patients in the study.
L439: As expected due to the specific patient population and administration of cytotoxic cancer chemotherapy, solutions producing osmotic diuresis as (mannitol) (37.7%), electrolyte solutions (19.3%), solutions affecting electrolyte balance (17.5%) and solutions for parenteral nutrition (16.6%) were very commonly reported classes of medications (ATC level 4 headings) during the present study.
L440: In addition, other commonly reported classes of medications (ATC level 4 headings) used concomitantly during the study were selective beta-blocking agents (15.9%), plain sulfonamides diuretics (furosemide and indapamide) (14.5%), heparin group (10.5%), H2-receptor antagonists (10.0%), platelet aggregation inhibitors excluding heparin (8.9%), proton pump inhibitors (8.6%), colony stimulating factors (7.5%), folic acid (7.5%), glucocorticoids (7.5%), other opioids (7.5%), serotonin (5-HT3) antagonists (7.3%), calcium (6.6%), dihydropyridine derivatives (6.4%), angiotensin II antagonists, plain (5.7%), HMG-CoA reductase inhibitors (5.7%), iron bivalent oral preparations (ferrous sulfate) (5.7%), propulsives (5.7%), and other cardiac preparations (5.5%).

L446: ## **Efficacy Results**
L447: ## **Primary Endpoint**
L448: The analysis of FAS revealed that 186 (82.7%) patients in the infusion group reported CR in the acute phase, as did 186 (86.5%) patients in the bolus group. The stratum-adjusted CMH method for difference in proportions, stratified by sex and country, with associated two sided 99% CI was used for non-inferiority testing. The difference in proportion between **the 30-minute infusion and 30-second bolus treatment groups was -3.8% (99% CI: -12.2%, 4.7%).**
L449: Since the lower limit of the two-sided 99% CI for the difference in proportions was greater (i.e., closer to zero) than the pre-defined non-inferiority margin of -15%, **non-inferiority of palonosetron 30-min infusion vs. 30-sec bolus was demonstrated.** Please see table below.
L450: Table 18 - Complete Response in the Acute Phase (FAS and PP) PALO 15-17
L451: Palonosetron 0.25 mg IV	30-min Infusion	30-sec Bolus
Full Analysis Set	N = 225	N = 215
Responder, n (%)	186 (82.7)	186 (86.5)
[95% confidence interval]a	[77.2, 87.1]	[81.3, 90.4]
CMH risk difference % (Infusion – Bolus)	-3.8 [-12.2; 4.7]
[99% CI]b	
P-valueb	p<0.001
Per-Protocol Population	N = 214	N = 211
Responder, n (%)	177 (82.7)	182 (86.3)
[95% confidence interval]a	[77.1, 87.2]	[81.0, 90.3]

[99% CI]b

L452: (Source – PALO 15-17 Study Report Page 86)
L453: ## Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
L454: The secondary endpoints of this trial did not have any bearing on the proposed indication or labeling. Intravenous palonosetron (30 sec) has not been shown to be effective in the delayed phase of HEC and currently does not carry this indication. Therefore, no meaningful conclusions can be drawn from these analyses. Intravenous palonosetron is approved for prevention of delayed phase of CINV associated with MEC. It might be reasonable to extrapolate the observation from this trial to the MEC setting, should the applicant pursue a MEC indication in the future; however, there was no prespecified noninferiority margin agreed upon for the delayed phase and there was no plan to control alpha for multiplicity to account for these additional analyses. The results for these endpoints are presented here only for descriptive purposes. (see Section 6.1.1). Please see tables below.

L466: ## Subgroup Analyses
L467: ## Sex
L468: A logistic regression model to test the interaction of treatment by sex was performed and is summarized below. No treatment-by-sex interaction was detected (p=0.409). It should be noted that the observed treatment difference was numerically greater in females than males, and the lower bound of the confidence interval for the difference was numerically lower; however, the sample size was small (only approximately 70 female patients per treatment group). L495: ## 6.2. NEPA 15-18
L496: ## 6.2.1. Study Design
L497: ## Overview and Objective
L498: NEPA 15-18 was titled “A phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, active control study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of IV pro-netupitant/ palonosetron (260mg/0.25mg) combination for the prevention of chemotherapy induced nausea and vomiting in repeated chemotherapy cycles in patients receiving highly emetogenic chemotherapy.”
L499: The primary objective of this study was to assess the safety and tolerability of a single dose of IV NEPA FDC (260 mg/0.25 mg) infused over 30 min, with oral dexamethasone, in initial and repeated cycles of HEC. The secondary objective of this study was to describe the efficacy of a single dose of IV NEPA FDC (260 mg/0.25 mg) infused over 30 min, with oral dexamethasone, during the acute (0-24 h), delayed (&gt;24-120 h) and overall (0-120 h) phases of initial and repeated cycles of HEC.
L500: ***Reviewer's Comment:** The critical aspect to understand about NEPA 15-18 is that it was designed to be a safety trial. Efficacy was a secondary objective, and the study was not designed or powered to be a non-inferiority trial. Only descriptive statistics were used in the efficacy analysis.*
L501: ## Trial Design
L502: ## Overview
L503: NEPA 15-18 was a phase 3 multicenter, multinational, randomized, active-controlled, double-blind, double-dummy, parallel group, stratified study conducted in patients receiving HEC. The study evaluated repeat dosing during 4 cycles of HEC chemotherapy.
L504: IV NEPA FDC (Akynzeo® for injection) was compared to oral NEPA FDC (Akynzeo®) as a control.
L505: ## Number of Patients
L506: The number of patients to be randomized into the study was 400 equally distributed in the two treatment groups (i.e., 200 patients/group).
L507: ## Pertinent Inclusion Criteria
L508: ## Cycle 1:
L509: - Signed written informed consent.
- Male or female patient \ge 18 years of age.
- Histologically or cytologically confirmed solid tumor malignancy.
- Naïve to cytotoxic chemotherapy. Previous biological or hormonal therapy is permitted.
- Scheduled to receive at least 4 repeated consecutive cycles of the following highly emetogenic reference chemotherapies (HEC), alone or in combination with other chemotherapeutic agents* on Day 1:
L510: - cisplatin administered as a single IV dose of \ge 70 mg/m^2
- cyclophosphamide \ge 1500 mg/m^2
- carmustine (BCNU) &gt; 250 mg/m^2
- dacarbazine (DTIC)
- mechloretamine (nitrogen mustard)
L511: *on Day 1, additional chemotherapeutic agents have to be administered after the start of the reference HEC administration and their administration must be completed no more than 6 hours after the start of reference HEC infusion. Low, minimally, or not emetogenic chemotherapies can be administered at any time from Day 2 onwards.
L512: - ECOG Performance Status of 0, 1, or 2.
- If a patient is female, she shall be: a) of non-childbearing potential or b) of childbearing potential using reliable contraceptive measures and having negative urine pregnancy test.
- Hematologic and metabolic status adequate for receiving a HEC regimen and fulfillment of the following criteria:Total Neutrophils \ge 1500/\text{mm}^3 (Standard units: \ge 1.5 \times 10^9/\text{L})
- Platelets \ge 100,000/\text{mm}^3 (Standard units: \ge 100.0 \times 10^9/\text{L})
- Bilirubin \le 1.5 \times Upper Limit of Normal (ULN)
- Liver enzymes:Without known liver metastases, Aspartate aminotransferase (AST) and Alanine aminotransferase (ALT) \le 2.5 \times ULN
- With known liver metastases, AST and ALT \le 5.0 \times ULN
- Serum Creatinine \le 1.5 m g/dL (Standard units: \le 132.6 \mu\text{MOL}/\text{L}) or Creatinine Clearance \ge 60 mL/min
- Able to read, understand, follow the study procedure and complete patient diary.
L513: ## Cycles 2 to 4:
L514: - Participation in the study during the next cycle of chemotherapy is considered appropriate by the Investigator and does not pose unwarranted risk to the patient.
- Scheduled to receive the same chemotherapy regimen as Cycle 1 or one of the reference chemotherapies as defined in Inclusion criterion #5 for Cycle 1.
- If a patient is female, she shall be: a) of non-childbearing potential or b) of childbearing
L515: potential using reliable contraceptive measures and having a negative urine pregnancy test.

4. Adequate hematologic and metabolic status according to the Investigator's opinion.
L516: ## Pertinent Exclusion Criteria
L517: ## Cycle 1:
L518: - Lactating woman.
- Active infection or uncontrolled disease except for malignancy that may pose unwarranted risks in administering the study drugs to the patient.
- Current use of illicit drugs or current evidence of alcohol abuse.
- Scheduled to receive moderately emetogenic chemotherapy or highly emetogenic chemotherapy from Day 2 to Day 5.
- Received or is scheduled to receive radiation therapy to the abdomen or the pelvis within 1 week prior to the start of the reference HEC administration on Day 1 or between Days 1 to 5.
- Any vomiting, retching, or nausea (grade \ge 1 as defined by National Cancer Institute) within 24 hours prior to the start of the reference chemotherapy administration on Day 1.
- Symptomatic primary or metastatic CNS malignancy.
- Known hypersensitivity or contraindication to 5-HT3 receptor antagonists (e.g., palonosetron, ondansetron, granisetron, dolasetron, tropisetron, ramosetron) or dexamethasone.
- Known contraindication to the IV administration of 50 mL 5% glucose solution.
- Previously received an NK-1 receptor antagonist.
- Participation in a previous clinical trial involving IV pro-netupitant or oral netupitant administered alone or in combination with palonosetron.
- Any investigational drugs (other than those given in this study) taken within 4 weeks prior to Day 1, and/or is scheduled to receive any investigational drug during the present study.
- Systemic corticosteroid therapy at any dose within 72 hours prior to the start of the reference HEC administration on Day 1. However, topical and inhaled corticosteroids are permitted.
- Scheduled to receive bone marrow transplantation and/or stem cell rescue therapy.
- Scheduled to receive any strong or moderate inhibitor of CYP3A4 or its intake within 1 week prior to Day 1.
- Scheduled to receive any of the following CYP3A4 substrates within 1 week prior to Day 1: terfenadine, cisapride, astemizole, pimozide.
- Received within 4 weeks prior to Day 1 or scheduled to receive any CYP3A4 inducer.
- Any medication with known or potential antiemetic activity within 24 hours prior to the start of the reference HEC administration on Day 1, including:5-HT3 receptor antagonists (e.g., ondansetron, granisetron, dolasetron, tropisetron, ramosetron, palonosetron)
- NK1 receptor antagonists (e.g., aprepitant or any other new drug of this class)
- benzamides (e.g., metoclopramide, alizapride)
- phenothiazines (e.g., prochlorperazine, promethazine, fluphenazine, perphenazine, thiethylperazine, chlorpromazine)
- benzodiazepines (except if the subject is receiving such medication for sleep or anxiety and has been on a stable dose for at least seven days prior to Day 1)
L519: - butyrophenones (e.g., haloperidol, droperidol)
- anticholinergics (e.g., scopolamine, with the exception of inhaled anticholinergics for respiratory disorders, e.g., ipratropium bromide)
- antihistamines (e.g., cyclizine, hydroxyzine, diphenhydramine, chlorpheniramine)
- domperidone
- mirtazapine
- olanzapine
- prescribed cannabinoids (e.g., tetrahydrocannabinol or nabilone)
- Over the Counter (OTC) antiemetics, OTC cold or OTC allergy medications
- History or predisposition to cardiac conduction abnormalities, except for incomplete right bundle branch block.
- History of Torsade de Point or known history of risk factors for Torsade de Point (heart failure, hypokalemia, family history of Long QT Syndrome).
- Severe cardiovascular diseases diagnosed within 3 months prior to Day 1 of first cycle, including myocardial infarction, unstable angina pectoris, significant valvular or pericardial disease, history of ventricular tachycardia, symptomatic Congestive Heart Failure (CHF) NYHA class III-IV, and severe uncontrolled arterial hypertension.
- Any illness or condition that, in the opinion of the Investigator, may confound the results of the study or pose unwarranted risks in administering the investigational product to the patient.
- Concurrent medical condition that would preclude administration of dexamethasone such as systemic fungal infection or uncontrolled diabetes.
L520: Cycles 2 to 4:
L521: - Lactating woman.
- Active infection or uncontrolled disease except for malignancy that may pose unwarranted risks in administering the study drugs to the patient.
- Started any of the restricted medications.
- Any vomiting, retching, or nausea (grade \ge 1 as defined by National Cancer Institute) within 24 hours prior to the start of reference chemotherapy administration on Day 1.
- Received or is scheduled to receive radiation therapy to the abdomen or the pelvis within 1 week prior to the start of the reference chemotherapy administration on Day 1 or between Days 1 to 5.
- Symptomatic primary or metastatic CNS malignancy.
L522: **Reviewer's Comments:** The inclusion/exclusion criteria for NEPA 15-18 were similar to those of PALO 15-17. There was additional language about active infection and conduction abnormalities as exclusions. In addition, there were inclusion/exclusion criteria assessments that were applied to cycle 1 with abbreviated criteria applied to cycles 2-4 (lactation, active infection/uncontrolled disease, restricted medication, vomiting, retching, or nausea grade \ge 1 as defined by the National Cancer Institute), received or scheduled radiation therapy to the abdomen or pelvis within 1 week of the start of reference chemotherapy administration, symptomatic primary or metastatic CNS malignancy. The inclusion/exclusion criteria are otherwise identical to that found in PALO 15-17. Please refer to Section 6.1.1.
L523: ## Dose Selection
L524: **Fosnetupitant Dose:** The dose of fosnetupitant in the IV NEPA FDC was selected based on the results of a relative bioavailability study in healthy volunteers (PNET-12-23). In PNET-12-23, a dose of 257 mg fosnetupitant was compared to the netupitant dose contained in oral NEPA FDC (i.e., 300 mg netupitant). Bioequivalence criteria were met (for AUC_{0-120h} and AUC_{0-\infty}). The dose of IV fosnetupitant was then rounded to 260 mg for further development of the IV NEPA FDC.
L525: **Palonosetron Dose:** The approved dose of Aloxi (palonosetron) IV for adults of 0.25 mg was used.
L526: ## Dosing Schedule
L527: **IV NEPA FDC Infusion:** The 30-minute (+/-5 minutes) IV NEPA FDC infusion (or placebo) was started 30 minutes prior to the start of the reference chemotherapy administration. The IV infusion was completed before starting chemotherapy administration.
L528: **NEPA FDC Capsule:** Oral NEPA FDC capsule (or placebo) was administered 60 minutes prior to the start of the reference chemotherapy administration.
L529: **Dexamethasone:** Oral dexamethasone administration was performed in open fashion. Dexamethasone dose and schedule were identical between test and control treatments, i.e., 12 mg oral dexamethasone once on Day 1, then 8 mg oral dexamethasone once daily on Days 2 through 4. On Day 1, oral dexamethasone (3 tablets of 4 mg, for a total daily dose of 12 mg) was taken 30 minutes prior to the start of the reference chemotherapy administration (immediately before the start of the IV NEPA FDC or placebo infusion). On Days 2 to 4, oral dexamethasone was taken in the morning (2 tablets of 4 mg, for a total daily dose of 8 mg).
L530: Please see table below for overall treatment administration schedule.
L531: Table 24 – Treatment administration schedules NEPA 15-18
L532: Group 1 (Test)	Group 2 (Control)
Day 1	1 capsule NEPA FDC placebo p.o., 60 minutes before start of reference chemotherapy administration	1 capsule NEPA FDC (300 mg netupitant/0.50 mg palonosetron), p.o., 60 minutes before start of reference chemotherapy administration
3 tablets, 4 mg dexamethasone 30 minutes before start of reference chemotherapy (immediately before the start of the IV NEPA FDC infusion)	3 tablets, 4 mg dexamethasone 30 minutes before start of reference chemotherapy (immediately before the start of the IV NEPA FDC placebo infusion)
1 vial, 260 mg pro-netupitant/ 0.25 mg palonosetron reconstituted with 50 mL 5% glucose solution and administered by IV as a 30-minute (+/- 5 minutes) infusion, starting 30 minutes before reference chemotherapy administration	1 vial, pro-netupitant/palonosetron placebo reconstituted with 50 mL 5% glucose solution and administered by IV as a 30-minute (+/- 5 minutes) infusion, starting 30 minutes before reference chemotherapy administration
Days 2-3-4	2 tablets, 4 mg dexamethasone p.o., morning	2 tablets, 4 mg dexamethasone p.o., morning
L533: (Source - NEPA 15-18 Protocol page 60)
L534: ## Randomization/Stratification
L535: Randomization was used to avoid bias in assigning treatments to patients. Randomized treatment assignments and maintenance of the blind were continued beyond initial cycle throughout all repeated cycles in this trial. Treatment assignment was managed through a static central blocked randomization stratified by sex (male, female) and country. The randomization scheme was reproducible and was prepared prior to start of the study via a computerized system by the IWRS vendor.
L536: ## Blinding
L537: The blinding of the study drugs was guaranteed by the use of identical placebos to the respective active drugs (double-dummy technique).
L538: The Investigator had the possibility to unblind the study treatment in case of an emergency, when he/she considered it essential to know what treatments the patient had received.
L539: ## Administrative Structure
L540: A Data Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) was to be convened for the evaluation of safety data in the study. The DSMB was to periodically review safety data during the conduct of the study. The DSMB was to not include employees of the sponsor and was to be comprised of two clinicians who are not investigators in the study and one statistician.
L541: Two contract research organizations (CRO) were involved in this study. PSI CRO AG, based out of Zug, Switzerland, was the clinical CRO, and [REDACTED](b) (4) based out of [REDACTED](b) (4) was the CRO for Statistics and Data Management (SDM). There were no adjudication committees.

L546: a) Day -1 is the day immediately preceding Day 1.

b) Approximately 24 hours after the oral capsule administration on Day 1.

c) 120 hours after the oral capsule administration on Day 1. If Day 6 is a holiday or a weekend day, Visit 4 may be scheduled within the two forthcoming days.

d) Day 21(\pm2), either on-site visit or phone contact. The follow-up visit of one cycle and the screening visit of the subsequent cycle may coincide.

e) Only at Cycle 1.

f) Including check of alcohol and illicit drugs consumption.

g) To be performed for females of childbearing potential within 24 hours prior to the oral capsule administration on Day 1 of each cycle. If the screening visit urine pregnancy test has been done within the 24 hours before the oral capsule administration, no urine pregnancy test is to be performed at Visit 2 (Day 1) of the referred cycle.

h) Serum pregnancy test will be performed at the central laboratory, as well as for urinalysis, hematology and blood chemistry.

i) Complete Physical examination at Visits 1 and 4, Limited Physical examination at Visit 3.

j) Vital signs assessments include: pulse rate, systolic blood pressure, and diastolic blood pressure at Visit 1 (Screening), Visit 2 (on Day 1 at pre-dose (i.e., before the oral capsule administration), after the end of IV study drug or placebo infusion (i.e., immediately [within 10 minutes] before to start the reference chemotherapy administration) and after 5 hours (\pm1 hour) from the oral capsule administration, Visit 3 (24 hours [\pm2 hours] after the oral capsule administration), and Visit 4 (120 hours [\pm54 hours] after the oral capsule administration). Pulse rate and systolic and diastolic blood pressure have to be measured after the patient has been in the sitting position for at least 5 minutes. Height has to be measured only at Visit 1 of Cycle 1. Weight has to be measured at Visit 1, Visit 2 and Visit 4.

k) 12-lead ECG (single recording) will be assessed in a central laboratory.

l) 12-lead ECG will be recorded, for each cycle, at screening, pre-dose, end of IV study drug infusion (i.e., immediately [within 10 minutes] before the start of reference chemotherapy), 5 hours (\pm1 hour) after the oral capsule administration, 24 hours [\pm2 hours], and 120 hours [from 114 hours to 174 hours, i.e., \pm54 hours] after the oral capsule administration on Day 1. 12-lead ECG (single recording) will be recorded after the patient has been in the supine position for at least 5 minutes.

m) A central laboratory will be used for urinalysis, haematology and blood chemistry analysis throughout the entire study. For study inclusion (Cycle 1), central laboratory haematological results are mandatory and should satisfy the inclusion criterion #8. For Investigator's decision about patient's inclusion in Cycles 2 to 4, local laboratory results are acceptable; nevertheless, central laboratory data will need to be collected at each cycle.

n) Patients will either receive IV NEPA FDC infusion (and placebo capsule) or oral NEPA FDC capsule (and placebo infusion) on Day 1, and dexamethasone on Days 1 to 4. Dexamethasone for Days 3 and 4 will be provided to the patient by the Investigator at Visit 3.

o) Patients will be asked to report date and time of onset of emetic episodes (episodes of retching or vomiting), date and time of intake of rescue medication and nausea intensity assessed by VAS on their diary. Intake of dexamethasone on Day 3 and Day 4 will also be recorded on patient diary.

p) Adverse Events will be collected from Informed Consent signature to the follow-up visit of last cycle or last patient contact.

q) In repeated cycles (i.e., cycles no. 2, 3 or 4), the Visit 1 may be performed from Day -7 up to study Day 1 inclusive (i.e., Visit 1 and Visit 2 may occur on the same day on study Day 1). In that case, the assessments planned at both Visit 1 and Visit 2 have to be performed only once at Day 1.
L547: (Source - NEPA 15-18 Study Report page 47)
L548: It should be noted that severity of nausea was evaluated by the patient in the patient diary for each day of the 0-120 hour interval (Days 1 to 5, inclusive), using a 100-mm horizontal visual analogue scale (VAS). The left end of the scale (0 mm) was labeled as 'no nausea' and the right end of the scale (100 mm) was labeled as 'nausea as bad as it could be'.
L549: ## Dietary Restrictions
L550: No specific dietary restrictions/ instructions were included in the study protocol. This was not an issue from a clinical perspective.
L551: ## Concurrent Medications
L552: Information on prior and concomitant medications was to be collected from 14 days prior to Day 1 of the first cycle up to the end of the study. The use of any medication for the prevention of nausea and vomiting or to increase the expected preventive effects of the study medication, or any medication with potential antiemetic effects within 24 h prior to the start of the reference chemotherapy and for the following 120 h was prohibited. A complete list of prohibited medications was listed in the exclusion criteria. The use of rescue medication was permitted.
L553: According to the protocol, at each cycle, systemic corticosteroid therapy (including but not limited to dexamethasone, hydrocortisone, methylprednisolone, or prednisolone) taken at any dose within 72 hours prior to the start of the reference chemotherapy administration on Day 1 and until 120 hours afterwards was not permitted; use of these prohibited medications during Cycle 1 was reported as a major protocol deviation (minor in subsequent cycles). Drugs that
L554: were moderate and strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were not permitted within 1 week prior to Cycle 1 Day 1; use of these prohibited medications during Cycle 1 was also considered a major protocol deviation. If the moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor was stopped 1 week or more before Day 1 of any cycle, no protocol deviation was reported. If the moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor was taken within 1 week of a repeated cycle but not within 1 week of Cycle 1 Day 1, this was reported as a minor deviation.
L555: Overall on Day 1 of Cycle 1, 387 (95.8%) patients received cisplatin (overall mean [SD] dose was 144.86 [22.98] mg). Fourteen (3.5%) patients received dacarbazine (overall mean [SD] dose was 1757.52 [391.67] mg) and 3 (0.74%) patients received cyclophosphamide (overall mean [SD] dose was 3003.33 [332.65] mg). In general, the mean (SD) doses of cisplatin, dacarbazine, and cyclophosphamide received on Day 1 of Cycles 2, 3, and 4 were similar to those on Day 1 of Cycle 1 for both overall and for each treatment group. No patient was treated with cyclophosphamide in the IV NEPA FDC group on Day 1 of Cycle 4 compared with 1 patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group, and no patient was treated with any of the remaining pre-defined reference HEC (carmustine or mechloretamine).
L556: Similar proportions of patients in each treatment group received chemotherapy concomitantly with their reference HEC up to Day 5 (inclusive) during each cycle (overall, 92.6% of patients during Cycle 1, 89.6% during Cycle 2, 88.2% during Cycle 3 and 89.5% during Cycle 4), mostly on Day 1 only (overall, 67.1% of patients during Cycle 1, 62.5% during Cycle 2, 62.3% during Cycle 3, and 65.3% during Cycle 4), with between 24.3% and 27.0% of patients overall receiving it on Days 1-5. Overall, between 22.6% and 30.4% of patients overall received chemotherapy after Day 5. Overall, the most commonly administered concomitant chemotherapy agents up to Day 5 (inclusive) in each cycle were etoposide (between 24.9% and 25.7% of patients), cyclophosphamide (between 15.1% and 19.7%), fluorouracil (between 11.1% and 11.8%), gemcitabine (between 9.2% and 10.9%), vinorelbine (between 7.0% and 8.4%), vinorelbine tartrate (between 5.9% and 7.9%), doxorubicin (between 5.0% and 7.1%), and gemcitabine hydrochloride (between 4.6% and 5.7%). Overall, the most commonly administered concomitant chemotherapy agents after Day 5 in each cycle were gemcitabine (between 8.2% and 10.1% of patients) and gemcitabine hydrochloride (between 3.8% and 5.2%) and vinorelbine (between 5.8% and 7.0%) and vinorelbine tartrate (between 3.3% and 6.4%).
L557: ## Treatment Compliance
L558: A patient was considered to be compliant with therapy if he/she took all study drugs (including dexamethasone) provided in the clinical study medication kit. Compliance was defined overall and for each cycle.
L559: ## Rescue Medication
L560: The protocol stated “Rescue medication should be administered to alleviate established, refractory or persistent nausea or vomiting and will be permitted on an as-needed basis.” Rescue medication was not allowed for prevention of emesis, or to increase the expected antiemetic effects of the study medication. The patient was to record in the patient diary the drug name, dosage and date/time of intake for each medication taken for the treatment of nausea and
L561: vomiting during the 0-120 hour interval (Day 1 to Day 5). Each rescue medication trade or generic name, dosage and route of administration, as well as the time and date of intake, was documented on the eCRF in accordance with the patient diary records. NK-1 receptor antagonists, commercial palonosetron (Aloxi®, Onicit®, Paloxi®) and commercial Akynzeo® were not allowed as rescue medications. Other 5-HT3 receptor antagonists were not be used as rescue medications.
L562: The Investigator was to be provided with a sufficient amount of rescue medication, e.g., metoclopramide tablets, to be distributed to the patients on an as-needed basis. However, the Investigator was authorized to use an alternative rescue medication, based on his/her judgment. The Investigator was to ensure that rescue medication was made available to patients whenever needed. NK1 receptor antagonists were not allowed and 5-HT3 receptor antagonists were not to be used as rescue medications. Commercial palonosetron (Aloxi®, Onicit®, Paloxi®) was not allowed as rescue medication.
L563: ## Subject Completion, Discontinuation, or Withdrawal
L564: **Completion:** A patient was defined as “completed” if s/he completed Visit 5 (either as on-site visit or telephone contact) of Cycle 4.
L565: **“Completed Cycle, Not Continuing”:** A patient was defined as “completed cycle, not continuing” if s/he completed Visit 5 (either as on-site visit or telephone contact) of any cycle before Cycle 4 without continuing in the subsequent cycle.
L566: **Discontinuation:** Study termination at a different time point (including screening visit for one of the repeated cycles) was considered as study discontinuation.
L567: **Replacement:** Patients who discontinued after randomization were not to be replaced.
L568: **Follow-up Procedures and/or Assessments:** If a subject was discontinued from the study after treatment with the investigational product, the Investigator tried his/her best to contact the subject 20 (\pm 2) days after Day 1 of the last study cycle (i.e., at approximately the same date when Visit 5 (Day 21) was to be scheduled), in view of completing the safety assessment by collecting information that may have been suggestive of AEs.
L569: ## Missing Data
L570: Any patients who did not provide data about occurrence of emetic episodes and rescue medication intake or did not provide a full set of these data throughout the 120 h after HEC administration (including a diary available but not covering the whole 0-120 h) were considered as treatment failures (i.e., non-responders) for the efficacy analysis of CR in the overall phase. The same approach was applied to analysis of CR in the acute (0-24 h) and delayed (&gt;24-120 h) phases. For consistency, the same approach for handling missing data as described for CR was applied to emetic episodes and rescue medication intake.
L571: ## Study Endpoints
L572: Time-related efficacy study parameters assessment started at “time 0,” defined as the start time of the reference chemotherapy agent administration on Day 1 of each cycle. Efficacy parameters were evaluated at each cycle in the acute phase (0-24 h after the start of reference chemotherapy), delayed phase (&gt;24-120 h after the start of reference chemotherapy), and overall phase (0-120 h after the start of reference chemotherapy).
L573: The following efficacy endpoints were defined:
L574: - proportion of patients with CR (no emetic episodes and no rescue medication) during the acute, (0-24 h after the start of reference chemotherapy), delayed (&gt;24-120 h after the start of reference chemotherapy), and overall (0-120 h after the start of reference chemotherapy) phases
- proportion of patients with no emetic episodes during the acute, delayed, and overall phases
- proportion of patients with no significant nausea (VAS &lt;25 mm) during the acute, delayed, and overall phases (since VAS was assessed daily, for delayed and overall phases the maximum VAS value in the relevant phase was to be considered)
L575: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L576: ## Definitions of Analysis Populations
L577: The following definitions of analysis populations were used:
L578: - **All-Randomized Population:** The All Randomized population included all patients who were randomized to treatment. Patients were assigned to the treatment group to which they were randomized. The All Randomized population was used for patient disposition.
- **Full Analysis Set:** The Full Analysis Set (FAS) included all randomized patients who received the HEC regimen and active study drug (including partial infusion). Following the intent-to-treat principle, patients were assigned to the treatment group to which they were randomized. The FAS was used for demography, other baseline characteristics, and all efficacy analyses.
- **Safety Population:** The Safety population included all patients who received at least one dose of active study drug (including partial infusion). Patients were assigned to treatment groups according to the actual treatment received. Safety population was used for demography, other baseline characteristics, and all safety analyses.
L579: ## Efficacy Analyses
L580: The assessment of efficacy was a secondary objective of the study. **Only descriptive statistics were planned for the efficacy endpoints.** At each cycle, for each phase (acute, delayed and overall), numbers and percentages (including 95% confidence interval [CI] using the Wilson score method) of patients with CR, with no emetic episodes, with no rescue medications, and with no significant nausea were to be summarized by treatment. CR was also to be summarized
L581: by treatment and sex and by treatment and country. Difference between groups in response rate and 95% CI for difference were presented using Newcombe-Wilson's method and also the stratum-adjusted (sex and country as strata) Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) method for the risk difference. Results were to be interpreted only in a descriptive manner.
L582: The stratum-adjusted CMH method for difference in proportions used for efficacy analyses was to include sex (male, female) and country (Austria, Croatia, Czech Republic, Germany, Israel, Italy, Poland, Serbia, Spain, Ukraine, and United States) as strata. These were the same factors to be taken into account at the randomization level.
L583: The procedure for handling missing data was applied to the FAS. Any patients who did not provide data about occurrence of emetic episodes and rescue medication intake or did not provide a full set of these data throughout the 120 h after HEC administration (including a diary available but not covering the whole 0-120 h) were considered as treatment failures (i.e., non-responders) for the efficacy analysis of CR in the overall phase. The same approach was to be applied to analysis of CR in the acute (0-24 h) and delayed (&gt;24-120 h) phases. For consistency, the same approach for handling missing data as described for CR was to be applied to emetic episodes and rescue medication intake. For data recorded on the patient diary, a patient was considered as having CR only if there was documented evidence of both no occurrence of emetic episodes and no rescue medication intake. Otherwise the patient was considered as a failure. Episodes of either emesis or of rescue medication intake confirmed to have occurred outside the time window of 0-120 h with reference to start of chemotherapy administration were kept as they were and not used for the statistical analysis of CR.
L584: ## Adjustment for Multiplicity
L585: No adjustment for multiplicity was made in the study.
L586: ## Interim Analysis
L587: No interim analysis was performed for this study.
L588: ## Planned Subgroup Analyses
L589: Subgroups that were to be explored include:
L590: - Sex (Male, Female)
- Country (Austria, Croatia, Czech Republic, Germany, Israel, Italy, Poland, Serbia, South Africa, Spain, Ukraine and USA)
L591: ## Protocol Amendments
L592: There were 3 amendments to the NEPA 15-18 protocol: Amendment #1 dated July 27, 2015, Amendment #2 dated October 19, 2015, and Amendment #3 dated March 21, 2016. There were also 2 amendments for Germany only: Amendment #1 dated December 3, 2015 and Amendment #2 dated March 21, 2016.
L593: Amendment #1 dated July 27, 2015 documented the use of a Data Safety Monitoring Board
L594: (DSMB) to periodically review the safety data.
L595: Amendment #2 dated October 19, 2015 was implemented to correct a minor inconsistency present in the protocol Synopsis and in section 4.3.2, as well as to simplify study procedure during the repeated cycles (i.e., Cycles 2, 3 or 4) by allowing unification of the Visit 1 (Screening Visit) and Visit 2 (Day 1), whenever possible.
L596: Amendment #3 dated March 21, 2016 was implemented to update general information to current status, to specify in detail and improve description of time windows for the study drug, additional non-investigational study drug and reference chemotherapy administration, and vital signs 12-lead ECG measurements. Additionally, minor editorial improvements were implemented with no impact on the meaning of the sentences.
L597: Amendment #1 dated December 3, 2015, Germany only, was implemented to address the requests of the competent authority (German CEC) provided with their letter dated November 11, 2015 following the review of the study documentation:
L598: - Added Exclusion criterion in Synopsis and in Section 4.3.3: (Pre-dose) QTcF Interval &gt;470 ms at Screening in all Cycles.
L599: - Added the sentence in Section 6.4 Premature Discontinuation: In addition, any patient with post-dose QTcF &gt;500 ms or with QTcF increase &gt;60 ms compared to last value before study drug intake in the cycle will complete the current cycle, but will not be allowed to enter any additional study cycle.
L600: Amendment #2 dated December 3, 2015, Germany only, was implemented to update general information to current status, to specify in detail and improve the description of time windows for the study drug, additional non-investigational study drug and reference chemotherapy administration, vital signs and 12-lead ECG measurements. Additionally, minor editorial improvements were implemented with no impact on the meaning of the sentences.
L601: ## **Data Quality and Integrity: Sponsor's Assurance**
L602: ## Monitoring
L603: The study was monitored by CRO's adequately qualified and trained clinical monitors on a regular basis throughout the study period to ensure the proper conduct of the clinical trial. The purposes of clinical trial monitoring were to verify that the rights and well-being of study patients were protected, that the reported trial data were accurate, complete, and verifiable against the source documents, and that the study was conducted in accordance with the current protocol, Good Clinical Practice guideline and applicable regulatory requirements. During the monitoring visits, monitors verified the following including but not limited to: patient informed consent, patient's eligibility, safety data and reporting, quality of source documents, and eCRF data against patient's medical records. If inconsistencies were identified, the corresponding corrections to the eCRF data had to be made by the Investigator. Monitors also checked patient compliance, accrual, drug handling, including dispensing procedures and accountability logs, delegation of responsibilities within the Investigator's team, relevant communications with
L604: family doctors, and if any, ancillary equipment and facilities, including refrigerators and freezers, local labs, etc. The Investigator and other site staff involved in the study allocated enough time to the monitor at these visits.
L605: Audits were performed by the CRO as well as by the Sponsor. Upon request by the Sponsor, or following the CRO's audit plan, on-site study audits were conducted to ensure that the study was following GCP, applicable regulatory requirements, and the study protocol. The auditing activities may have also been conducted after study completion.
L606: In addition, Regulatory Authorities may have conducted on-site inspections (during the study or after its completion). If a Regulatory Authority notified the Investigator of an inspection or visited the site unannounced for purposes of conducting an inspection, the Investigator had to inform the study Sponsor and CRO immediately. The Investigator facilitate the conduct of the audits and inspections, giving access to all necessary facilities, data and documents.
L607: ## Laboratory Accreditation
L608: An accredited central laboratory was used for urinalysis, hematology, and blood chemistry analysis throughout the entire study. For study inclusion (Cycle 1), central laboratory hematological results were mandatory and were to satisfy the inclusion criterion #8. For Investigator's decision about patient's inclusion in Cycles 2 to 4, accredited local laboratory results were acceptable; nevertheless, central laboratory data were to be collected at each cycle.
L609: ## Data Reporting and Management
L610: Electronic CRFs (eCRFs) were used for recording patient's study data using a validated web-based Electronic Data Capture (EDC) system. It was the responsibility of the Investigator to ensure that the eCRFs were properly and filled in. The eCRFs had to be completed for all patients who were included in the study.
L611: Each authorized site personnel were assigned a login and password to enter the EDC system via a secure network. Each login was to uniquely identify the user and appropriate permissions were set-up per the user role. The access to the system was granted once the user's training had been completed and documented.
L612: Data Clarification Forms (DCFs) were used to validate/clarify data entered on an eCRF page. Some DCFs were generated automatically by the system when the user saved data that did not meet the criteria pre-defined for the data field. In addition, other DCFs were created manually in the eCRF and sent to the Investigator either by the study monitor (as result of SDV) or by Clinical Data Management.
L613: Discrepancies raised were reviewed online to determine the corrective action needed. Changes were made directly by authorized site staff. The EDC system's audit trail was to keep track of any information entered, modified, and deleted. The audit trail was to include, as a minimum, what was entered/changed/modified, who made the action, and when the action was made. Any answered DCF was reviewed and closed by authorized users. The Investigator was to
L614: electronically sign the eCRF to validate the content.
L615: Patient's source documentation (i.e., hospital charts) were to be maintained at the clinic. Source data to be recorded directly onto the eCRFs were to be communicated in advance. In cases where source documents were not eCRFs, the information on the eCRFs had to match the source documents. Source data verification was regularly performed by the study monitor.
L616: Paper patient diaries were used to collect episodes of retching and vomiting, rescue medication intake, daily nausea assessment and dexamethasone intake at home. Considering the patient population, the short re-call period and the fact that rescue medication intake and emetic episodes are objective data, the paper diary was considered as a reliable method of data collection and as the most suitable method for this patient population. At each visit, the Investigator was to review the filled-in paper diary and discuss with the patient any issues or missing/incomplete data, so that the patient was enabled to make all necessary corrections, if any, during the visit. The Investigator (or designee) was to review, sign, date, and collect the complete patient diaries after they were returned at Visit 4 and also to transfer all data from each paper diary to the respective eCRF. The paper diaries were to be kept at the trial site and considered as source documents.
L617: The medical (hospital) files of trial patients were to be retained in accordance with national legislation (and in accordance with the maximum period permitted by the hospital or institution/private practice). If the Investigator wished to assign the study documentation to another party or to move to another location, the Sponsor was to be promptly notified. No study documentation could be destroyed without prior written approval from the Sponsor.
L618: The Sponsor had to ensure that it was specified in the protocol or other written agreement that the Investigator(s)/institution(s) would permit trial-related monitoring, audits, IRB/EC review, and regulatory inspection(s), providing direct access to source data/documents.
L619: ## 6.2.2. Study Results
L620: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L621: NEPA 15-18 was conducted in compliance with good clinical practices with the CFR regarding protection of human subjects, Institutional Review Boards, Independent Ethics Committees, and obligations of clinical investigators.
L622: Reporting of suspected unexpected serious adverse reactions (SUSARs) to the relevant IRB/IEC, was in accordance with the European Union (EU) Clinical Trial Directive 2001/20/EC, the United States (US) Code of Federal Regulations 313.32, the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) E6 Guidance on Good Clinical Practices (GCP), and ICH E2A Guidance on Clinical Safety Management and was also the responsibility of the Sponsor (or Sponsor's designee).
L623: ## Financial Disclosure
L624: A review of this study revealed no financial conflicts of interest. Please see financial disclosure
L625: forms in Section 13.2. The applicant provided adequate documentation to show that there were no financial conflicts of interest between themselves and the 362 Investigators that participated in this study.
L626: ## Patient Disposition
L627: A total of 405 patients were randomized, and 404 (99.8%) received study treatment. The number of patients randomized into each treatment group, patient disposition, and the reasons for early discontinuation from the study are summarized in the tables below.
L628: The 405 patients randomized were distributed between treatment groups as follows: 203 patients randomized to IV NEPA FDC and 202 patients to Oral NEPA FDC. As shown in the table below, 404 (99.8%) patients were treated (203 [100%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group, and 201 [99.5%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group). One randomized patient (patient ID 8270417) in the Oral NEPA FDC group did not receive active study drug or reference HEC. The FAS and the safety populations were the same in this study.

L632: A total of 237 (58.5%) patients completed the four study cycles (120 [59.1%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group, and 117 [57.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group).
L633: Eighty-three (20.5%) patients (41 [20.2%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group, and 42 [20.8%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group) completed a cycle (Cycle 1, 2, or 3) but did not continue on the subsequent study cycle.
L634: Eighty-five (21.0%) patients (42 [20.7%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group, and 43 [21.3%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group) discontinued from the study during a cycle, either before receiving study drug (45 patients) or after receiving study drug (40 patients).
L635: Overall, 168 (41.5%) patients did not complete the study (either completed a cycle and did not continue on the subsequent one [83 (20.5%) patients] or discontinued from the study during a cycle [85 (21.0%) patients]). The most frequent (\ge 5\% of patients overall) reasons for
L636: discontinuation from the study were the following: overall study closure in 43 (10.6%) patients (24 [11.8%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group, and 19 [9.4%] in the Oral NEPA FDC group), AE in 34 (8.4%) patients (16 [7.9%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group, and 18 [8.9%] in the Oral NEPA FDC group), death in 25 (6.2%) patients (10 [4.9%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group, and 15 [7.4%] in the Oral NEPA FDC group), and physician decision in 20 (4.9%) patients (8 [3.9%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group, and 12 [5.9%] in the Oral NEPA FDC group). Other less frequent reasons for premature discontinuation were inclusion/exclusion criteria not met for a repeated cycle, withdrawal by subject, patient lost to follow-up, and other reasons (see table below for details).

L641: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L642: Protocol deviations that were considered as having a major impact on efficacy at Cycle 1 are summarized for FAS in the table below. Overall, 27 (6.7%) patients in the FAS had protocol deviations with a major impact on efficacy at Cycle 1: 13 (6.4%) patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 14 (7.0%) patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group. The most common (affecting 10 [37.0%] patients) protocol deviation referred to “use of medications with potential antiemetic effects or use of CYP3A4 strong or moderate inhibitors, use of CYP3A4 inducers or substrates during Cycle 1 in the interval 0-120 h after start of reference HEC.” All remaining protocol deviations with a major impact on efficacy at Cycle 1 were related to use of prohibited prior or concomitant medications, improper administration of study medication or dexamethasone, improper completion or collection of the diary, or inclusion criterion not met. Please see table below.

L652: ## Other Baseline Characteristics
L653: Cancer history is summarized for the safety population in the table below. At study entry, primary cancer location was similar between treatment groups, with the most frequent primary cancer location being lung and respiratory tract (54.9%), followed by other location (18.8%), and ovarian (12.1%). The overall median time since diagnosis was 33 days.
L654: The extent of cancer at study entry was categorized as either primary, metastatic, or locally recurrent, with no notable differences between the treatment groups in the proportions of patients in each category. The majority of patients had primary (47.0%) or metastatic (51.5%) disease. For metastatic cancer, the most commonly reported sites for metastases were lymph nodes (31.9%), lung (17.1%), other sites (10.9%), and liver (10.1%). A small proportion of patients (1.5%) had locally recurrent cancer. Please see table below.

L658: ## Chemotherapy
L659: ## Reference Chemotherapy
L660: Reference HEC administered to patients on Day 1 of each cycle is summarized for the FAS in the table below. Overall on Day 1 of Cycle 1, 387 (95.8%) patients received cisplatin (overall mean [SD] dose was 144.86 [22.98] mg). Fourteen (3.5%) patients received dacarbazine (overall mean [SD] dose was 1757.52 [391.67] mg) and 3 (0.74%) patients received cyclophosphamide (overall mean [SD] dose was 3003.33 [332.65] mg). In general, the mean (SD) doses of cisplatin, dacarbazine, and cyclophosphamide received on Day 1 of Cycles 2, 3, and 4 were similar to those on Day 1 of Cycle 1 for both overall and for each treatment group. No patient was treated with cyclophosphamide in the IV NEPA FDC group on Day 1 of Cycle 4 compared with 1 patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group, and no patient was treated with any of the remaining predefined reference HEC (carmustine or mechloretamine).

L664: ## Concomitant Chemotherapy
L665: Similar proportions of patients in each treatment group received chemotherapy concomitantly with their reference HEC up to Day 5 (inclusive) during each cycle (overall, 92.6% of patients during Cycle 1, 89.6% during Cycle 2, 88.2% during Cycle 3 and 89.5% during Cycle 4), mostly on Day 1 only (overall, 67.1% of patients during Cycle 1, 62.5% during Cycle 2, 62.3% during Cycle 3, and 65.3% during Cycle 4), with between 24.3% and 27.0% of patients overall receiving it on Days 1-5. Overall, between 22.6% and 30.4% of patients overall received chemotherapy after Day 5.
L666: Overall, the most commonly administered concomitant chemotherapy agents up to Day 5 (inclusive) in each cycle were etoposide (between 24.9% and 25.7% of patients), cyclophosphamide (between 15.1% and 19.7%), fluorouracil (between 11.1% and 11.8%), gemcitabine (between 9.2% and 10.9%), vinorelbine (between 7.0% and 8.4%), vinorelbine tartrate (between 5.9% and 7.9%), doxorubicin (between 5.0% and 7.1%), and gemcitabine hydrochloride (between 4.6% and 5.7%) See table below.
L667: Overall, the most commonly administered concomitant chemotherapy agents after Day 5 in each
L668: cycle were gemcitabine (between 8.2% and 10.1% of patients) and gemcitabine hydrochloride (between 3.8% and 5.2%); and vinorelbine (between 5.8% and 7.0%) and vinorelbine tartrate (between 3.3% and 6.4%) See table below.

L672: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L673: ## Treatment Compliance
L674: Treatment compliance is summarized in the table below. A patient was considered to be compliant with therapy if he/she took all study drugs (including dexamethasone) provided in the clinical study medication kit. Overall, the majority (95.8%) of patients were compliant with their study treatment throughout the study (96.1% in the IV NEPA FDC group and 95.5% in the Oral NEPA FDC group). The cases of non-compliance were exclusively due to missing doses of dexamethasone on any of the dosing days (Days 1-4), during one or more cycles.

L678: ## Prior Medications
L679: Use of prior medications was recorded for 47.8% of patients in the study. The most commonly reported classes of medications (\ge 5\% of patients overall; ATC level 4 headings) used prior to study entry were heparin group (9.7%), proton pump inhibitors (8.7%), selective beta blocking agents (6.4%), and platelet aggregation inhibitors excluding heparin (5.4%).
L680: ## Concomitant Medications
L681: Use of concomitant medications was recorded for 97.0% of patients in the study. As expected due to the specific patient population and administration of cytotoxic cancer chemotherapy, solutions producing osmotic diuresis such as mannitol (45.3%); solutions affecting electrolyte balance (39.6%) and electrolyte solutions (20.5%); and solutions for parenteral nutrition (21.0%) were very commonly reported classes of medications (ATC level 4 headings) during the present study.
L682: In addition, other commonly reported classes of medications (&gt;10\% of patients overall; ATC level 4 headings) used concomitantly during the study were plain sulfonamides (45.3%, mainly furosemide), proton pump inhibitors (21.8%), heparin group (21.5%), H2-receptor antagonists (20.8%), selective beta blocking agents (20.5%), plain ACE inhibitors (18.8%), colony stimulating factors (16.8%), propulsives (15.1%), anilides (14.9%, mainly paracetamol), and glucocorticoids (14.6%).

L721: ## Country
L722: The results for the countries with the largest enrollment, Ukraine (162 patients), Poland (112 patients), and Serbia (55 patients) are summarized below. There was a numerically higher treatment difference for CR (CR in IV group minus CR in PO group) for the highest enrolling country (Ukraine) in Cycle 1 particularly in the delayed phase compared to the second and third highest enrolling country (Poland and Serbia). The Ukraine treatment difference for CR in the delayed phase appeared to drive the overall treatment difference for CR in the delayed phase. No clear conclusion can be drawn from this observation.
