L733: Please refer to OSI analysis in Section 4.1. There were no anomalies in the results with respect to different study sites. No other concerns with the study results were identified.

L739: Efficacy was not the primary endpoint of this study; thus, persistence of effect is not relevant.
L740: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L741: ## Initial Request for Explanation by Applicant of 9 Percent Lower Observed CR in Delayed Phase (IV Akynzeo vs. PO Akynzeo)
L742: An IR was sent to the applicant on 07/10/2017 requesting what they thought accounted for the observed 9 percent lower delayed phase CR in the IV Akynzeo® (NEPA FDC) arm compared to the oral Akynzeo® arm in Cycle 1, given that fosnetupitant and oral netupitant had been shown to be associated with comparable netupitant exposures in the dedicated relative bioavailability study. On 07/19/2017, the applicant responded, and reported that 9 patients in Cycle 1 took rescue medications “in the absence of emetic episodes and significant nausea” (absence of
L743: significant nausea was defined as VAS &lt; 25 mm).14,15 As per the applicant, 7 of these patients were enrolled in the IV NEPA FDC arm, and 5 of them were enrolled at site 3107 (Ukraine). The applicant asserted that this uneven distribution explained the observed treatment distribution as a higher number of patients would have been disqualified as being complete responders in the IV arm.
L744: ## Cycle 1 Results - Listing of Sites with Patients Reported to have Taken Rescue Medications Inappropriately
L745: On 07/31/2017, an IR was sent to the applicant requesting a listing of the patients by site and phase who were reported to have taken rescue medications inappropriately. The applicant responded on 08/16/2017; see the table below.
L746: 14 “No significant nausea” was defined as VAS &lt; 25 mm (see Section 6.2.1).
L747: 15 In another response to Information request (received 9/26/17), the Applicant provided (for patients identified by the Applicant as having taken rescue medications inappropriately in a given phase and cycle) the following: pertinent portions of the CRF’s documenting (by phase and cycle) absence of emetic episodes, absence of significant nausea (i.e., VAS &lt; 25 mm), and administration of rescue medication.

L759: This analysis did not reveal consistent results across cycles. The treatment difference became slightly less negative in Cycles 1, 3, and 4 in the FAS population; however, it became more negative in Cycle 2.
L760: ## **Discussion of Study NEPA 15-18**
L761: The efficacy data from this trial were included and discussed for completeness, and there were many post hoc analyses performed to explore the observed difference in CR in the delayed phase between the oral and IV NEPA FDC arms. However, the study was conducted to evaluate safety of fosnetupitant in a controlled trial setting; it was neither designed nor powered to be a non-inferiority trial to evaluate efficacy. The Statistical reviewers noted in their review of this trial that the noninferiority margin for the oral combination product (containing 300 mg of netupitant) for the delayed phase is very small, i.e., 1%; therefore, Study NEPA 15-18 had only 4% power to establish noninferiority of the IV combination product to the oral combination. In this NDA, fosnetupitant's efficacy was established by demonstrating that the netupitant exposures (AUC_{0-120hr} and AUC_{0-\infty}) associated with fosnetupitant and oral netupitant met bioequivalence criteria in a relative bioavailability study (Study PNET-12-23). As described in Section 4.5.3 above, the reviewers concluded that the observed differences in Study NEPA 15-18 could not be attributed to differences in metabolite exposures between the products.
L762: This reviewer, in conjunction with the other members of the review team, concluded that the observed differences in Study NEPA 15-18 were not interpretable because the study was not designed and adequately powered to evaluate noninferiority, and should not alter the efficacy conclusion supported by the results of the relative bioavailability study (Study PNET-12-23), i.e., the efficacy associated with fosnetupitant in the setting HEC (excluding AC combination chemotherapy) is comparable to that associated with oral netupitant. The CDTL, Division Director, and ODE III Director concur with that conclusion.
L763: ## 7 Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L764: ## 7.1. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L765: ## Fosnetupitant:
L766: Study PNET 12-23 was a relative bioavailability study in healthy subjects which included a comparison of 260 mg of IV fosnetupitant (a prodrug of netupitant) with 300 mg of oral netupitant (the dose of netupitant in the approved Akynzeo capsules). The fosnetupitant dose selected was shown to meet bioequivalence criteria (for AUC_{0-120} and AUC_{0-\infty}) in a comparison of relative bioavailability with the netupitant dose in Akynzeo capsules. The oral netupitant dose selection was from a dose-ranging study (Study NETU-07-07) submitted for Akynzeo capsules [see reviews from Akynzeo capsules NDA (NDA 205718) particularly the Clinical Pharmacology and Statistics Reviews]. The study showed that for the delayed phase, there was a flat dose-response relationship over the range of 100 to 300 mg netupitant when netupitant + palonosetron 0.5 mg was compared to palonosetron 0.5 mg alone; each of the doses showed statistical significance over placebo. However, for the acute phase, only netupitant 300 mg was shown to be statistically significantly higher than placebo for the Complete Response endpoint. Because bioequivalence criteria were met, a bridging argument for efficacy of fosnetupitant can be supported for Akynzeo IV based on the oral Akynzeo efficacy data.
L767: ## Palonosetron:
L768: Study PALO 15-17 was a non-inferiority (NI) study in the acute phase conducted in a HEC population that did not include patients treated with AC comparing palonosetron infused over 30 minutes (as in the proposed product) to palonosetron infused over 30 seconds (as approved for Aloxi IV).
L769: ## 7.1.1. Primary Endpoints
L770: ## PALO 15-17
L771: Non-inferiority of a 30-minute infusion of IV palonosetron 0.25 mg to a 30-second bolus injection of palonosetron 0.25 mg (the approved posology for Aloxi IV) was established for CR in the acute phase of CINV in a single cycle of HEC (not including AC combination chemotherapy).
L772: ## 7.1.2. Secondary and Other Endpoints
L773: ## PALO 15-17
L774: No adjustment for multiplicity was made in this study. Results for the secondary and other endpoints from this study are included for completeness only (see Section 6.1.2).
L775: ## NEPA 15-18
L776: Analyses of all efficacy endpoints in this study were descriptive. Results for these endpoints are included for completeness only (see Section 6.2.2).
L777: ## 7.1.3. Subpopulations
L778: A summary of the applicant's subgroup analyses is provided below. In both PALO 15-17 and NEPA 15-18, the applicant conducted subgroup analyses of CR based on sex and country.
L779: ## PALO 15-17
L780: Sex: No treatment by sex interaction was detected (see Section 6.1.2).
L781: Country: No treatment-by country interaction was detected (see Section 6.1.2).
L782: ## NEPA 15-18
L783: Sex: The proportion of female patients with CR was numerically lower compared with male patients in each phase and in each cycle for both treatment groups (IV NEPA FDC and Oral NEPA FDC). These results were expected as female sex is one of the risk factors for CINV (see Section 2.1). See Section 6.2.2.
L784: Country: No clear relation between CR across phases and cycles and country was appreciated. See Section 6.2.2.
L785: ## 7.1.4. Dose and Dose-Response
L786: Dose and Dose response was not specifically addressed by either PALO 15-17 or NEPA 15-18.
L787: Palonosetron: The approved dose of Aloxi (palonosetron) IV was used.
L788: Fosnetupitant: The fosnetupitant dose was determined from Study PNET 12-23.
L789: ## 7.1.5. Onset, Duration, and Durability of Efficacy Effects
L790: Onset, duration, and durability of efficacy effects were not specifically addressed by either PALO 15-17 or NEPA 15-18. A discussion of these issues can be found in the oral Akynzeo review written by Dr. Nancy Snow.
L791: ## 7.2. Additional Efficacy Considerations
L792: ## 7.2.1. Considerations on Benefit in the Postmarket Setting
L793: No potential efficacy issues in the postmarket setting have been identified.
L794: ## 7.2.2. Other Relevant Benefits
L795: Akynzeo® for injection has some additional benefits. It can be reasonably expected to prevent/treat CINV in patients receiving non-AC HEC chemotherapy who cannot tolerate oral medication. Though the indication is more narrow for Akynzeo® for injection, its route of administration will still likely be of benefit to patients receiving the most emetogenic of chemotherapy regimens. The fact that it is a combination product (5HT3 and NK1 antagonists) is also of benefit as both medication classes are recommended for the treatment of CINV in HEC per the most recent ASCO guidelines. As such, one medication could be administered to the patient as opposed to two.
L796: ## 7.3. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L797: Akynzeo® for injection has met the statutory evidentiary standard. The evidence for the effectiveness of Akynzeo for Injection, an intravenous fixed combination product (of fosnetupitant and palonosetron) is based on bridging to the approved fixed combination oral Akynzeo product (netupitant and palonosetron) and the currently approved intravenous product.
L798: - Trials that supported the approval of oral Akynzeo capsules established the efficacy of the netupitant component for prevention of acute and delayed phases of HEC CINV and the efficacy of palonosetron for acute phase of HEC CINV. (The NDA also included trials that established the oral netupitant's efficacy for prevention of acute and delayed phase CINV associated with AC combination chemotherapy, when it was combined with oral palonosetron.)
- Trials that supported the approval of intravenous Aloxi (**palonosetron, IV**) established its efficacy for prevention of acute phase of HEC CINV. (The NDA also included trials that established the efficacy of intravenous palonosetron for prevention of CINV, associated with AC combination chemotherapy, in both the acute and delayed phases.
- Studies submitted in the current NDA to bridge to the approved products are:Non-Inferiority Trial (Palonosetron 30 minute vs. 30 second infusion; Study PALO 15-17): This was a non-inferiority (NI) study of prevention of **acute phase CINV** in HEC (not including AC population). It compared **palonosetron infused over 30 minutes** (as in the proposed product) to palonosetron infused over 30 seconds (as approved for Aloxi IV). Non-inferiority was demonstrated for the Complete Response endpoint in the acute phase.
- Relative Bioavailability Study (IV fosnetupitant vs. oral netupitant; Study PNET 12-23): This was a relative bioavailability study in healthy subjects which included a comparison of 260 mg of **IV fosnetupitant** (a prodrug of netupitant) with 300 mg of oral netupitant (the dose of netupitant in the approved Akynzeo capsules). Bioequivalence criteria were met (for AUC0-120h and AUC0-∞). Cmax was approximately 50% higher with fosnetupitant 260 mg IV than with netupitant 300 mg PO (see Clinical Pharmacology Review); thus, fosnetupitant 260 mg IV is expected to be at least as efficacious as netupitant 300 mg PO.
L799: The effect of **palonosetron infused over 30 minutes** was demonstrated in the **acute phase** in the population of **HEC not including AC**, and the effect of **IV fosnetupitant** was demonstrated in the **acute and delayed phases** in the population of **HEC not including AC**. It should be
L800: noted that because palonosetron IV has a delayed phase indication in the AC population but palonosetron PO does not, **demonstration of the contribution of fosnetupitant IV in the delayed phase in the AC population would be needed for an Akynzeo IV indication in the delayed phase in the AC population.**
L801: A description and results of Study Palo-15-17 should be included in the Clinical Studies section of the label. A description and results of Study NEPA 15-18 should not be included in the Clinical Studies section of the label because only descriptive efficacy analyses were performed in this study, which was intended to evaluate safety in a controlled trial setting, to assess the safety of fosnetupitant and the higher Cmax of netupitant associated with intravenous Akynzeo.
L802: ## 8 Review of Safety
L803: ## 8.1. Safety Review Approach
L804: The safety review involved analysis of three trials, PNET 12-23 (Phase 1 ascending dose and relative bioavailability study), PALO 15-17, and NEPA 15-18. PNET 12-23 involved healthy volunteers. In addition, PALO 15-17 only studied the infusion duration of palonosetron. NEPA 15-18 was the primary safety study as it involved cancer patients receiving HEC who were also given Akynzeo® for injection or oral Akynzeo capsules. Because of these key differences in these study populations, each trial was considered separately. The safety data were analyzed by evaluating the applicant's data in the study reports, reviewing patient narratives where appropriate, reviewing safety data from the oral Akynzeo® program, and using J-Review.
L805: ## 8.2. Review of the Safety Database
L806: ## 8.2.1. Overall Exposure
L807: ## PNET 12-23
L808: The study was conducted with 8 single ascending pro-netupitant dose levels from 19.5 mg up to a maximum dose of 390 mg, with a crossover arm from 130 mg up to 390 mg pro-netupitant. A total of 10 subjects were planned to be treated per dose cohort. Two additional crossover parts, each with 2 dose cohorts and with 20 subjects per dose cohort, were also performed. See the figure below.
L809: Figure 3. Study PNET-12-23: Overview of Design
L810: DSMB: Drug Safety Monitoring Board, F-CO: final crossover, IV: intravenous, N: number of subjects, P-CO: pilot crossover, PK: pharmacokinetics, PO: per os, SAD: single ascending dose, SAD-CO: single ascending dose crossover
L811: The figure above is taken from Page 30 of the Study Report.
L812: One hundred and sixty (160) subjects were randomized in the study. Two (2) randomized subjects were not treated due to premature discontinuation of the study prior to the first drug administration. All other 158 randomized subjects were treated during the study. In the ascending dose part, 30 subjects received only 1 dose as planned, either 1 IV dose of 19.5, 32.5, or 65 mg pro-netupitant (8 subjects each, 24 subjects overall) or 1 oral dose of 300 mg netupitant/0.5 mg palonosetron (6 subjects overall).
L813: In the crossover parts, 3 subjects received only 1 oral dose of 300 mg netupitant and 0.5 mg palonosetron (together with placebo infusion) due to premature discontinuation of the study prior to administration of the IV dose (together with a placebo capsule).
L814: All other 125 subjects of the crossover parts received 2 doses as planned, 1 IV dose of 130 mg to 390 mg pro-netupitant (together with a placebo capsule) and 1 oral dose of 300 mg netupitant/0.5 mg palonosetron (together with a placebo infusion).
L815: In total, 149 subjects received 1 dose of the IV pro-netupitant treatment and 134 subjects received 1 dose of the oral 300 mg netupitant/0.5 mg palonosetron treatment. One (1) subject of the 286 mg dose cohort received, however, the wrong oral treatment during the second period of the crossover part. Thus, this subject received both, 1 oral dose of 300 mg netupitant/0.5 mg palonosetron (instead of the oral placebo assigned according to randomization) together with the IV infusion of 286 mg pro-netupitant. The planned IV dose was not completely administered due to infusion interruption following the discovery of the erroneous oral medication administration; 80.1 mL instead of 110 mL of the infusion solution were administered, resulting in an administered dose of 208.26 mg IV pro-netupitant.
L816: Furthermore, 1 subject of the 325 mg dose cohort probably did not receive the complete IV dose of 325 mg pro-netupitant in the first period of the crossover part due to a probable extravasation of the IV study drug. In fact, during the infusion the subject experienced swelling at the infusion site. Infusion was immediately interrupted and restarted in another vein, where the entire remaining volume of the infusion solution was administered. Extravasation of part of the volume infused prior to interruption (32.5 mL of the 125 mL corresponding to a dose of 84.5 mg pro-netupitant) could not be excluded.
L817: ## PALO 15-17
L818: Treatment exposure to palonosetron is summarized in the table below. All patients treated in the infusion group except one received the planned dose of palonosetron 0.25 mg IV in 50 mL solution, and all patients treated in the bolus group received the planned dose of palonosetron 0.25 mg IV in 5 mL solution. The exception in the infusion group was patient 1907-02, who received the correct dose of 0.25 mg palonosetron in 55 mL due to erroneous preparation of the infusion solution. No patients in the infusion group had interruptions of the study treatment infusion.

L827: ## 8.2.2. Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L828: For the purposes of the safety populations, PNET 12-23 was conducted in healthy volunteers. NEPA 15-18 and PALO 15-17 were conducted in cancer patients that were receiving cisplatin based HEC. The specifics of each population were discussed in section 6.1.1 and 6.2.1.
L829: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of the safety database:
L830: The study data base for the Akynzeo® for injection development program was adequate. The
L831: safety data from the oral development program was considered along with the safety data from the populations studied in PNET 12-23, NEPA 15-18, and PALO 15-17. In addition, the studies (excluding PNET 12-23) were conducted in cancer patients receiving HEC chemotherapy. Safety was assessed in the 120 hours following drug administration, including with repeat dosing in NEPA 15-18.
L832: NEPA 15-18 was the only dedicated safety study that was conducted during the Akynzeo® for injection development program. These results will be emphasized given that this was the study where cancer patients receiving HEC received both components of the drug (palonosetron and fosnetupitant).
L833: ## 8.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L834: ## 8.3.1. Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L835: The quality and integrity of the safety data was adequate for this review. No concerns were identified with any study sites. Though there was a minimal amount of missing data in the data sets, quality of the data was acceptable. An Office of Computational Science (OCS) consultation was not required. The submission documents were reasonably well organized. A review of the diary modules and VAS identified no concerns. Multiple information requests were sent to the applicant for additional information and data analysis, and there were no issues with the responses that were received.
L836: ## 8.3.2. Categorization of Adverse Events
L837: For all three studies, AEs and serious adverse events (SAEs) were adequately defined per the regulations in 21 CFR 312.32.
L838: Planned surgical interventions or planned hospitalizations scheduled prior to the informed consent but performed during the study (study procedures, chemotherapy cycles, etc.) were not considered (serious) AEs.
L839: Signs and symptoms considered as lack of efficacy (nausea and vomiting) and occurring during the studies up to 5 days after the study drugs administration were to be recorded on the AEs section of the eCRF, except on the condition that, in the Investigator's opinion, nausea and vomiting was caused by any reason different from lack of efficacy of study treatment or meet the definition of a serious AE.
L840: An Unexpected Adverse Event was any experience not previously reported (in nature, severity or incidence) in the current Investigator's Brochures for palonosetron, oral NEPA FDC, and/or IV NEPA FDC.
L841: A pre-existing condition was one that is present at the start of the study. A pre-existing condition was recorded as an adverse event if the frequency, intensity, or the character of the condition worsened during the study period.
L842: The Investigator classified AEs based on their severity and relationship to IMP. Every effort must be made by the Investigator to categorize each AE according to its severity and its relationship.
L843: The severity of an AE was rated by the Investigator according to the descriptions and grading scales of the Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) (for details please refer to the integral document Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE, Version 4.03: June 14, 2010); U.S. Department of Health and Human Services, National Institutes of Health, National Cancer Institute).
L844: For these trials, an AE cause and effect relationship to the study drug was classified by the Investigator as reported as definitely related, probably related, possibly related, unlikely related, not related/ none, and un-assessable.
L845: Adverse events were coded using the MedDRA dictionary to give a preferred term (PT) and a system/organ class term (SOC) for each event. The number and percentage of patients who experienced at least one treatment emergent adverse event (TEAE), drug related TEAE (defined as adverse events with relationship classified as definitely, probably, possibly, un-assessable or missing), serious TEAE, serious related, severe TEAE (i.e. TEAE with CTCAE grade \ge 3), and the number and percentage of patients withdrawn due to TEAE were summarized by treatment arm.
L846: For each SOC and preferred term, summaries were made with respect to the proportion of patients having at least one occurrence of that event during the study and the total number of events. The incidence of TEAEs in each treatment arm were presented overall, by SOC and preferred term, and additional grouping by severity according to the CTCAE grade and relationship to the study treatment. Tables by drug relationship were presented by relationship to study drugs and relationship to additional study drug (dexamethasone) separately. Additional tables including any relationship were presented.
L847: No formal test was used for “between groups” comparison. All adverse events were listed.
L848: AE reporting was in accordance with the ICH E6 Guidance on GCP and ICH E2A Guidance on Clinical Safety Management.
L849: During the course of the study, all AEs (including SAEs), irrespective of the relatedness to the study drugs, were recorded in detail in the source records and transcribed onto the AEs pages of the eCRF. During each monitoring visit, the Investigator and the site monitor reviewed all AEs and perform Source Data Verification (SDV). The Investigator was responsible for ensuring that the correct information concerning all AEs is entered on the appropriate eCRF pages.
L850: The reporting period for AEs was the period starting from the time of Informed Consent signature and lasting until Day 21 (20 [\pm 2] days post study drug administration on Day 1). All unresolved AEs (including SAEs) were documented on the eCRF as “ongoing”.
L851: All SAEs occurring from the time of signing of the informed consent until Day 21 (20 [\pm 2] days
L852: post study drug administration on Day 1) were reported immediately to the CRO pharmacovigilance. Information on the actual fax and phone numbers were provided in the Investigator file as well as at Section General Information of the present protocol. The Investigator or designated study coordinator completed the Investigational Serious AE Form and sent it to the CRO within 24 hours of observation or notification of a serious AE. All of these events were recorded on the appropriate eCRF pages.
L853: It was the responsibility of the Investigator to inform his or her local Institutional Review Board (IRB)/Ethics Committee (EC) about SAEs according to the local IRB/EC requirements. It was the responsibility of the Sponsor (or Sponsor's designee) to submit applicable SAE Reports to the Competent Authorities. Reporting of suspected unexpected serious adverse reactions (SUSARs) to the relevant IRB/EC, in accordance with the EU Clinical Trial Directive 2001/20/EC, the US Code of Federal Regulations 313.32 and ICH E6 Guidance on GCP and ICH E2A Guidance on Clinical Safety Management, was also be the responsibility of the Sponsor (or Sponsor's designee).
L854: A safety contact sheet was provided to the Investigator and was maintained in the Investigator file at the site.
L855: SAEs were followed by the Investigator until the outcome was resolved, had reached a stable condition in the Investigator's opinion, or until the patient was lost to follow-up. When the investigational site received any information about a serious adverse event which changed or added to the information on the initial investigational SAE form, the site filled out a new investigational SAE form and ticked the "follow-up" box of the SAE form and faxed it within 24 hours to the CRO, especially if this new information had an effect on the seriousness, relatedness or expectedness of an adverse event.
L856: Analysis of reported term and dictionary derived term did not reveal any issues with coding for each of the study. Furthermore, there was no indication of lumping or splitting of the terms. All of the AEs were grouped and analyzed using the MedDRA hierarchy.
L857: Considering that QT prolongation and constipation are known class effects of 5-HT3 receptor antagonist, special attention was paid to both events, in particular on severe forms of constipation (CTCAE grade 3 or 4). In such cases, an accurate follow-up was to be performed by the Sponsor.
L858: ## 8.3.3. Routine Clinical Tests
L859: Clinical evaluations for safety assessments included physical examinations, monitoring AEs, vital signs, ECGs, and laboratory tests. Laboratory tests consisted of blood chemistry, hematology, and urinalysis.
L860: The following laboratory parameters were assessed:
L861: - Hematology: hematocrit, hemoglobin, erythrocytes, platelets, leukocytes, neutrophils, lymphocytes, basophils, eosinophils, monocytes.
- Blood Chemistry: urea, creatinine, creatinine clearance (derived from blood creatinine
L862: value by application of the Cockcroft formula), total bilirubin, alkaline phosphatase, ALT, AST, sodium, potassium, chloride, bicarbonate, calcium, albumin, total protein, and blood glucose. A serum beta-hCG pregnancy test was also performed.
L863: c) Urinalysis: protein, glucose, WBC, erythrocytes, and bacteria.
L864: The laboratory data were analyzed and described as listings, descriptive statistics for observed values, and change from baseline. ECG data were summarized highlighting differences compared to the baseline values for quantitative variables and frequencies of treatment emergent abnormalities. Descriptive statistics were presented by treatment for the following parameters: heart rate, PR, QRS, QT, QTcB and QTcF. An outlier analysis depicted the number and proportion of patients who meet specific pre-defined criteria will be performed. The number of patients who had treatment emergent abnormalities in ECG results were summarized in frequency tables. All ECG data will be listed.
L865: Vital signs were summarized by treatment using descriptive statistics for absolute values and change from baseline, in addition to being listed.
L866: Physical examination results, apart from baseline, that were summaries in a frequency table (normal/abnormal), were listed.

L884: One patient who died experienced two TEAEs, atrial flutter [Grade 5] (onset at Day 6) and dyspnea [Grade 4] (onset at Day 12) which were both classified as severe. According to the narrative, subject (b) (6) had history of atrial flutter, advanced small cell neuroendocrine carcinoma, myocardial ischemia, and diabetes. Though the dyspnea and atrial flutter were possibly attributed to the study drug, the subject died of respiratory failure and cardiac arrest which were not attributed to the study drug. Cardiac side effects will be discussed further in Sections 8.4.5 and 8.5.1. The narratives of the other patients were reviewed, and these deaths were not likely related to either the infusion time or the study drug itself.

L909: ## 8.4.3. Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L910: ## PNET 12-23
L911: None of the subjects discontinued this study after drug administration due to an adverse event. Two subjects discontinued prior to first drug administration. In the crossover parts, three subjects
L912: received only one oral dose of 300 mg netupitant and 0.5 g palonosetron (together with placebo infusion) due to premature discontinuation of the study prior to administration of the IV dose and placebo capsule. One subject (Subject No. [b] (6) discontinued due to vasovagal syncope and dizziness which according to the narrative could have been attributed to insertion of the cannula. Infusion of the study drug was interrupted and restarted in two subjects (Subject No. [b] (6) and Subject No. [b] (6)) due to adverse events (swelling around the infusion site and strange sensation at infusion site). According to the narratives, the swelling was due to extravasation, and the sensation was mild, and interruption of the infusion resolved the event.
L913: ## PALO 15-17
L914: No patient withdrew from this study due to a TEAE.
L915: ## NEPA 15-18
L916: TEAEs leading to discontinuation from the study (the majority being not related to study drug) were reported for a total of 36 (8.9%) patients: 16 (7.9%) patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 20 (10.0%) patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group. The most frequently reported TEAEs leading to discontinuation were disease progression (6 [3.0%] patients in each of the two treatment groups) and renal failure (3 [1.5%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 1 [0.5%] patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group). Two patients (1.0%) in the Oral NEPA FDC group had TEAEs leading to discontinuation from the study that were serious not-related TEAEs leading to death (sudden death). Please see table below.
L917: In all cases, the patients that developed renal failure did so after marked periods of time after drug exposure. Subject [b] (6) developed renal failure 21 days after drug exposure. Subject [b] (6) developed renal failure 29 days after drug exposure. Subject [b] (6) experienced renal failure 51 days after drug exposure. Finally, subject [b] (6) developed renal failure 8 days after drug exposure. It is unlikely, therefore, that the renal failure is attributable to the study drug. In addition, the applicant also included language in the draft labeling concerning slightly more patients having elevated creatinine levels IV NEPA FDC treatment group. This issue is addressed in section 8.5.2.
L918: This Reviewer conducted further analyses (see Hy's Law plot in Section 8.5.2 of this review) which showed that none of the patients met Hy's Law criteria.

L922: ## 8.4.4. Significant Adverse Events
L923: ## PNET 12-23
L924: There were no serious or severe adverse events reported after drug administration. The safety evaluation after completion of the study showed a comparable safety profile after IV administration of pro-netupitant infusion solution at 2.6 mg/mL concentration and oral administration of netupitant 300 mg/palonosetron 0.5 mg. Most of the TEAEs observed belonged to the MedDRA system organ classes General disorders and administration site conditions, Nervous system disorders, and gastrointestinal disorders. The frequency of subjects with gastrointestinal disorders was higher with the oral formulation compared to the IV formulation (14.9% vs. 6.1%). The frequency of subjects presenting administration site reactions was only slightly higher with the IV formulation compared to the oral formulation (18.9% vs. 14.9%) due to vessel puncture site thrombosis at the contralateral arm observed in both groups. The most frequently reported TEAE after both administration routes was headache, which is a common and easy manageable event in clinical trials. The frequency of subjects with headache was higher with the oral formulation compared to the IV formulation (15.7% vs. 8.1%). Please refer to table below.

L943: ## 8.4.5. Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L944: ## PNET 12-23
L945: The most frequently reported TEAEs assessed as drug-related by the Investigator and DSMB were headache, constipation, infusion site thrombosis, abdominal pain upper, fatigue, dizziness, and nausea. The number of drug-related TEAEs or frequency of subjects with drug-related TEAEs did not increase with ascending pro-netupitant doses.
L946: It is plausible that the route of administration, together with the presence of palonosetron in the oral FDC formulation, could have played a role in the development of the observed events after administration of the new IV formulation and the approved oral formulation; i.e., more gastrointestinal disorders were observed after oral administration and more administration site reactions were observed after IV administration. In fact, gastrointestinal adverse events as constipation, abdominal pain upper and nausea occurred more frequently following the administration of oral netupitant 300 mg/palonosetron 0.5 mg compared to IV pro-netupitant, while infusion site thrombosis, fatigue and dizziness occurred more frequently with IV pro-netupitant administration. The most frequently reported TEAE after the active drug administration following both routes was headache. The frequency of headache was lower after IV administration (8.1% of IV treated subjects) compared to oral administration (12.7% of oral treated subjects).
L947: The local safety and tolerability profile of pro-netupitant was very good throughout all dose groups, as confirmed by thorough vascular surveillance by Doppler ultrasound examinations as well as by specific laboratory parameters. There was a total of about 283 IV infusions during the study, being applied via indwelling cannulas and there has been the same number of indwelling cannulas inserted at the contralateral site for blood drawing reasons. Altogether, a total of 38 thrombotic events have been observed. These TEAEs were reported as 28 events detected at the blood drawing sites and 10 events detected on the infusion arm, respectively. This pattern strongly suggests an underlying mechanical rather than a chemical cause. The numerical difference of AEs by almost a factor of 3 between both insertion sites may be explained by the difference in the time of presence of the cannulas inside the vein; in fact, infusion site catheters were generally withdrawn shortly after the end of the 30-minute infusion, whereas the blood drawing cannulas were generally removed after 12 hours. The different length of time of device permanence supports the mechanical origin of the observed events.
L948: No drug-related infusion site thrombosis events were observed after IV pro netupitant administration at doses of 19.5 mg, 32.5 mg, 65 mg and 130 mg pro-netupitant, nor at the two highest dose levels tested (325 mg and 390 mg pronetupitant). Few drug-related infusion site
L949: thrombosis cases were reported at the intermediate IV pro-netupitant dose levels 195 mg (1 out of 28 treated subjects, 3.6%), 234 mg (2 out of 19 treated subjects, 10.5%), 260 mg (4 out of 30 treated subjects, 13.3%) and 286 mg (1 out of 18 treated subjects, 5.6%). These data, together with the absence of thrombosis adverse events at 325 mg and 390 mg IV-pro-netupitant as well as considering the other local tolerability assessments, allows concluding on the good local tolerability of the IMP. Please refer to table below.

L953: ## PALO 15-17
L954: In general, TEAEs were distributed evenly between the treatment groups. The most frequent TEAE affecting study patients throughout the study was neutropenia reported in 119 (29.5%) patients (61 [30.0%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 58 [28.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group). The second most frequent TEAE was anemia reported in 71 (17.6%) patients (33 [16.3%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 38 [18.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group). Other frequently reported TEAEs included alopecia (32 [15.8%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 29 [14.4%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group), constipation (21 [10.3%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 26 [12.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group), and leukopenia (21 [10.3%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 22 [10.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group).
L955: The frequency of TEAEs potentially referred to local tolerability was small and reported also for the Oral treatment group (where the placebo infusion was administered); these TEAEs included: injection site extravasation (1 [0.5%] patient in the IV NEPA FDC group [verbatim term: lesion on the skin of the left forearm, probably after extravasation of last dose chemotherapy] and 0
L956: patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group); infusion site extravasation (0 patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 1 [0.5%] patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group [verbatim term: erythema on left arm after extravasation of drug]); and injection site reaction (0 patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 1 [0.5%] patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group [verbatim term: 2 episodes of cutaneous reaction in the place of injection]).

L974: ## NEPA 15-18
L975: In general, TEAEs were distributed evenly between the treatment groups. The most frequent TEAE affecting study patients throughout the study was neutropenia reported in 119 (29.5%) patients (61 [30.0%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 58 [28.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group). The second most frequent TEAE was anemia reported in 71 (17.6%) patients (33 [16.3%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 38 [18.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group). Other frequently reported TEAEs included alopecia (32 [15.8%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 29 [14.4%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group), constipation (21 [10.3%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 26 [12.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA
L976: FDC group), and leukopenia (21 [10.3%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 22 [10.9%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group).
L977: The frequency of TEAEs potentially referred to local tolerability was small and reported also for the Oral treatment group (where the placebo infusion was administered); these TEAEs included: injection site extravasation (1 [0.5%] patient in the IV NEPA FDC group [verbatim term: lesion on the skin of the left forearm, probably after extravasation of last dose chemotherapy] and 0 patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group); infusion site extravasation (0 patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 1 [0.5%] patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group [verbatim term: erythema on left arm after extravasation of drug]); and injection site reaction (0 patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 1 [0.5%] patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group [verbatim term: 2 episodes of cutaneous reaction in the place of injection]).
L978: In addition, the frequency was small and very similar between the two treatment groups for phlebitis (2 [1.0%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 1 [0.5%] patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group), phlebitis superficial (0 patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 2 [1.0%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group), thrombophlebitis (1 [0.5%] patient in each treatment group), and thrombophlebitis superficial (2 [1.0%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 1 [0.5%] patient in the Oral NEPA FDC group). L985: - Transaminase Elevations: There appeared to be an imbalance in transaminase elevations in the oral Akynzeo arm vs. the IV Akynzeo arm. However, this Reviewer conducted further analyses (see Hy's Law plot in Section 8.5.2 of this review) which showed that none of the patients met Hy's Law criteria.
- Elevated Creatinine: For a discussion of elevated creatinine, see Section 8.5.2.

L986: (Source – Reviewers Table and Section 16.2.7 Adverse Event Listings in the [REDACTED](b) (6))
L987: Study-drug-related TEAEs were reported for 26 (12.8%) patients (75 events) in the IV NEPA FDC group and 23 (11.4%) patients (71 events) in the Oral NEPA FDC group. There were only 2 TEAEs reported in \ge 2\% of patients, i.e., constipation and ALT increased. The most frequently reported study-drug-related TEAE in both treatment groups was constipation (13 [6.4%] patients in the IV NEPA FDC group and 12 [6.0%] patients in the Oral NEPA FDC group). ALT increase was experienced by 4 patients (2.0%) in each treatment group. Please see table below.

L995: ## 8.4.6. Laboratory Findings
L996: ## PNET 12-23
L997: No safety-relevant changes were observed compared to baseline, and no remarkable difference was observed between mean values of both treatments. The number of subjects with abnormal values was low for most of the parameters. None of the abnormal laboratory values were assessed as clinically significant by the Investigator, and no adverse events associated with safety laboratory abnormalities were reported.
L998: ## PALO 15-17
L999: For most hematology parameters, the mean values at baseline and the mean changes from baseline to Day 6 (the time point selected for the summary analysis) were similar between the treatment groups.
L1000: For most blood chemistry parameters, the mean values at baseline and the mean changes from baseline to Day 6 were also similar between the treatment groups.
L1001: The mean creatinine clearance value was 55.2 mL/min for the infusion group and 53.0 mL/min for the bolus group; by Day 6, the value was lower for the infusion group with respect to the bolus group (44.9 mL/min and 47.6 mL/min, respectively). For ALP, the mean count at baseline was higher for the bolus group (96.8 U/L for the infusion group, 103.2 U/L for the bolus group), but by Day 6, the mean values were almost identical (90.0 U/L and 90.1 U/L, respectively).
L1002: Other notable changes in blood chemistry parameters were similar between the treatment groups. For ALT, the mean values at baseline were 24.0 U/L for the infusion group and 22.9 U/L for the bolus group; by Day 6, the mean values increased in both groups to 40.7 U/L and 38.4 U/L, respectively. For blood urea nitrogen (BUN), the mean values at baseline were 5.48 mmol/L for the infusion group and 5.26 mmol/L for the bolus group; by Day 6, the mean values increased in both groups to 9.57 mmol/L and 9.33 mmol/L, respectively. For total bilirubin, the mean values at baseline were 8.50 \mumol/L for the infusion group and 8.88 \mumol/L for the bolus group; by Day 6, the mean values had increased substantially in both groups to 11.22 \mumol/L and 11.71 \mumol/L, respectively.
L1003: For most urinalysis parameters, the percentage of patients with a positive test result at screening was comparable between the treatment groups; frequency distributions were also similar between the treatment groups on Day 6.
L1004: Overall, the proportion of patients with values within the normal ranges at baseline and on Day 6 was similar between the treatment groups, and changes from normal to abnormal or from abnormal to normal were generally similar between the treatment groups on Day 6. In hematology, the greatest shifts from baseline in the percentage of patients with normal values were observed for monocytes/total cells and neutrophils/total cells. For monocytes/total cells, 31.1% of patients in the infusion group and 24.9% of patients in the bolus groups had shifts from normal at baseline to low measurements on Day 6. For neutrophils/total cells, 28.8% of patients in the infusion group and 27.4% of patients in the bolus groups had shifts from normal at baseline to high measurements on Day 6, as might be expected following exposure to corticosteroids, which may transiently release mature neutrophils from bone marrow.
L1005: For blood chemistry parameters, the greatest shifts from baseline normal to high ranges on Day 6 were observed for blood urea nitrogen (45.7% and 43.5% of patients in the infusion and bolus groups, respectively), ALT (23.3% and 25.6%), creatinine clearance (12.6% and 15.5%), and creatinine (10.8% and 14.0%). The greatest shifts from baseline normal to low ranges on Day 6 were observed for chloride (30.0% and 36.7% of patients in the infusion and bolus groups, respectively), creatinine kinase (31.8% and 33.8%), and calcium (23.8% and 20.8%).
L1006: The type, frequency, and intensity of clinically significant laboratory abnormalities were comparable across the treatment groups.
L1007: A number of laboratory abnormalities were recorded that are common for oncology patients receiving chemotherapy: either chemotherapy-induced toxicity (leukocytes, neutrophils, hemoglobin, platelets, ALT, ALP, AST, total bilirubin, creatinine) and/or potential corticosteroid-induced toxicity (i.e., glucose, potassium and sodium). Marked abnormalities (i.e., those of CTC grades 3 or 4) in hematological and blood chemistry variables reported during the study are summarized in the tables below.
L1008: Marked abnormalities in hematology parameters included decreased white blood cell count for 3 (1.4%) patients in the infusion group and for 2 (1.0%) patients in the bolus group, decreased neutrophil count and anemia, each for 1 (0.5%) patients in the infusion group and for 2 (1.0%)
L1009: patients in the bolus group, and decreased platelet count for 1 (0.5%) patient in the infusion group and no patients in the bolus group. Please refer to table below.

L1015: For most hematology and blood chemistry parameters, the mean values at baseline and the mean changes from baseline throughout the study were similar between the treatment groups.

L1017: Overall, the percentage of patients with values within the normal range was comparable between the treatment groups, and changes from normal to abnormal or from abnormal to normal throughout the study were generally comparable between the treatment groups. In general, the percentage of patients whose values shifted from normal to abnormal was comparable between the treatment groups.

L1020: The type, frequency, and intensity of clinically significant laboratory abnormalities were comparable across the treatment groups.

L1035: ## 8.4.7. Vital Signs
L1036: ## PNET 12-23
L1037: No safety-related changes were observed over time compared to baseline and no clinically relevant difference was observed between both treatments. No statistically significant results were obtained for blood pressure changes over doses. For pulse rate changes over doses, statistically significant results were observed at several timepoints. No clear trend was, however, observed over all doses, i.e. median values of changes increased or decreased with ascending doses. Thus, no clear trend in vital sign changes was observed for the analyzed dose levels.
L1038: There were two clinically significant abnormalities. After IV administration of 130 mg pro-netupitant, mild intermittent palpitations were reported for 1 subject (Subject No. [b] (6) about 18.5 hours after start of infusion (a.s.i.) and lasted 7 hours and 30 min. The event was assessed as possibly related to the investigational medicinal product (IMP) by the Investigator and by the DSMB. After administration of 390 mg pro-netupitant, orthostatic hypotension was observed in 1 subject (Subject No. [b] (6) about 8 hours a.s.i. and lasted 4 min. The event was assessed as possibly related to the IMP by the Investigator and by the DSMB. No other TEAEs associated with vital sign abnormalities were reported.
L1039: ## Clinical Reviewer's Comment:
L1040: *Regarding mild intermittent palpitations, this reviewer notes that the onset was 18.5 hours a.s.i. suggesting that the event is not related to the IMP. Even if it was related to the IMP as the Investigator and DSMB concluded, this reviewer does not recommend it be included in the labeling as the event was reported at low frequency in Study NEPA 15-18 (IV Akynzeo arm: 0; PO Akynzeo arm: 2/201 (1%)). The current labeling for Akynzeo PO (Section 6.1 Clinical Trials Experience) includes a table with adverse reactions occurring at \ge 3\% of patients in Cycle 1 of the single-cycle study of oral Akynzeo described.*
L1041: *The above reported orthostatic hypotension case is consistent with the description of the effects observed in the nonclinical studies; see the following (from the QT-IRT Review):*
L1042: *"In conscious dogs and in anesthetized whole animals netupitant and its metabolites or palonosetron/netupitant combinations have been associated with bradycardia, hypotension dose-dependent decreases in atrioventricular conduction (increased PR) and in ventricular depolarisation rate (prolonged QRS) and prolonged QT."*
L1043: *This Reviewer notes that orthostatic hypotension is not currently included in the labeling (Section 6 Adverse Reactions). This Reviewer notes that this adverse event occurred at a supratherapeutic dose that is not recommended in the labeling; therefore, this reviewer does not recommend that orthostatic hypotension be added to the labeling.*
L1044: ## PALO 15-17
L1045: The mean values for pulse rate, systolic blood pressure and diastolic blood pressure were similar in the two treatment groups at baseline and all time points reported thereafter. The mean changes from baseline were small, not clinically relevant and similar between the treatment groups.
L1046: ## NEPA 15-18
L1047: The pulse, systolic blood pressure, and diastolic blood pressure were analyzed with respect to changes from baseline at each study visit. The changes from baseline were not clinically significant and were reasonably comparable between the two treatment groups.
L1048: ## 8.4.8. Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1049: ## PNET 12-23
L1050: No safety-relevant changes were observed compared to baseline and no marked difference was observed between both treatments. Most of the subjects had normal ECGs (according to Investigator's interpretation) on Day -1. In both, active IV infusion and oral active treatment groups, 20 subjects (13.5% and 14.9% of the subjects, respectively) had ECG abnormalities on Day -1, all assessed as not clinically significant. During the study, ECG abnormalities were observed in less than 15% of the subjects in each treatment group on Days 1, 3, and 6. All ECG abnormalities were assessed as not clinically significant.
L1051: ## PALO 15-17

L1062: The Clinical Reviewers concluded that the numeric differences do not reflect cardiac safety differences between the products. First, there were no nonclinical findings suggesting a reason for the differences. Second, the QT evaluation results do not provide an explanation for the differences; see discussion of the QT evaluation results in Section 4.5.3 Pharmacokinetics subsection “QT Evaluation”. Third, because all patients were on background medications, is possible that these EKG findings are attributable to background medications and do not reflect a true difference.
L1063: ## 8.4.9. QT
L1064: The CDER DCRP QT Interdisciplinary Review Team (QT-IRT) determined prior to submission of the NDA that the sponsor had conducted sufficient evaluation of the QTc interval in both the oral and IV programs to support a TQT study waiver for IV NEPA FDC. The QT-IRT was consulted during the review of this NDA; see Section 4.5.3 above for a summary of their review conclusions.
L1065: ## PNET 12-23
L1066: No QTcB and QTcF intervals &gt;480 ms were observed. QTcB intervals between 450 ms and 480 ms were observed in 1 subject at baseline on Day -1 of Period 1 prior to active IV treatment, 2 subjects at baseline on Day -1 of Period 2 prior to active oral treatment and in 2 subjects after active treatment on Day 6 (1 IV treated subject and 1 oral treated subject). QTcF intervals between 450 ms and 480 ms were observed in 2 subjects at baseline on Day -1 of Period 2 prior to active oral treatment, in 4 subjects after active treatment on Day 1 (1 IV treated subject and 3 oral treated subjects), in 2 subjects after active treatment on Day 3 (1 IV treated subject and 1
L1067: oral treated subject), and in 3 subjects after active treatment on Day 6 (2 IV treated subjects and 1 oral treated subject).
L1068: No QTcB or QTcF changes from baseline &gt; 60 ms were observed. QTcB changes from baseline between 30 ms and 60 ms were observed after active treatment in 2 subjects (1 IV treated and 1 oral treated subject) on Day 1, 4 subjects (2 IV treated and 2 oral treated subject) on Day 3, and 5 subjects (4 IV treated and 1 oral treated subject) on Day 6. QTcF changes from baseline between 30 ms and 60 ms were observed after active treatment in 2 subjects (2 IV treated subject) on Day 6.
L1069: None of the ECG findings, including the QTcB and QTcF observed changes described above, was assessed as clinically significant by the Investigator and no TEAEs associated with ECG abnormalities were reported.
L1070: ## PALO 15-17
L1071: At Visit 4, the mean (SD) PR intervals had decreased slightly compared to baseline in both treatment groups; the mean change observed with the 30-minute infusion was similar to that of the 30 second bolus group [-7.2 (14.9) msec for the infusion group vs. -9.3 (13.9) msec for the bolus group]. Thus, no greater risk of effects from PR interval decreases from baseline is expected with the 30-minute infusion of palonosetron in the proposed product vs. the approved administration of palonosetron in Aloxi IV of 30 seconds. At Visit 4, changes from baseline in HR, QRS interval, QT interval, QTcB interval and QTcF interval were small and similar between treatment groups. See additional discussion in Section 4.5.3 of this Review.
L1072: QT prolongation was identified as an AE of special interest for the study, and changes in QT intervals were monitored closely. At baseline, the mean (SD) QTcF interval was comparable between treatment groups: 412.5 (22.7) msec for the infusion group and 409.7 (22.8) msec for the bolus group. At Visit 4, the mean (SD) changes in QTcF interval from baseline were small and comparable between treatment groups: -0.8 (22.3) msec for the infusion group and 1.7 (21.3) msec for the bolus group. Please see table below.

Only one patient (0.4%, infusion group) recorded a measurement outside the criteria of HR decrease \ge 25\% and &lt;50 bpm.
L1077: No patient met outlier criteria for PR interval. For QRS and QT intervals, the percentages of patients whose ECG data met outlier criteria were comparable between the treatment groups. The highest percentage of patients whose ECG data met the QT interval outlier criteria were in the range of QT increase of &gt;30 and \le 60 msec, affecting 11.1% and 10.2% of patients in the infusion and bolus groups, respectively. QT increases of &gt;60 msec affected 3.1% and 5.1% of patients in the infusion and bolus groups, respectively. QTcB outliers were reported for similar proportions of patients in the infusion and bolus groups in each outlier category (from \le 450 msec, \le 480 msec, and \le 500 msec at baseline to &gt;450 msec, &gt;480 msec &gt;500 msec, respectively, at Visit 4). Only 3 (1.4%) patients in the bolus group had an increase in QTcB from \le 500 msec at baseline to a value of &gt;500 msec. QTcB increases of &gt;30 and \le 60 msec affected 3.1% and 6.5% of patients in the infusion and bolus groups, respectively, while increases of &gt;60 msec affected 2.7% and 0.9% of patients in the respective groups. Comparing to QTcB outliers, QTcF outliers were reported for lower proportions of patients in the infusion and bolus groups in each outlier category. Only one (0.5%) patient in the bolus group had an increase in QTcF from \le 500 msec at baseline to a value of &gt;500 msec. QTcF increases of &gt;30 and \le 60 msec affected 4.9% and 5.1% of patients in the infusion and bolus groups, respectively, while increases of &gt;60 msec affected 1.8% and 0.9% of patients in the respective groups. Please see table below.

L1081: ## NEPA 15-18
L1082: At Cycle 1 Visit 4 (120 h post-dose), there was a slight mean (SD) decrease from baseline in PR interval that was comparable between the treatment groups (-10.8 [16.7] msec for the IV NEPA FDC group and -10.1 [14.1] msec for the Oral NEPA FDC group). Similar decreases from baseline in PR interval were observed at Visit 4 during Cycles 2, 3, and 4 for both treatment groups.
L1083: QT prolongation was identified as an AE of special interest for the study, and changes in QT intervals were monitored closely. At Cycle 1 Visit 4 (120 h post-dose), there was a slight mean (SD) decrease from baseline in QTcF interval that was comparable between the treatment groups (-3.0 [19.5] msec for the IV NEPA FDC group and -5.2 [17.7] msec for the Oral NEPA FDC group). Similar decreases from baseline in QTcF interval were observed at Visit 4 during Cycles 2, 3, and 4 for both treatment groups.
L1084: Changes for heart rate and all other intervals were small and comparable between treatment groups.
L1085: For all ECG parameters over all cycles, the percentages of patients who had ECG data that met the outlier criteria were comparable between the treatment groups. The highest percentage of patients who met the outlier criteria was for heart rate increase from baseline \ge 25\% and &gt;100 bpm for all cycles at Visit 4, 120 h post-dose (6.4% for the IV NEPA FDC group and 8.0% for the Oral NEPA FDC group).
L1086: Across all cycles, the percentage of patients with a QTcB post-baseline value &gt;500 msec and baseline value ≤500 msec was similar for both treatment groups and less than 1.0% overall at each timepoint. The percentage of patients with QTcB change from baseline &gt;60 msec was also similar for both treatment groups and less than or equal to 2.0% overall at each timepoint. Please see table below.

L1104: ## 8.5.1. PALO 15-17 – Additional Analysis
L1105: ## Specific TEAEs Occurring at ≥2% Higher Frequency in Infusion Arm vs. Bolus Arm
L1106: The percentage of patients who experienced at least 1 TEAE was slightly higher in the IV palonosetron infusion group, 85 (37.8 %), as opposed to IV palonosetron bolus group, 77 (35 %). In addition, there was slightly more diarrhea and hypertension in the 30-minute infusion group. Please see table below.

L1110: (Source – Reviewers Table)
L1111: These adverse events are addressed in the Aloxi (palonosetron) labels. Furthermore, given the multiple other confounding variables (concurrent chemotherapy administration, overall health of the subjects, etc.), these findings cannot be conclusively attributed to the longer infusion time of the palonosetron.

L1150: The number of patients with normal creatinine levels that shifted to a high level at the end of the study was 11 for the IV arm and 10 for the oral arm. L1151: ## Transaminase Elevations
L1152: Finally, the number of patients in the NEPA 15-18 study with an increased ALT in the IV arm was 15 (7.4%) and 24 (11.9%) in the oral arm. L1157: ## 8.7. Additional Safety Explorations
L1158: ## 8.7.1. Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1159: Assessments of Human Carcinogenicity and/or Tumor Development were not made in any of the human trials previously discussed (PNET 12-23, PALO 15-18, and NEPA 15-18). Please see Section 4.4.
L1160: ## 8.7.2. Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1161: No patients became pregnant during the three studies in this development program. As such, no further information on pregnancy and lactation was obtained from these studies.
L1162: ## 8.7.3. Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1163: There are active two post-marketing requirements in place (for Akynzeo Capsules):
L1164: 2769-2 - A PK/PD dose finding study of netupitant to characterize the netupitant PK/PD relationship for complete response in the delayed phase following oral administration of a single dose of netupitant given concomitantly (in separate formulations) with an oral single administration of palonosetron in pediatric cancer patients ages 0 to 17 years undergoing treatment with emetogenic chemotherapy, including highly emetogenic chemotherapy. You must conduct this study with an age appropriate formulation.
L1165: Final Protocol Submission: 11/01/2015
Study Completion: 04/30/2018
Final Report Submission: 09/30/2018
L1166: 2769-3 - An adequate, well-controlled, double-blind, randomized study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of a dose of the netupitant/palonosetron fixed combination compared to standard therapy in pediatric cancer patients ages 0 to 17 years undergoing treatment with emetogenic chemotherapy, including highly emetogenic chemotherapy. You must conduct this study with an age appropriate formulation.
L1167: Final Protocol Submission: 04/30/2019
Study Completion: 12/31/2021
Final Report Submission: 04/30/2022

L1180: The PeRC agreed with the Division to grant a deferral in all pediatric studies because the product is ready for approval in adults.
L1181: ## 8.7.4. Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1182: No specific information is available on the treatment of overdosage with Akynzeo® for injection. The highest dose of fosnetupitant administered to 203 cancer patients in a multiple cycle study was 235 mg (the recommended dose). The highest fosnetupitant dose administered to 9 healthy subjects was 353 mg (1.5 times the recommended dose). In addition, there is a low expectation of abuse potential, withdrawal, and/ or rebound with administration of Akynzeo® for injection based upon its mechanism of action and context of use.
L1183: The Controlled Substances Staff (CSS) concluded that Akynzeo for Injection should not be recommended for control under the Controlled Substances Act (CSA) because “the
L1184: results from the preclinical abuse potential studies show that the drug combination lacks abuse potential.” This conclusion is primarily based on the Applicant’s submission of pharmacokinetic information showing that fosnetupitant converts completely to netupitant in rats, dogs, and monkeys, and information showing that fosnetupitant is not CNS-active because it cannot cross the blood-brain barrier (although its metabolite, netupitant, does penetrate the CNS). Although CSS informed the Applicant (in a meeting in 201421) that additional self-administration studies with fosnetupitant alone or with the combination of fosnetupitant + palonosetron would not be necessary, the Applicant conducted two self-administration studies in baboons with netupitant (the active metabolite of fosnetupitant) and with netupitant + palonosetron anyway; CSS concluded that neither drug treatment produced self-administration that was statistically significantly different than that produced by placebo, thus suggesting that these drug treatments do not produce rewarding effects.
L1185: ## 8.8. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1186: ## 8.8.1. Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1187: 21Minutes of December 9, 2014 Meeting filed in DARRTS under IND 115,191
L1188: The Division of Pharmacovigilance – 1 (DPV-1) conducted a postmarket safety analysis dated August 29, 2016. This review was conducted in fewer than 10,000 patients, and at the time of its completion, Akynzeo® had been approved for 18 months. This analysis involved conducting a FAERS database search. The results are summarized in the following table.

L1196: (Source – DPV-1 Review Page 3)
L1197: A search of the FAERS database from October 10, 2014 (date of US approval) through August 25, 2016 yielded 15 reports; 11 reported a serious outcome. There were no reports with a serious outcome of death. Of the 27 most frequently reported MedDRA PTs, four were labeled events (Alanine aminotransferase increased, Constipation, Fatigue, Type I hypersensitivity) within the Akynzeo product label and six were indication-related or related to the patient's underlying disease (Nausea, Vomiting, Disease progression, Drug ineffective, Dehydration, Retching). The remaining 17 most frequently reported MedDRA PTs were associated with six unique reports. Because each of the six reports contained strong alternative causes for the adverse event (such as receipt of concomitant chemotherapy medications or progression of underlying disease), no causal role of Akynzeo with any of the unlabeled events could be established.
L1198: The postmarket safety summary suggested no new potential safety issues requiring additional safety reviews.
L1199: DPV-1 conducted another FAERS search on 11/22/2017 which identified 38 adverse event reports with netupitant and palonosetron. Of the 38 reports, 4 were duplicates, 2 contained limited information for assessment, and 1 did not report an adverse event. Of the remaining 31 reports, four were associated with a serious outcome of death. The four reports of death were not attributable to netupitant and palonosetron; two were from disease progression, one was from ileus in a patient who received multiple concomitant medications, and one did not provide a cause of death.
L1200: Twenty-six non-fatal reports described events that were indication-related (nausea/vomiting n=6), related to the patient's underlying disease (disease progression n=3, pain n=1), associated with strong alternative causes (n=13), or were already contained in the netupitant and palonosetron product label (allergic reaction n=2, headache n=1). The 13 reports that contained strong alternative causes described the events of cytopenias (n=9), cardiac dysfunction (n=2), or dermatitis (n=1) after receipt of concomitant chemotherapy and back pain from pre-existing degenerative vertebral changes (n=1). No change in frequency or severity was noted with the three reports of the labeled adverse events of allergic reaction (n=2) and headache (n=1).
L1201: The remaining non-serious report contained the unlabeled event of euphoric mood. The report described a 75-year-old female receiving denosumab and erlotinib (administration dates unknown) for treatment of lung cancer, who felt "high" after receiving netupitant and palonosetron for prophylaxis of nausea and vomiting. Netupitant and palonosetron was discontinued and the patient recovered on an unknown date. Euphoric mood is listed in the Adverse Reactions (Section 6.1) of the Aloxi (palonosetron) label.
L1202: *Reviewer Comment: No safety issues/signals were identified that are expected to require further post-marketing requirements or commitments for Akynzeo® for injection.*
L1203: ## 8.8.2. Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1204: Please see Section 8.8.1. There were no safety issues identified in this drug development program that would be cause for concern in the post-marketing setting.
L1205: ## 8.9 Additional Safety Issues from Other Disciplines
L1206: There are no additional safety issues from other disciplines requiring discussion here.
L1207: ## 8.10 Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1208: PNET 12-23 was conducted in healthy subjects. No new safety signals were identified in this study.
L1209: The PALO 15-17 study's focus was determining non-inferiority the 30-minute infusion dosing of IV palonosetron compared to the previously approved 30 second bolus dosing. Patients in this trials did not receive both components of Akynzeo® for injection. As such, the safety focus was to evaluate for safety signals were related to change in the infusion duration. In general, the TEAEs were evenly balanced between the two treatment arms. The adverse events that were higher in the 30-minute infusion arm were not new issues and had been addressed in the Aloxi label. Review of the narratives for cardiac events that occurred in the 30- minute infusion arm revealed that the events could not be conclusively attributed to the change in duration in infusion. Moreover, the patients in the 30-minute infusion arm collectively had more cardiac diseases in their medical histories. As such, no new safety signals were identified in this study.
L1210: The NEPA 15-18 trial was the dedicated safety study. The subjects were cancer patients, and they received both components of the drug. Again, the TEAEs were evenly balanced between the two treatment arms. For the most part, there were no new adverse events that were not already addressed in the label. The two exceptions were nausea and blood creatinine increase. When analyzed using shift tables, the numbers of subjects with normal creatinine levels that became elevated by the end of the study was similar between the two treatment arms. Nausea was considered part of the underlying disease (i.e., CINV) rather than a true adverse event. Again, no new safety signals were identified in this study.
L1211: Akynzeo® for injection does not appear to have new safety signals based on the data from PNET 12-23, PALO 15-18, and NEPA 15-18.
L1212: *Reviewer Comment: From a safety perspective, Akynzeo® for injection poses no additional risks to cancer patients compared to oral Akynzeo®.*
L1213: ## 9 Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations

L1219: FDA Proposed	Rationale
Combining the Labels of oral and IV Akynzeo®	
In a meeting that occurred with the Sponsor on November 2016, the Division encouraged the Sponsor to combine oral and IV products into one label unless there was a compelling reason to separate them, such as a different indication. The Division determined that the information of the two products was similar enough to keep the labels together.

1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE
AKYNZEO for injection is indicated in combination with dexamethasone in adults for the prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy not including anthracycline plus cyclophosphamide chemotherapy.
“in combination with dexamethasone”: Per the labeling regulations (21 CFR 201.57), if a drug is used for an indication only in conjunction with a primary mode of therapy (e.g., another drug), a statement that the drug is indicated as an adjunct to that mode of therapy must be included in the Indications and Usage section.
“not including anthracycline plus cyclophosphamide chemotherapy”: The applicant has not submitted a study that establishes the contribution of the fosnetupitant component of Akynzeo® for injection to prevention of delayed phase CINV in cancer patients receiving anthracycline plus cyclophosphamide chemotherapy. Intravenous palonosetron is approved for prevention of delayed phase CINV, based on a trial that primarily enrolled patients treated with anthracycline plus cyclophosphamide chemotherapy. As such, this statement is required per prior agreement between the Division and applicant.

14 CLINICAL STUDIES
Study 5
This language must be deleted because the study was not designed to answer an efficacy question. As such, efficacy claims could not be made in the label per prior agreement between the Division and the Sponsor. This safety study is presented in Section 6 (Adverse Reactions) of the label.

L1221: ## 6.1 Clinical Trials Experience
L1222: ## Akynzeo for Injection
L1223: The safety of AKYNZEO for injection was evaluated in 203 patients in an active-controlled multi-cycle (median 4 cycles, range of 1-4 cycles) safety clinical study in patients receiving HEC (including cisplatin, cyclophosphamide, carmustine, dacarbazine and mechlorethamine) compared to 201 patients receiving AKYNZEO capsules (NCT 02517021). The median age was 60 years, 46% were female, 99.5 % were White, 0.3% were Asian, and 0.3% were Hispanic. All patients received a single dose of AKYNZEO for injection 30 minutes prior to the start of each chemotherapy cycle; dexamethasone was co-administered with AKYNZEO. The safety profile of AKYNZEO for injection was generally similar to that seen with AKYNZEO capsules.

L1299: ## 1. Executive Summary
L1300: ## 1.1 Introduction
L1301: AKYNZEO IV is a fixed-dose combination of two active drug substances, fosnetupitant chloride hydrochloride and palonosetron hydrochloride. Fosnetupitant is quickly converted to netupitant in vivo. Netupitant is a substance P/NK-1 (neurokinin-1) receptor antagonist. Palonosetron is a 5-HT3 receptor antagonist.
L1302: Oral AKYNZEO® (capsules) was approved as a fixed-dose combination of 300 mg netupitant and 0.5 mg palonosetron equivalent in 2014 under NDA 205718 for the prevention of chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting (CINV).
L1303: Palonosetron HCl is approved as a single agent for prevention of CINV under the tradename ALOXI® (0.25 mg solution for intravenous injection, under NDA 21372). An oral formulation (50 mg softgel capsules) was approved under NDA 22233, but was later withdrawn from marketing.
L1304: The sponsor seeks market approval for AKYNZEO injection for the prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy.
L1305: ## 1.2 Brief Discussion of Nonclinical Findings
L1306: Given the rapid metabolic conversion of fosnetupitant to netupitant, the Agency accepted the sponsor's proposal to conduct intravenous toxicity studies of fosnetupitant alone and the combination of fosnetupitant + palonosetron using a maximum duration of 4 weeks, to support marketing approval. It is noted that the toxicology program for oral Akynzeo included oral studies of up to 6 months in rats and 9 months in dogs using netupitant alone, and 3-month studies of the combination of netupitant + palonosetron in rats and dogs. The toxicity studies of fosnetupitant alone and the combination of fosnetupitant + palonosetron did not produce any novel findings, as compared to the toxicology program for oral Akynzeo.
L1307: Fosnetupitant alone was tested in intravenous toxicity studies of up to 4 weeks in rats and dogs.
L1308: In the 4-week intravenous toxicity study in rats, fosnetupitant was tested at 0, 3.95, 13.16, and 39.47 mg/kg/day. The high dose of 39.47 mg/kg/day is considered as the NOAEL.
L1309: In the 4-week intravenous toxicity study in dog, fosnetupitant was tested at 0, 1.32, 3.95, and 13.16 mg/kg/day. The high dose of 13.16 mg/kg/day was NOAEL.
L1310: 4-Week intravenous toxicity studies with the combination of fosnetupitant and palonosetron were conducted in rats and dogs. In the 4-week intravenous toxicity study in rats, fosnetupitant was tested at 3.95, 13.16, and 39.47 mg/kg/day in combination with palonosetron at 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg/day, respectively. The high dose of 39.47/10 mg/kg/day was the NOAEL.
L1311: In the 4-week intravenous toxicity study in dogs, fosnetupitant in combination with palonosetron was tested at 1.316 + 1 mg/kg/day, 3.95 + 3 mg/kg/day, and 13.16 + 10/6 mg/kg/day. The dose of 10 mg/kg/day palonosetron was reduced to 6 mg/kg/day on day 15 due to convulsions. The treatment resulted in convulsion, salivation, and swelling at injection site in high dose group. Treatment increased heart rate and prolonged QT_c intervals in the mid- and high-dose groups. The dose of 1.316 mg/kg/day fosnetupitant + 1 mg/kg/day palonosetron was the NOAEL.
L1312: Fosnetupitant was negative in the Ames test, in vitro chromosome aberration test in human lymphocytes, and the in vivo rat micronucleus test.
L1313: In an embryo-fetal development study in rats, intravenous administration of 39.47 mg/kg/day fosnetupitant (3 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) produced delayed ossification of pubis. No effects on embryo-fetal development were observed with IV administration of up to 13.16 mg/kg/day fosnetupitant in rats (2 times the human AUC for netupitant). In a rabbit embryo-fetal development study, an increase in resorptions was observed with intravenous administration of fosnetupitant at 6.25 mg/kg/day and higher in rabbits (9 times the human AUC for fosnetupitant and 0.4 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy). No effects were observed in rabbits at 3.13 mg/kg/day (5.4 times the human AUC for fosnetupitant and 0.4 times the human AUC for netupitant). In rabbits, the embryo-fetal effects of intravenous fosnetupitant differed from the effects of oral netupitant, and this difference can be attributed to the minimal transformation of fosnetupitant to netupitant in rabbits, in contrast to the rapid metabolism of fosnetupitant in rats and humans.
L1314: In a pre-/postnatal study in rats, intravenous administration of 39.47 mg/kg/day fosnetupitant in rats (3 times the human AUC for netupitant) produced lower
L1315: bodyweight in offspring at birth through maturation, and delayed physical development (pinna detachment, eye opening, and preputial separation). These effects were associated with maternal toxicity (reduced weight gain and food consumption). No effects occurred in offspring or dams at 13.16 mg/kg/day (2 times the human AUC for netupitant).
L1316: Fosnetupitant produced no effects on fertility or early embryonic development in rats, at doses up to 39.47 mg/kg IV.
L1317: ## 1.3 Recommendations
L1318: ## 1.3.1 Approvability
L1319: From a nonclinical standpoint, there are no approvability issues.
L1320: ## 1.3.2 Additional Nonclinical Recommendations
L1321: None.
L1322: ## 1.3.3 Labeling
L1323: ## Established Pharmacologic Class (EPC) in HIGHLIGHTS
L1324: The following statement containing the proposed EPC (FDA text phrase) for fosnetupitant appears in the Indications and Usage section of the Highlights:
L1325: *AKYNZEO is a (b) (4) combination of palonosetron, a serotonin-3 (5-HT3) receptor antagonist, and netupitant or fosnetupitant, (b) (4) a substance P/neurokinin-1 (NK-1) receptor antagonist*
L1326: The term, “*substance P/neurokinin-1 (NK-1) receptor antagonist*”, is the FDA text phrase for netupitant, shown in the eList. Since fosnetupitant is rapidly converted to netupitant following administration, the proposed fosnetupitant EPC, “*substance P/neurokinin-1 (NK-1) receptor antagonist*”, is acceptable.
