L1331: ## Animal Data
L1332: Daily administration of up to 30 mg/kg netupitant in rats (3.7 times the human AUC at the recommended single human dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) during the period of organogenesis produced no effects on
L1333: embryo-fetal development. However, an increased incidence of external and skeletal abnormalities in rabbit fetuses was observed following daily administration of netupitant in rabbits at 10 mg/kg/day and higher (0.2 times the human AUC at the recommended single human dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) during the period of organogenesis. These abnormalities included positional abnormalities in the limbs and paws, and fused sternebrae. Reduction in fetal rabbit weight occurred at 30 mg/kg/day. Maternal toxicity in rabbits (i.e., loss of bodyweight during the treatment period) was also observed at 30 mg/kg/day. Daily administration of up to 30 mg/kg netupitant (3.7 times the human AUC at the recommended human dose) in rats during organogenesis through lactation produced no adverse effects in the offspring.
L1334: (b) (4)
L1335: In animal reproduction studies with palonosetron, no effects on embryo-fetal development were observed in pregnant rats given oral doses up to 60 mg/kg/day (921 times the recommended human oral dose based on body surface area) or pregnant rabbits given oral doses up to 60 mg/kg/day (1841 times the recommended human oral dose based on body surface area) during the period of organogenesis.
L1336: **Evaluation:** The proposed version is compliant with the PLLR format, and the text that includes information on studies with oral netupitant or palonosetron is the same as that of the currently approved label for Akynzeo® capsules. Minor changes are recommended for this text, as a result of the addition of studies with intravenous fosnetupitant. We also recommend changes in the text and the animal to human AUC ratios (fosnetupitant and netupitant) for the intravenous studies with fosnetupitant. The animal and human AUC values that were used to calculate the recommended AUC ratios are shown in the tables presented in the Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation. The recommended version below
L1337: was developed in collaboration with the Maternal Health review team (Carrie Ceresa, Jane Liedtka, and Miriam Dinatale).
L1338: ## **Recommended Version:**
L1339: ## **Risk Summary**
L1340: Limited available data with AKYNZEO use in pregnant women are insufficient to inform a drug-associated risk of adverse developmental outcomes.
L1341: In animal reproduction studies with netupitant, no effects on embryo-fetal development were observed following daily oral administration in pregnant rats during the period of organogenesis at doses up to 3.7 times the human AUC (area under the plasma concentration-time curve) at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy. However, a dose-dependent increase in adverse effects on embryo-fetal development was observed following daily oral administration of netupitant in pregnant rabbits during the period of organogenesis with doses at least 0.2 times the human AUC at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy. Daily oral administration of netupitant in rats up to 3.7 times the human AUC at the recommended dose during organogenesis through lactation produced no adverse effects in the offspring (see Data).
L1342: In animal reproduction studies with fosnetupitant, delayed ossification of pubis occurred after intravenous administration in rats during the period of organogenesis at a dose 3 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy. In pregnant rabbits, an increase in resorptions was observed with daily intravenous administration of fosnetupitant during the period of organogenesis at doses up to 9 times the human AUC for fosnetupitant and 0.4 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy. Daily intravenous administration of fosnetupitant (3 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) in rats during organogenesis through lactation produced lower bodyweight in offspring at birth through maturation, and delayed physical development (see data).
L1343: In animal reproduction studies with palonosetron, no effects on embryo-fetal development were observed following oral administration during the period of organogenesis at doses up to 921 and 1841 times the recommended oral dose in rats and rabbits, respectively (see Data).
L1344: Based on animal data from netupitant studies, advise pregnant women of the potential risk to a fetus.
L1345: The estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage for the indicated populations are unknown. All pregnancies have a background risk of birth defect, loss, or other adverse outcomes. In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2 to 4% and 15 to 20%, respectively.
L1346: ## Data
L1347: ## Animal Data
L1348: ## Netupitant
L1349: Daily oral administration of up to 30 mg/kg netupitant in rats (3.7 times the human AUC at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) during the period of organogenesis produced no effects on embryo-fetal development. However, an increased incidence of external and skeletal abnormalities in rabbit fetuses was observed following daily oral administration of netupitant in rabbits at 10 mg/kg/day and higher (0.2 times the human AUC at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) during the period of organogenesis. These abnormalities included positional abnormalities in the limbs and paws, and fused sternebrae. Reduction in fetal rabbit weight occurred at 30 mg/kg/day. Maternal toxicity in rabbits (i.e., loss of bodyweight during the treatment period) was also observed at 30 mg/kg/day. Daily oral administration of up to 30 mg/kg netupitant (3.7 times the human AUC at the recommended dose) in rats during organogenesis through lactation produced no adverse effects in the offspring.
L1350: ## Fosnetupitant
L1351: Daily intravenous administration of 39 mg/kg/day fosnetupitant in rats (3 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) during the period of organogenesis produced delayed ossification of pubis. No effects on embryo-fetal development were observed with daily administration of up to 13 mg/kg fosnetupitant in rats (2 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy). Due to the limited systemic exposure to fosnetupitant in pregnant rats, it is not possible to provide an AUC-based comparison of fosnetupitant exposure in rats and humans. An increase in resorptions was observed with daily intravenous administration of fosnetupitant at 6 mg/kg/day and higher in rabbits (9 times the human AUC for fosnetupitant and 0.4 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) during the period of organogenesis. No effects were observed in rabbits at 3 mg/kg/day (5.4 times the human AUC for fosnetupitant and 0.4 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended
L1352: single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy). Daily intravenous administration of 39 mg/kg fosnetupitant in rats (3 times the AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) during organogenesis through lactation produced lower bodyweight in offspring at birth through maturation, and delayed physical development (pinna detachment, eye opening, and preputial separation). These effects were associated with maternal toxicity (reduced weight gain and food consumption). No effects occurred in offspring or dams at 13 mg/kg/day (2 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy).
L1353: ## Palonosetron
L1354: In animal reproduction studies with palonosetron, no effects on embryo-fetal development were observed in pregnant rats given oral doses up to 60 mg/kg/day (921 times the recommended oral dose based on body surface area) or pregnant rabbits given oral doses up to 60 mg/kg/day (1841 times the recommended oral dose based on body surface area) during the period of organogenesis.
L1355: ## Sponsor's Proposed Version:
L1356: ## 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility
L1357: ## Netupitant
L1358: Long-term studies in animals to evaluate carcinogenic potential have not been performed with netupitant. Netupitant was not genotoxic in the Ames test, the mouse lymphoma cell mutation test, or the *in vivo* rat micronucleus test.
L1359: Daily oral administration of netupitant in rats at doses up to 30 mg/kg (1.9 times the human AUC in male rats and 3.7 times the human AUC in female rats at the recommended human dose) had no effects on fertility or reproductive performance.
L1360: ## Fosnetupitant
L1361: Long-term studies in animals to evaluate carcinogenic potential have not been performed with fosnetupitant.
L1362: Fosnetupitant was not genotoxic in the Ames test and *in vivo* rat micronucleus test. In human lymphocytes did not induce structural chromosomal aberrations.
L1363: (b) (4)
L1364: (b) (4)
L1365: ## Palonosetron
L1366: In a 104-week carcinogenicity study in CD-1 mice, animals were treated with oral doses of palonosetron at 10, 30, and 60 mg/kg/day. Treatment with palonosetron was not tumorigenic. The highest tested dose produced a systemic exposure to palonosetron (plasma AUC) of about 90 to 173 times the human exposure (AUC=49.7 ng•h/mL) at the recommended oral dose of 0.5 mg. In a 104-week carcinogenicity study in Sprague-Dawley rats, male and female rats were treated with oral doses of 15, 30, and 60 mg/kg/day and 15, 45, and 90 mg/kg/day, respectively. The highest doses produced a systemic exposure to palonosetron (plasma AUC) of 82 and 185 times the human exposure at the recommended dose. Treatment with palonosetron produced increased incidences of adrenal benign pheochromocytoma and combined benign and malignant pheochromocytoma, increased incidences of pancreatic islet cell adenoma and combined adenoma and carcinoma and pituitary adenoma in male rats. In female rats, it produced hepatocellular adenoma and carcinoma and increased the incidences of thyroid C-cell adenoma and combined adenoma and carcinoma.
L1367: Palonosetron was not genotoxic in the Ames test, the Chinese hamster ovarian cell (CHO/HGPRT) forward mutation test, the ex vivo hepatocyte unscheduled DNA synthesis (UDS) test, or the mouse micronucleus test. It was, however, positive for clastogenic effects in the Chinese hamster ovarian (CHO) cell chromosomal aberration test. Palonosetron at oral doses up to 60 mg/kg/day (about 921 times the recommended human oral dose based on body surface area) was found to have no effect on fertility and reproductive performance of male and female rats.
L1368: **Evaluation:** The text that includes information on studies with oral netupitant or palonosetron is the same as that of the currently approved label for Akynzeo® capsules. The fertility study of fosnetupitant in rats was conducted using intravenous administration, not oral administration
L1369: (b) (4)
L1370: We recommend changes in the text and the animal to human AUC ratios (fosnetupitant and netupitant) for this study. The animal and human AUC values that were used to calculate the recommended AUC ratios are shown in the tables presented in the Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation. Since TK evaluation was not performed in the fertility study with fosnetupitant, AUC values from the 4-week intravenous toxicity study in rats (mean of male female values) were used for the calculations.
L1371: ## **Recommended Version:**
L1372: ## **13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis and Impairment of Fertility**
L1373: ## Netupitant
L1374: Long-term studies in animals to evaluate carcinogenic potential have not been performed with netupitant. Netupitant was not genotoxic in the Ames test, the mouse lymphoma cell mutation test, or the *in vivo* rat micronucleus test.
L1375: Daily oral administration of netupitant in rats at doses up to 30 mg/kg (1.9 times the human AUC in male rats and 3.7 times the human AUC in female rats at the recommended human dose) had no effects on fertility or reproductive performance.
L1376: ## Fosnetupitant
L1377: Long-term studies in animals to evaluate carcinogenic potential have not been performed with fosnetupitant.

L1379: Daily intravenous administration of fosnetupitant in rats at doses up to 39 mg/kg (1.3 times the human AUC for fosnetupitant and 4.3 times the human AUC for netupitant at the recommended single dose to be given with each cycle of chemotherapy) had no effects on fertility or reproductive performance.
L1380: ## Palonosetron
L1381: In a 104-week carcinogenicity study in CD-1 mice, animals were treated with oral doses of palonosetron at 10, 30, and 60 mg/kg/day. Treatment with palonosetron was not tumorigenic. The highest tested dose produced a systemic exposure to palonosetron (plasma AUC) of about 90 to 173 times the human exposure (AUC=49.7 ng•h/mL) at the recommended oral dose of 0.5 mg. In a 104-week carcinogenicity study in Sprague-Dawley rats, male and female rats were treated with oral doses of 15, 30, and 60 mg/kg/day and 15, 45, and 90 mg/kg/day, respectively. The highest doses produced a systemic exposure to palonosetron (plasma AUC) of 82 and 185 times the human exposure at the recommended dose. Treatment with palonosetron produced increased incidences of adrenal benign pheochromocytoma and combined benign and malignant pheochromocytoma, increased incidences of pancreatic islet cell adenoma and combined adenoma and carcinoma and pituitary adenoma in male rats. In female rats, it produced hepatocellular adenoma and carcinoma and increased the incidences of thyroid C-cell adenoma and combined adenoma and carcinoma.
L1382: Palonosetron was not genotoxic in the Ames test, the Chinese hamster ovarian cell (CHO/HGPRT) forward mutation test, the ex vivo hepatocyte unscheduled DNA synthesis (UDS) test, or the mouse micronucleus test. It was, however, positive for clastogenic effects in the Chinese hamster ovarian (CHO) cell chromosomal aberration test. Palonosetron at oral doses up to 60 mg/kg/day (about 921 times the recommended oral dose based on body surface area) was found to have no effect on fertility and reproductive performance of male and female rats.

L1427: ## 2.6 Proposed Clinical Population and Dosing Regimen
L1428: AKYNZEO for injection is indicated for prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy.
L1429: One vial of AKYNZEO for injection containing 235 mg fosnetupitant equivalent and 0.25 mg palonosetron equivalent will be reconstituted in 50 ml of 5%
L1430: dextrose injection or 0.9% sodium chloride injection, which will be given via 30-minute infusion starting approximately 30 minutes prior to the chemotherapy.
L1431: ## Regulatory Background
L1432: AKYNZEO (fosnetupitant and palonosetron) for injection was developed under IND 115191 for the prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy. A pre-NDA meeting was held on December 23, 2016. The sponsor agreed to submit final study reports of all required nonclinical studies. All study reports needed to support approval were submitted in this NDA.
L1433: Oral AKYNZEO® (capsules) was approved as a fixed-dose combination of 300 mg netupitant and 0.5 mg palonosetron equivalent in 2014 under NDA 205718 for the prevention of chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting (CINV).
L1434: Palonosetron HCl is approved as a single agent for prevention of CINV under the trade name ALOXI® (0.25 mg solution for intravenous injection under NDA 21372.

L1451: ## 4 Pharmacology
L1452: ## 4.1 Primary Pharmacology
L1453: Fosnetupitant is quickly converted to netupitant in vivo. Netupitant is a substance P/ NK-1 (neurokinin-1) receptor antagonist. For information on the pharmacology of netupitant and its metabolites, see the Pharmacology/ Toxicology review of NDA 205718 by Dr. Ke Zhang, dated 06/19/2014. Palonosetron is a 5-HT3 receptor antagonist. Some published reports suggest that a combination of a NK1 receptor antagonist and a 5-HT3 receptor antagonist is more effective in the control of acute and delayed emesis as compared to a 5-HT3 receptor antagonist alone.
L1454: ## In vitro studies:
L1455: Binding assays were conducted to assess fosnetupitant in a broad panel of 80 receptors and ion channels. The investigations with an initial batch of
L1456: fosnetupitant (batch no: 2014-Netu/219/1) revealed that it produced more than 50% inhibition of the following ligand binding activities: hNK1 agonist (IC_{50} &lt; 0.28 nM), hNK3 antagonist and agonist ligands (IC_{50} = 0.8 \muM and IC_{50} = 0.42 \muM, respectively), h5-HT6 agonist (IC_{50} = 6.3 \muM), Ca2+ channel (diltiazem site) antagonist (IC_{50} = 2 \muM), and Ca2+ channel (verapamil site) antagonist (IC_{50} = 5.4 \muM) (PNET-12-32).
L1457: An additional receptor/ion channel screening assay with a purified batch of fosnetupitant (batch no. SJ-506-Pool-X) was conducted on the same panel of receptors and ion channels, and the results indicated that fosnetupitant with the purified batch also induced more than 50% inhibition on the same receptors/ion channels as in the above binding study (PNET-13-12).
L1458: The in vitro effect of fosnetupitant vs netupitant has been investigated in CHO cells expressing human NK1 receptors (calcium mobilization assay induced by Substance P) and in the assay for Substance P-induced contraction of guinea pig ileum. In CHO cells, 100 nM fosnetupitant was more effective than 100 nM netupitant in causing a rightward shift of the Substance P response with pKb values of 8.72 and 7.54 for fosnetupitant and netupitant, respectively. In guinea pig ileum preparations, both fosnetupitant and netupitant inhibited the Substance P-induced contractions with pKa ~9 when pre-incubated for 5 minutes. However, with a 2-minute pre-incubation, only fosnetupitant inhibited the Substance P-induced contractions (Peptides 69: 26–32, 2015, Ruzza et al).
L1459: ## **In vivo studies:**
L1460: In mice, intravenous administration of netupitant and fosnetupitant (0.1, 1 and 10 mg/kg) 120 minutes before dosing with Substance P produced significant dose-dependent inhibition of nociceptive behavior, including scratching, biting, and licking, as induced by Substance P.
L1461: ## **4.2 Secondary Pharmacology**
L1462: No studies with fosnetupitant were submitted. For information on the secondary pharmacology of netupitant, see the Pharmacology/Toxicology review of NDA 205718 by Dr. Ke Zhang, dated 06/19/2014.
L1463: ## **4.3 Safety Pharmacology**
L1464: ## **Central Nervous System:**
L1465: A single IV dose of fosnetupitant at 13.16 and 39.47 mg/kg had no effects on behavioral, neurological, or autonomic functions in rats. At a higher dose of 118.42 mg/kg, fosnetupitant induced ataxic gait, increased pain response, and increased heart and respiratory rates.
L1466: Single IV doses of fosnetupitant at 13.16, 39.47 and 79 mg/kg did not enhance metrazol-induced convulsant activity (twitches and tremors) in rats, but at a dose of 79 mg/kg, fosnetupitant resulted in tonic convulsion in one rat. In another study in rats, single IV doses of fosnetupitant at 13.16, 39.47 and 79 mg/kg did not have any effects on metrazol-induced seizures.
L1467: ## **Respiratory:**
L1468: Single intravenous doses of fosnetupitant at 13.2, 39.5 and 79.2 mg/kg did not have any effects on respiratory functions in rats.
L1469: ## **Cardiovascular:**
L1470: ## **In Vitro studies:**
L1471: Fosnetupitant at 10 \muM and 30 \muM slightly inhibited the delayed rectifier potassium current (I_{Kr}) encoded by hERG in stably transfected human embryonic kidney (HEK293) cells by ~7-8% as compared to vehicle control (-1.8%). In previous studies using a CHO-cell expression system, netupitant and its metabolites M1, M2, and M3 also inhibited hERG channel potassium currents, with IC_{50} values of 0.76, 0.84, 43, and 4.4 \muM, respectively. For information on the cardiovascular pharmacology of netupitant and its metabolites, see the Pharmacology/Toxicology review of NDA 205718 by Dr. Ke Zhang, dated 06/19/2014.
L1472: ## **In Vivo studies:**
L1473: Repeated intravenous dosing of fosnetupitant at 1.32, 3.95, and 13.2 mg/kg/day for 2 weeks did not have any effects on cardiovascular functions including blood pressure, heart rate, and ECG in conscious dogs.
L1474: ## **Fosnetupitant/palonosetron combination:**
L1475: Repeated intravenous combination doses of fosnetupitant and palonosetron at 1.32/1, 3.95/3, and 13.2/6 mg/kg/day for 2 weeks had no effects on blood pressure, heart rate, or body temperature in conscious dogs. However, at a dose of 13.2/6 mg/kg/day, the combination of fosnetupitant and palonosetron prolonged PR, PQ, and QT intervals, and QRS complex duration.
L1476: ## **Renal/Urinary systems:**
L1477: A single intravenous dose of fosnetupitant at 13.2 mg/kg had no effects on renal function in rats. At higher doses of 39.5 and 79.0 mg/kg, fosnetupitant decreased urine output and K^+ and Cl^- excretion.
L1478: ## **Gastrointestinal system:**
L1479: Single intravenous doses of fosnetupitant at 13.2 and 39.5 mg/kg had no effects on gastrointestinal motility in rats. At 79.0 mg/kg, fosnetupitant significantly decreased the gastrointestinal transit of a charcoal meal (52.3% of total length of small intestine) as compared to vehicle control (65.5%).

L1538: ## Distribution:
L1539: ## Rat:
L1540: To evaluate the tissue distribution, [14C]-fosnetupitant was given intravenously at 6.5 mg/kg to albino or pigmented rats (PNET-13-39). The total radioactivity in blood and tissues was measured using whole-body autoradioluminography. The highest level of radioactivity was detected in the harderian gland, followed by pituitary in males, meibomian gland in females and lungs. L1549: ## Dog
L1550: Measurement of drug distribution in heart tissue can be found in the 4-week IV toxicity studies in dogs (see General Toxicology section below).
L1551: ## Protein binding studies
L1552: The human plasma protein binding of fosnetupitant was approximately 92-95% (PNET-15-90).
L1553: The mean blood to plasma concentration ratio in humans was ~0.4-0.6 (PNET-15-91).
L1554: ## **Metabolism:**
L1555: Following IV administration, fosnetupitant was quickly converted to netupitant and then to the three major netupitant metabolites (M1, M2 and M3) in rats, dogs, and monkeys. The metabolic pathway is depicted in the following figure (submitted by the sponsor).
L1556: Figure 6: Proposed Pathways for the Formation of Major Metabolites of Fosnetupitant (methylene netupitant)
L1557: Following IV administration in rats, fosnetupitant was quickly converted to netupitant in plasma. M1 was the major metabolite and accounted for 27% of the total radioactivity. M2 and M3 together accounted for approximately 4% of the total radioactivity in plasma. Feces contained 76% of the administered dose, and the major metabolite was M1 (~13-16% of the total radioactivity). Bile contained ~35% of the administered dose and only very small fraction (&lt;0.2%) of the administered dose was found in urine.
L1558: In dogs, M2 was the major metabolite identified in plasma following an IV dose of fosnetupitant.
L1559: ## **Excretion:**
L1560: The excretion pattern of fosnetupitant is similar to that of netupitant. The administered dose of fosnetupitant was quickly converted to netupitant, which was eliminated by biliary excretion and hepatic metabolism, mainly mediated by CYP3A and to a lesser extent by CYP2C9 and CYP2D6.
L1561: Following IV administration of radiolabeled fosnetupitant, elimination of radioactivity was mainly found in bile and feces, with very small fraction in urine in both rats and dogs. For information on the excretion of netupitant, see the Pharmacology/Toxicology review of NDA 205718 by Dr. Ke Zhang, dated 06/19/2014.
L1562: ## **Effects on liver enzymes and human transporters:**
L1563: The inhibitory effect of fosnetupitant on CYP isoforms was evaluated in human liver microsomes. The results indicated that fosnetupitant produced slight to moderate inhibition of CYP2C9 (IC_{50} = 27.9 \muM), CYP2C19 (IC_{50} = 46.1 \muM), CYP2C8 (IC_{50} = 56.7 \muM), CYP2B6 (IC_{50} = 106.8 \muM), CYP1A2 (IC_{50} = 208.4 \muM), CYP2D6 (IC_{50} = 217.2 \muM), and CYP3A4 (IC_{50} = 196.8 or 299.2 \muM).
L1564: The results of fosnetupitant on human efflux and uptake transporters were summarized in the following tables (submitted by the sponsor).

L1578: ## Rat:
L1579: **Study title / number:** Pronetupitant Phosphate 4 Week Intravenous Toxicity Study in Rats Followed by A 2-Week Recovery Period / PNET-13-18
L1580: **Conducting laboratory and location:** [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L1581: **GLP compliance:** Yes
L1582: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L1583: In a 4-week intravenous toxicity study in rats, fosnetupitant was tested at 0, 3.95, 13.16, and 39.47 mg/kg/day (equivalent to 3, 10 and 30 mg/kg/day of netupitant, respectively). The high dose of 39.47 mg/kg/day is considered as the NOAEL.
L1584: ## **Methods:**
L1585: **Dose and frequency of dosing:** daily dose of 3.95, 13.16 and 39.47 mg/kg (equivalent to 3, 10 and 30 mg/kg/day of netupitant)
L1586: **Route of administration:** IV
L1587: **Formulation/Vehicle:** 5% glucose
L1588: **Species/Strain:** Wistar Hannover rats
L1589: **Number/Sex/Group:** 10/sex/group
L1590: **Age:** 6-8 weeks old
L1591: **Satellite groups/unique design:** 3 (control) or 12/sex/group
L1592: **Deviation from study protocol affecting interpretation of results:**
L1593: None

L1691: ## **Study title/ number:** Pronetupitant Phosphate – Palonosetron 4-week Intravenous Administration Toxicity Study in Dogs with A 2-Week Treatment-Free Period / NEPA-13-52 (b) (4) Study # 8292-336)
L1692: **Key Study Findings:** Fosnetupitant (pronetupitant phosphate) in combination with palonosetron was tested at 1.316 + 1 mg/kg/day, 3.95 + 3 mg/kg/day, and 13.16 + 10 / 6 mg/kg/day. The dose of 10 mg/kg/day palonosetron was reduced
L1693: to 6 mg/kg/day on day 15 due to convulsions. The test article produced convulsions, salivation, and swelling at injection site in the high dose group. Increase heart rate and prolonged QTc intervals occurred in the mid- and high-dose groups. The dose of 1.316 mg/kg/day fosnetupitant + 1 mg/kg/day palonosetron was the NOAEL.

L1738: ## General toxicology (additional studies)
L1739: 2-Week IV toxicity studies were conducted in rats (#PNET-11-21) and dogs (#PNET-11-22). Intravenous doses of 0, 3.95, 13.16, and 39.47 mg/kg/day (equivalent to 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day of netupitant, respectively) was tested in rats, and 0, 1.32, 3.95, and 13.16 mg/kg/day (equivalent to 0, 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg/day of netupitant, respectively) was tested in dogs. The results of these studies were similar to those of the 4-week studies of fosnetupitant (reviewed above), in which the same doses were tested.

L1873: ## 9.2 Embryonic Fetal Development
L1874: **Study title/ number:** Pronetupitant Phosphate Intravenous Embryo-Foetal Development Study in Rats with Toxicokinetics /PNET-13-56
L1875: **Key Study Findings:** One high-dose fetus (39.47 mg/kg/day) had a malrotated hindlimb and 6 high-dose fetuses from 3 different litters had no ossification of pubis. One high-dose fetus was found dead. Maternal bodyweight was decreased in the high-dose group. In pregnant rats treated with 3.95 or 13.16 mg/kg/day, there were no effects on litter parameters, including the number of viable fetuses, fetal weight, resorptions, or malformations (skeletal or visceral). The NOAEL was 13.16 mg/kg/day.
L1876: **Conducting laboratory and location:** [REDACTED] (b) (4)

**Formulation/Vehicle:** 5% glucose
**Species/Strain:** Wistar Hannover rats
**Number/Sex/Group:** 24
**Satellite groups:** See table below
**Study design:** See table below
L1881: Fosnetupitant was given to pregnant rats by IV injection at 0 (vehicle), 3.95, 13.16, and 39.47 mg/kg/day during gestation days 6-17. The females were sacrificed on day 20 post coitum and assessment of embryo-fetal development was conducted.

L1944: ## 9.3 Prenatal and Postnatal Development
L1945: **Study title and number:** Fosnetupitant Intravenous Pre- and Postnatal Toxicity Study in Rats, Including Maternal Function / PNET-15-85
L1946: **Key Study Findings:** The high dose (39.47 mg/kg/day) produced a reduction in body weight gain in F0 and F1 rats (birth through maturation), and increases in pre-birth loss, missing pups or pups found dead (particularly males), and clinical signs such as cold to touch, absence of milk intake, small size, and pale appearance. The mid-dose of 13.16 mg/kg/day was the NOAEL for dams and their offspring (F0 and F1 generations, respectively).
L1947: (b) (4)

L1949: **GLP compliance:** Yes

L2042: ## 10 Special Toxicology Studies
L2043: ## Local Tolerance Studies:
L2044: The local tolerance of fosnetupitant and palonosetron combination was evaluated following single intravenous, para-venous, and intra-arterial doses in male New Zealand White rabbits (NEPA-14-33). Study design is summarized in the following table (taken from the study report).
L2045: ## Fosnetupitant + Palonosetron
L2046: Group Number	Treatment Site*	Treatment Item	Route of admin.	Dose Volume (mL/site/animal)	Animal Numbers
1	Right ear	Control Item	Intravenous	0.5	2, 4, 6, 8
2	Right ear	Control Item	Intra-arterial	0.5	10, 12, 14, 16
3	Right ear	Control Item	Paravenous	0.2	18, 20, 22, 24
4	Right ear	Test Item	Intravenous	0.5	26, 28, 30, 32
5	Right ear	Test Item	Intra-arterial	0.5	34, 36, 38, 40
6	Right ear	Test Item	Paravenous	0.2	42, 44, 46, 48
L2047: * Each animal served as its own control. The left ear remained untreated and acted as control.
L2048: The final drug product contained 260 mg of fosnetupitant and 0.25 mg of palonosetron as a lyophilized powder, and was reconstituted prior to administration. The final concentration was 13 mg/ml and 0.0125 mg/mL of fosnetupitant and palonosetron, respectively.
L2049: No histopathological change was observed following intravenous or intra-arterial administration; however inflammation occurred at the injection site following para-venous injection. Ulceration of cutis, necrosis, and hemorrhage of dermis were also noted following para-venous injection in one rabbit.
L2050: The local tolerance of fosnetupitant alone was evaluated following single intravenous, para-venous, and intra-arterial doses in male New Zealand White
L2051: rabbits (PNET-14-12). The study design is summarized in the following table (taken from the study report).
L2052: ## Fosnetupitant
L2053: Group Number	Treatment Site	Treatment	Route of admin.	Dose Volume (mL/site/animal)	Animal Numbers
1	Right ear	Control item	Intravenous	0.5	2, 4, 6, 8
2	Right ear	Control item	Intra-arterial	0.5	10, 12, 14, 16
3	Right ear	Control item	Paravenous	0.2	18, 20, 22, 24
4	Right ear	Test item	Intravenous	0.5	26, 28, 30, 32
5	Right ear	Test item	Intra-arterial	0.5	34, 36, 38, 40
6	Right ear	Test item	Paravenous	0.2	42, 44, 46, 48
L2054: The final drug product contained 130 mg of fosnetupitant in 10 ml vehicle (13 mg/ml).
L2055: Intravenous injection resulted in perivascular and/or vascular minimal chronic inflammation in two treated rabbits, with minimal perivascular necrosis in one of these two rabbits. Treatment-related changes including scab formation, epidermal hyperplasia, chronic inflammation, focal minimal hemorrhage, and edema of dermis were noted following para-venous injection at the injection site. No histopathological change was observed following intra-arterial administration.
L2056: Application of 0.5 mL solution containing fosnetupitant at 13 mg/mL to the prepared dorsal skin of New Zealand White female rabbits for 4 hours resulted in slight erythema (PNET-13-44).
L2057: Fosnetupitant at 13 mg/mL did not produce eye irritation in bovine corneas (PNET-13-65).
L2058: ## Skin Sensitization Tests:
L2059: Intradermal injection of fosnetupitant (20%) did not induce a sensitization response in guinea pigs (PNET-13-46).
L2060: Fosnetupitant did not induce anaphylaxis in the active systemic anaphylaxis test in guinea pigs (PNET-14-10).
L2061: Fosnetupitant did not induce anaphylaxis in the passive cutaneous anaphylaxis test in guinea pigs (PNET-14-11).
L2062: ## **In Vitro Hemolysis Study:**
L2063: Fosnetupitant and netupitant at concentrations up to 500 µg/ml did not have hemolytic effects in human blood (NETU-09-18).
L2064: ## **Phototoxicity:**
L2065: Fosnetupitant tested positive in the in vitro phototoxicity test using Balb/c 3T3 cells (PNET-13-66). The biological relevance of this finding is uncertain.
L2066: ## 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L2067: AKYNZEO injection is a fixed-dose combination of two active drug substances, fosnetupitant chloride hydrochloride and palonosetron HCl. Fosnetupitant is quickly converted in vivo to netupitant, an NK1 receptor antagonist. Palonosetron is a 5-HT3 receptor antagonist. Oral AKYNZEO® (capsules) was approved in 2014 as a fixed-dose combination of 300 mg netupitant and 0.5 mg palonosetron (free base equivalent) under NDA 205718 for the prevention of chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting (CINV). Palonosetron hydrochloride is an approved drug that is available as Aloxi® Injection (approved in 2003). An oral formulation, Aloxi® Capsules, was approved in 2008 but was later withdrawn from marketing. Aloxi® Injection is indicated for prevention of CINV and prevention of PONV (postoperative nausea and vomiting). The approved IV dose is 0.25 mg and the approved oral dose is 0.5 mg (doses expressed as the free base equivalent). The sponsor seeks approval of AKYNZEO injection for prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy in adults.
L2068: Given the rapid conversion of fosnetupitant to netupitant in humans and in most animal species, the in vivo pharmacological profile for fosnetupitant is assumed to be highly similar to that of netupitant. Fosnetupitant at 10 and 30 µM slightly inhibited the delayed rectifier potassium current (I_{Kr}) mediated by the hERG channel in stably transfected human embryonic kidney (HEK293) cells. Repeated intravenous dosing of fosnetupitant at doses up to 13.2 mg/kg/day for 2 weeks did not have any effects on cardiovascular function, including blood pressure, heart rate, and ECG in conscious dogs. Repeated intravenous combination doses of fosnetupitant and palonosetron at 1.32/1, 3.95/3, and 13.2/6 mg/kg/day for 2 weeks had no effects on blood pressure, heart rate, or body temperature in conscious dogs. However, at a dose of 13.2/6 mg/kg/day, the combination of fosnetupitant and palonosetron prolonged PR, PQ, and QT intervals, and QRS complex duration.
L2069: Following intravenous administration, fosnetupitant was quickly converted to netupitant in rats, dogs, and monkeys. The human plasma protein binding of
L2070: fosnetupitant was approximately 92-95%. Netupitant exhibits high protein binding (&gt;99%) in plasma in rats, dogs, and humans. Three major metabolites (M1, M2, and M3) were identified in rats, dogs, and humans. Following IV administration of radiolabeled fosnetupitant, elimination of radioactivity was mainly found in the bile and feces, with only a very small fraction in urine for both rats and dogs. The excretion pattern of fosnetupitant is similar to that of netupitant. The administered dose of fosnetupitant was quickly converted to netupitant, which was eliminated mainly by biliary excretion.
L2071: Given the rapid metabolic conversion of fosnetupitant to netupitant, the Agency accepted the sponsor's proposal to conduct intravenous toxicity studies of fosnetupitant alone and the combination of fosnetupitant + palonosetron using a maximum duration of 4 weeks. It is noted that the toxicology program for oral Akynzeo included oral studies of up to 6 months in rats and 9 months in dogs using netupitant alone, and 3-month studies of the combination of netupitant + palonosetron in rats and dogs. The toxicity studies of fosnetupitant alone and the combination of fosnetupitant + palonosetron did not produce any novel findings, as compared to the toxicology program for oral Akynzeo.
L2072: Fosnetupitant alone was tested in intravenous toxicity studies of up to 4 weeks in rats and in dogs. In the 4-week intravenous toxicity study in rats, fosnetupitant was tested at 0, 3.95, 13.16, and 39.47 mg/kg/day (equivalent to 3, 10 and 30 mg/kg/day of netupitant, respectively). The high dose of 39.47 mg/kg/day is considered as the NOAEL.
L2073: In the 4-week intravenous toxicity study in dogs, fosnetupitant was tested at 0, 1.32, 3.95, and 13.16 mg/kg/day. The high dose of 13.16 mg/kg/day was the NOAEL.
L2074: 4-Week intravenous toxicity studies with the combination of palonosetron and fosnetupitant were conducted in rats and dogs. In the 4-week intravenous toxicity study in rats, fosnetupitant was tested at 3.95, 13.16, and 39.47 mg/kg/day in combination with palonosetron at 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg/day, respectively. The high dose of 39.47/10 mg/kg/day was the NOAEL.
L2075: Fosnetupitant in combination with palonosetron was tested at 1.316 + 1 mg/kg/day, 3.95 + 3 mg/kg/day, and 13.16 + 10 / 6 mg/kg/day in dogs. The dose of 10 mg/kg/day palonosetron was reduced to 6 mg/kg/day on day 15 due to convulsion. The treatment resulted in convulsion, salivation, and swelling at the injection site in high dose group. The test article increased heart rate and prolonged the QT_c intervals in mid- and high-dose groups. The dose of 1.316 + 1 mg/kg/day was NOAEL.
L2076: Fosnetupitant was negative in the Ames test, in vitro chromosome aberration test using human lymphocytes, and the in vivo rat micronucleus test.
L2077: Long-term studies in animals to evaluate carcinogenic potential of fosnetupitant are not needed to support approval for the proposed indication; therefore no carcinogenicity studies with fosnetupitant were conducted.
L2078: In the segment I fertility and early embryonic development study in rats, male rats were treated with pronetupitant at 3.95, 13.16, or 39.47 mg/kg by IV administration for four weeks prior to mating, continuing throughout the mating period and up to day 7 post-coitum. Female rats were treated with the same IV doses for two weeks prior to mating, continuing throughout the mating period and up to day 7 post-coitum. Both males and females were sacrificed on day 15 post-coitum. The high dose of 39.47 mg/kg/day was the NOAEL.
L2079: In the segment II embryo-fetal development study in rats, fosnetupitant was given to pregnant rats by IV injection at 0 (vehicle), 3.95, 13.16, or 39.47 mg/kg/day during gestation days 6-17. One high-dose fetus showed hindlimb malrotation and 6 high-dose fetuses from 3 different litters had no ossification of pubis. One high-dose fetus was found dead. No malformations were detected in the control, low-, or mid-dose groups. The NOAEL was 13.16 mg/kg/day.
L2080: In the segment II embryo-fetal development study in rabbits, pregnant rabbits were treated with fosnetupitant at 3.13, 6.25, and 12.5 mg/kg/day by intravenous injection during Day 6 through Day 19 post-coitum. The high dose of 12.5 mg/kg/day was considered the NOAEL for maternal toxicity. The low dose of 3.13 mg/kg/day was the NOAEL for developmental toxicity based on the increased number of resorptions at doses of 6.25 mg/kg/day or higher. The embryo-fetal effects in this study differed from the effects of oral netupitant in rabbits, and this difference can be attributed to the minimal transformation of fosnetupitant to netupitant, in contrast to the rapid metabolism of fosnetupitant in rats and humans.
L2081: In the pre-/postnatal development study in rats, female rats were treated with fosnetupitant at 0, 3.95, 13.16, and 39.47 mg/kg/day by intravenous injection from implantation (Day 6 post-coitum) through weaning (Day 20 post-partum). The high dose reduced body weight gain in the F0 and F1 rats. The offspring had lower bodyweight at birth through maturation, and delayed physical development (pinna detachment, eye opening, and preputial separation). These effects were associated with maternal toxicity (reduced weight gain and food consumption). The mid-dose of 13.16 mg/kg/day was the NOAEL for dams and pups (F0 and F1 generations, respectively).
L2082: The table below summarizes the animal to human AUC multiples for fosnetupitant calculated from the animal AUC values associated with the NOAEL.

L2090: The high doses tested in general toxicity studies in rats and dogs were NOAELs, and the animal to human AUC multiples for netupitant ranged from 3.7 to 7.1. These data support the recommended human dose with little safety concern. It is noted that oral administration of netupitant in repeated dose toxicity studies produced phospholipidosis, which was not seen in the intravenous toxicity studies with fosnetupitant (Pharmacology/Toxicology review of NDA 205718 by Dr. Ke Zhang, dated 06/19/2014).
L2091: In summary, fosnetupitant alone was tested intravenously for up to 4-weeks in rats and in dogs. No treatment-related changes were noted in these studies. Intravenous toxicity studies of 4-weeks duration with the combination of fosnetupitant and palonosetron were performed in rats and dogs. The combination of fosnetupitant and palonosetron did not produce additional toxicity as compared to either compound given alone, based on comparison to the intravenous toxicity studies with fosnetupitant alone or the intravenous toxicity studies previously conducted with palonosetron alone. Fosnetupitant was
L2092: negative in the Ames test, in vitro chromosomal aberration test, and in vivo rat micronucleus test.

L2104: ## 12 Appendix/Attachments
L2105: ## QSAR Assessments for Impurities:
L2106: Statistical-based and expert rule-based methods were used for the QSAR assessment of 19 potential impurities for the Ames test (DMF (b) (4), Section 3.2.S.3.2). The statistical-based QSAR method included three different models: ACD/Percepta statistical model, Leadscope Genetox QSAR Statistical Suite, and Vega Mutagenicity statistical model. The expert rule-based method also included three models: ACD/Percepta expert rule-based system, Leadscope Genetox Expert Alert Model, and Toxtree (a Benigni/Bossa rulebase for mutagenicity). The assessment for each method (statistical and expert-based) compared results across the three respective models in order to reach a consensus prediction. L2132: ## 1 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
L2133: The applicant submitted one phase 3 efficacy study with IV palonosetron in cancer patients (PALO-15-17) and one phase 3 safety study with fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC (NEPA-15-18) to support the use of IV fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC in preventing acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy.
L2134: Before conducting these two phase 3 studies, the applicant also conducted three phase 1 studies (two PK and safety studies in healthy volunteers, i.e., PNET-12-23 and PNET-13-63, with fosnetupitant administration, and one PK and safety study in cancer patients, i.e., NEPA-15-19, with fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC administration). Based on the FDA's advice after negotiation, the ultimate requirements for the IV NEPA FDC clinical development program should include a bioequivalence trial (PNET-12-23), a non-inferiority (NI) efficacy trial on the palonosetron component (PALO-15-17), a clinical safety trial (NEPA-15-18) and two clinical pharmacology trials (i.e., PNET-13-63, drug-drug interaction in healthy volunteers; NEPA-15-19, PK in cancer patients).
L2135: The statistical reviewer had carefully evaluated this application and successfully confirmed the applicant's efficacy findings in Study PALO-15-17. The applicant had successfully demonstrated the efficacy of IV palonosetron component in preventing acute nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of HEC. However, the rate of complete response (CR) in the delayed phase for IV NEPA FDC arm was observed to be 9% more lower than that for the concurrent oral Akynzeo arm in the safety Study NEPA-15-18. In addition, the observed CR rate in the delayed phase was 12% more lower than that for oral Akynzeo in Study NETU-07-07. Whether the data supported the efficacy of delayed phase for IV fosnetupitant-palonosetron is deferred to the clinical pharmacology reviewer's evaluation.
L2136: In conclusion, data of Study PALO-15-17 indeed supported the efficacy of IV Akynzeo in preventing the nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy (HEC) only for the acute phase.
L2137: ## 2 INTRODUCTION
L2138: ## 2.1 OVERVIEW
L2139: The applicant is seeking the following indication for AKYNZEO® (fosnetupitant and palonosetron) for injection, for intravenous use, for the prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy. In the US, oral Akynzeo® (netupitant + palonosetron HCl) capsules were approved (NDA 205718) in October 2014 for the prevention of both the acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeated courses of chemotherapy, including but not limited to highly emetogenic chemotherapy (HEC). Fosnetupitant is a neputitant prodrug. In Europe, Akynzeo® was approved in May 2015. The therapeutic indication is also the prevention
L2140: of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting but associated with highly emetogenic cisplatin-based cancer chemotherapy and moderately emetogenic cancer chemotherapy.
L2141: Nausea and vomiting are among the side effects associated with cancer treatment that patients and their families fear the most (Laslo J, 1981; Griffin AM, 1996; De Boer-Dennert M, 1997; Hofman M, 2004; Basch E, 2010). The negative aspects of nausea and vomiting can influence all facets of a patient's life. If nausea and vomiting are not controlled in a cancer patient, serious metabolic problems such as fluid and electrolyte balance disturbances and nutritional status deficiencies can develop. Psychological problems associated with nausea and vomiting may include anxiety and depression. In addition, uncontrolled nausea and vomiting may also lead to the decision by the physician to reduce chemotherapy dose intensity or to the wish by the patient to stop potentially beneficial cancer therapy (Oo T.H., 2005). CINV is classified as either acute, i.e., occurring within the first 24 hours after chemotherapy, or delayed, i.e., occurring after the first 24 hours, extending until the fifth day. The development of acute emesis is known to largely depend on serotonin (5-HT). CINV mainly occurs due to input from the chemoreceptor trigger zone (CTZ).
L2142: The 5-HT3 receptor has been demonstrated to selectively participate in the emetic response, thus providing a physiologic explanation for the demonstrated and clinically useful antiemetic effects of 5-HT3 receptor antagonists (RAs). The 5-HT3 and NK1 Receptor Antagonists (RA) are among the drugs of choice for an optimal antiemetic prophylaxis in cancer patients receiving chemotherapy; clinical practice guidelines in oncology (Navari et al, 2016) recommend that patients receiving HEC or MEC regimens should be treated with a combination of a 5-HT3 RA, NK1 RA and a systemic corticosteroid. 5-HT3 RAs are designed to block one or more of the signals that cause nausea and vomiting. The most sensitive signal during the first 24 hours after chemotherapy appears to be 5-HT3 mediated. Approved 5-HT3 inhibitors include: ondansetron (Zofran®), granisetron (Kytril®), dolasetron (Anzemet®) and palonosetron (Aloxi®). Aprepitant (Emend®) was the first selective NK1 RA approved for CINV in the US (2003), in combination with other antiemetic agents, with a subsequent IV formulation (fosaprepitant, prodrug of aprepitant) approved. In 2015, oral rolapitant (Varubi®) was approved and marketed in the US for prevention of delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of emetogenic cancer chemotherapy, including, but not limited to highly emetogenic chemotherapy.
L2143: Helsinn developed an oral NEPA fixed-dose combination (FDC), which includes a 5-HT3 receptor antagonist (palonosetron, 0.5 mg) and an NK1 receptor antagonist (netupitant, 300 mg) in the form of a hard-gelatin capsule to be administered as a single dose 1 hour (h) prior to chemotherapy. In the US, oral NEPA FDC (Akynzeo® capsules) was approved in October 2014 for the prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeated courses of chemotherapy, including but not limited to highly emetogenic chemotherapy (HEC). In Europe, Akynzeo® was approved in May 2015. The therapeutic indication is the prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with highly emetogenic cisplatin-based cancer chemotherapy and moderately emetogenic cancer chemotherapy.
L2144: An IV formulation (IV NEPA FDC) was developed as a benefit for patients who are not able to tolerate an oral formulation. The IV NEPA FDC development program was initially designed and proposed based on a bioequivalence approach, with the concept that IV NEPA FDC was a line extension of oral NEPA FDC and the precedent being the approval of fosaprepitant (Emend®).
L2145: After negotiating with FDA, the ultimate requirements of the IV NEPA FDC clinical development program were to include a bioequivalence trial (PNET-12-23), a non-inferiority efficacy trial on the palonosetron component (PALO-15-17), a clinical safety trial (NEPA-15-18) and two clinical pharmacology trials (PNET-13-63, drug-drug interaction in healthy volunteers; NEPA-15-19, PK in cancer patients).
L2146: Although the focus of this NDA is the IV NEPA FDC data, much of the information is derived from the oral Akynzeo® development program (ref. NDA 205718) which comprised 23 phase 1 trials (20 in healthy volunteers, 2 in patients with cancer and 1 in patients with hepatic impairment), one phase 2 dose-ranging efficacy trial and 3 phase 3 efficacy and safety trials in cancer patients. In addition, additional supportive safety information from the 2 studies conducted (b) (4) was included.
L2147: Overall, the applicant submitted reports and results for three phase 1 studies (two PK and safety studies in healthy volunteers, PNET-12-23 and PNET-13-63, with fosnetupitant administration, one PK and safety study in cancer patients, NEPA-15-19, with fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC administration), one phase 3 efficacy study with IV palonosetron in cancer patients (PALO-15-17) and one phase 3 safety study with fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC (NEPA-15-18) to support their prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy (see Table 1). In this statistical review, we only focus on the efficacy Study PALO-15-17.
L2148: **Table 1: Overview of Clinical Trials Providing Key Efficacy and Safety Data for the IV NEPA FDC Program**

L2159: ## 12/09/2014 meeting minutes:
L2160: The indication for Aloxi I.V. in HEC is limited to acute phase; therefore, it is not necessary to show the contribution of intravenous netupitant to delayed phase in HEC.
L2161: FDA recommended the application “conduct an additional study with a non-inferiority design comparing the efficacy of the two palonosetron infusions (palonosetron 0.25 mg I.V. infusion for 30 min vs. palonosetron 0.25 mg I.V. infusion for 30 seconds), in the setting of HEC. The primary endpoint should be CR, defined as no emetic episodes and no rescue medication, in the acute phase, and the primary objective should be to demonstrate non-inferiority of the efficacy of the new proposed infusion time compared to the approved rapid infusion”.
L2162: ## Type B teleconference minutes on 2/4/2015:
L2163: ## Clinical program
L2164: The favorable PK, safety and efficacy data from PNET-12-23, NEPA-15-18 and PALO-15-17 studies collectively comprise the IV NEPA FDC NDA clinical development program as agreed with FDA and are considered adequate to support the proposed indication cited above.
L2165: - Fosnetupitant (Netupitant) acute and delayed phase HEC efficacy and safety data supporting the proposed IV NEPA FDC indication.
- Palonosetron (30- minute infusion) HEC acute phase efficacy and safety data supporting the proposed IV NEPA. FDC indication.
L2166: ## NI margin (FDA response to Question 1)
L2167: Regarding the non-inferiority margin, we understand that we accepted your proposed 15% margin for the analysis of Study PALO-10-01. However, we cannot agree to this margin for your proposed new study. Because the margin used for the original comparison of I.V. palonosetron 0.25 mg/30 second infusion to ondansetron in Study PALO-99-05 was 15%, allowing for a
L2168: margin of 15% for the current proposed comparison would allow as much as 30% inferiority of I.V. palonosetron 0.25 mg/30 minute infusion to ondansetron.
L2169: To ensure the efficacy of intravenous palonosetron 0.25 mg/30 minute infusion will not be more than 15% inferior to ondansetron (the same standard used in Study PALO-99-05), we recommend you use ondansetron as a comparator; or if you choose to study the 30 minute infusion with a comparator arm of intravenous palonosetron 0.25 mg/30 second bolus, we recommend the margin should be 6% (=15-9), since the lower bound of the 97.5% confidence interval of I.V. palonosetron 0.25 mg/30 second bolus effect from Study PALO-99-05 was 9%, when it was compared to ondansetron. To account for the variability associated with reliance on evidence from one historical trial, if you choose to pursue a comparison of the two palonosetron infusions, we recommend the margin be 10%.
L2170: ## **Discussion Summary:**
L2171: Based on previous precedent of FDA's acceptance of the 15% noninferiority (NI) margin in the PALO-10-01 study, which was a NI study comparing IV and oral palonosetron (FDA agreement to NI margin indicated in the SPA no agreement letter issued June, 18, 2010, in response to May 4, 2010 SPA request for PALO-10-01), ***FDA and the applicant agreed that the NI margin of 15% is acceptable based on the applicant's plan to use a two-sided 99% confidence interval for the PALO-15-XX study.***
L2172: ## **2/27/2015 teleconference:**
L2173: Design, population and primary endpoint, PALO-15-XX (which became PALO-15-17). "We agree that the design of study PALO-15-XX is adequate to support efficacy of the palonosetron component infused over 30 minutes in I.V. Akynzeo. Specifically, we agree with the planned primary endpoint. However, your proposed indication would need to be revised (b) (4)
L2174: indication. For example, the indication could be revised to state Akynzeo I.V. is indicated for prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with HEC, (b) (4) with a limitation of use stating that it has not been studied in the setting of anthracycline plus cyclophosphamide chemotherapy."
L2175: "We agree with the design of study NEPA-15-YY (which became NEPA-15-18) describe the safety of I.V. Akynzeo. Specifically, we agree with the choice of comparator, study duration, and planned safety assessments. As your proposed indication is for HEC only, you need to have adequate number of patients who will receive HEC in this study, because (b) (4)
L2176: Should a safety signal be found, additional studies and/or patients may be required."
L2177: The overall I.V. FDC NDA clinical program was proposed to be comprised of the following five studies to support the target indication: "prevention of acute and delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy. Akynzeo is an intravenous (b) (4) combination of palonosetron and pronetupitant: palonosetron prevents
L2178: nausea and vomiting during the acute phase and pronetupitant prevents nausea and vomiting during both the acute and delayed phase after cancer chemotherapy.”
L2179: - PALO-15-17: a phase 3 noninferiority study of I.V. palonosetron 0.25 mg given as a 30 minute infusion versus FDA-approved I.V. palonosetron 0.25 mg given as a 30 second bolus, to support efficacy of the I.V. palonosetron 30 minute infusion in the I.V. FDC.
- NEPA-15-18: a phase 3 HEC clinical safety study to assess the safety of the I.V. pronetupitant/palonosetron (260 mg/0.25 mg) FDC infused over 30 minutes compared to approved oral Akynzeo (netupitant/palonosetron 300/0.5 mg) capsules for the prevention of HEC chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting in four repeated consecutive chemotherapy cycles. The intent is to assess the safety of the I.V. FDC which has a higher Cmax than Akynzeo capsules.
- PNET-13-63: a clinical drug-drug interaction study in healthy volunteers to assess the effect of I.V. pronetupitant infused over 30 minutes on the PK of oral dexamethasone.
- PNET-12-23: a phase 1, double-blind, single ascending dose study in healthy subjects to assess safety and define the maximum tolerated dose of I.V. pronetupitant. An FDA-requested BE analysis from this trial demonstrated that the 260 mg pronetupitant dose is BE for AUClast and AUCinf to oral netupitant 300 mg from the approved FDC capsule. Netupitant Cmax for I.V. pronetupitant 260 mg was greater and not bioequivalent to netupitant 300 mg from the approved oral FDC thereby requiring HEC safety study NEPA-15-18.
- NEPA-15-19: a phase 1, open label study to evaluate the PK profile and safety of the I.V. pronetupitant/palonosetron (260 mg/0.25 mg) FDC administered for the prevention of HEC in cancer patients receiving a single cycle of HEC chemotherapy. The intent is to obtain PK data in cancer patients to support labeling the planned I.V. FDC NDA.
L2180: “Yes, we agree the planned clinical development program appears adequate for NDA submission, as proposed. The adequacy of the submission for the indication proposed will be a review issue.”
L2181: ## *FDA Biostatistics Written Replies on 6/06/2016*
L2182: - Please provide justification for the assumptions used to calculate the sample size, including the response rates and the anticipated dropout rates.
- Since you intend to enroll 440 adult patients from approximately 88 sites worldwide for Study PALO-15-17, county/region effect should be taken into consideration for both studies. Moreover, based on the anticipated number of patients in the study, it is possible that there will only be about 5 patients, on average, from each site. To ensure the blinding of the study, you should use centralized randomization.
- To assess the impact of the dropouts on the final analysis, the statistical analysis plan (SAP) should include several missing data strategies for the primary endpoint. For example, one sensitivity analysis is to impute dropouts as responders or non-responders. In addition, you should consider other approaches that can handle data missing at random (MAR) such as the MI (i.e., multiple imputations). Furthermore, since the missing data can be informative [i.e., missing not at random (MNAR)], you should also consider approaches such as pattern mixture models or selection models.
- For the proposed non-inferiority (NI) study, we expect that the NI analysis based on the per-protocol (PP) needs to be consistent with the analysis based on the FAS population. Any analysis of the secondary endpoints (i.e., ‘Key’ secondary endpoint) for which the
L2183: results are intended to be described in the labeling need to be pre-specified and agreed upon with the review division. In addition, you need to propose a valid multiplicity procedure in order to control the overall type I error for the primary and key secondary endpoints.
L2184: 5. Please submit your revised phase 3 protocols and SAP for review prior to starting your studies. Note that key features of your statistical plan, including primary and secondary endpoints and analyses, type I error control, missing data handling strategy, etc., should be pre-specified in your protocol and not introduced in the SAP after start of study.”
L2185: ## Pre NDA meeting on 11/30/2016
L2186: For Study PALO-15-17, it is a review issue to determine whether efficacy data from the study is adequate to support the submission of the planned NDA for the target indication. In addition, in order to assess the validity of efficacy analysis presented in Table 6 at page 22 of the meeting Briefing Package, please submit the following information when you submit the clinical study report for this NDA:
L2187: - Published articles with formula for Wilson score confidence interval method which you used to generate confidence intervals for the non-inferiority analysis;
- Publish articles for CMH stratum-adjusted methods proposed by Koch et al. and O'Gorman et al. which you used to test proportion difference in the non-inferiority analysis;
- Please conduct the secondary endpoint analyses based upon primary endpoint non-inferiority method using two-sided 99% confidence intervals.
L2188: For Study NEPA-15-18, as the briefing package indicated, since efficacy assessment was a secondary study objective, only descriptive statistics were planned and performed. We would like to remind you that the descriptive analysis results for the efficacy endpoints cannot be claimed in the labeling.

L2191: ## 3 STATISTICAL EVALUATION
L2192: ## 3.1 DATA and ANALYSIS QUALITY
L2193: The statistical reviewer confirmed the applicant's analysis results for the pivotal study. Overall, the data and analysis quality of this NDA submission are acceptable.
L2194: ## 3.2 EVALUATION of EFFICACY
L2195: The following sections contain the study description, where mostly was extracted directly from the applicant's Clinical Study Report (CSR). If there is any major discrepancy between the CSR and the study protocol or amendments, the detailed discussion will be included in the Section of Reviewer's Findings and Conclusions. The two studies, PALO-15-17 and NEPA-15-18 had
L2196: different designs and statistical methods; the former was a non-inferiority efficacy study and the later was a safety study with secondary efficacy assessments (descriptive statistics only).
L2197: ## 3.2.1 Study PALO-15-17
L2198: Study PALO-15-17 is titled “A phase 3, single-dose, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, parallel group study to assess the efficacy and safety of palonosetron 0.25 mg administered as a 30-minute IV infusion compared to palonosetron 0.25 mg administered as a 30-second IV bolus for the prevention of chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting in cancer patients receiving highly emetogenic chemotherapy.” It was conducted from September 30 2015 through March 09 2016 in 76 study centers in 9 countries (Belarus, Bosnia and Herzegovina, Bulgaria, Georgia, Greece, Hungary, Lithuania, Romania, and Russia).
L2199: ## 3.2.1.1 Study Objective and Study Design
L2200: This was a phase 3, single-dose, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, parallel group study to assess the efficacy and safety of palonosetron 0.25 mg administered as a 30-minute IV infusion compared to palonosetron 0.25 mg administered as a 30-second IV bolus for the prevention of chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting in cancer patients receiving highly emetogenic chemotherapy. A total of 440 patients, stratified by country and gender, are to be randomized to receive one of the two treatment regimens in a 1:1 randomization ratio according to specific procedures using the interactive web response system (IWRS). Before the start of the reference HEC administration on study Day 1, patients will be randomly allocated to receive one of the two treatment regimens for prevention of chemotherapy induced nausea and vomiting:
L2201: - Test group - IV PALO 0.25 mg administered as a 30-minute infusion on Day 1.
- Control group - IV PALO 0.25 mg administered as a 30-second bolus on Day 1.
Oral
L2202: Oral dexamethasone administration is open-label, 20 mg (5 tablets of 4 mg) given as single dose 30 minutes before the start of the reference HEC at Day 1 and 8 mg bid (2 tablets of 4 mg bid) on Days 2 to 4. Identical dosages and posology will be applied in both treatment groups. The blinding of the study medication will be guaranteed by the use of placebo of the alternative treatments (double-dummy technique). A placebo matching the IV PALO 0.25mg administered as 30-minute infusion and a placebo matching the IV PALO 0.25 mg administered as a 30-second bolus will be prepared and administered.
L2203: The primary objective of Study PALO-15-17 was to demonstrate the non-inferiority of a single intravenous (IV) dose of palonosetron 0.25 mg administered as a 30-minute (min) infusion with oral dexamethasone versus a single IV dose of palonosetron 0.25 mg administered as a 30-second (sec) bolus with oral dexamethasone, in terms of proportion of patients with complete response (CR), defined as no emetic episodes and no rescue medication, in the acute phase (0-24 h after start of reference highly emetogenic chemotherapy [HEC]).
L2204: The target population consisted of chemotherapy naïve patients. A naïve patient is defined as a patient having no history of cytotoxic chemotherapy when entering the present study. Adult
L2205: chemotherapy naïve male and female patients with a diagnosis of malignant solid tumor scheduled to receive their first course of HEC (cisplatin administered as a single IV dose of \ge 70 \text{ mg/m}^2, cyclophosphamide \ge 1500 \text{ mg/m}^2, carmustine (BCNU) &gt; 250 \text{ mg/m}^2, dacarbazine (DTIC), mechloretamine (nitrogen mustard)) are to be enrolled in this study.
L2206: ## 3.2.1.2 Efficacy Endpoints and Analyses
L2207: Three phases are defined for the efficacy evaluation. The acute phase starts at 'time 0', which is the start of the reference HEC administration and lasts for the first 24 hours. The delayed phase starts at &gt; 24 hours after the start of reference HEC administration and lasts until 120 hours. The overall phase lasts from time 0 till 120 hours after the start of HEC administration.
L2208: Primary efficacy endpoint is the proportion of patients with CR, defined as no emetic episodes and no rescue medication, in the acute phase of CINV.
L2209: Secondary efficacy endpoints are:
L2210: - the proportion of patients with CR during the delayed and overall phases of CINV;
- the proportion of patients with no emetic episodes during the acute, delayed, and overall phases of CINV;
- the proportion of patients with no rescue medication during the acute, delayed, and overall phases of CINV.
L2211: ## Blinding
L2212: The blinding of the study medication will be guaranteed by the use of identical placebos to the respective active drugs (double-dummy technique). When the 30-minute IV palonosetron 0.25 \text{ mg} infusion will be administered, a 30-second IV placebo bolus will be also administered (Group 1). Oppositely, when the 30-second IV palonosetron 0.25 \text{ mg} bolus, a 30-minute placebo infusion will be also administered (Group 2). Therefore, each patient will receive a 30-second IV bolus and a 30-minute IV infusion. No blinding is required for dexamethasone, which is administered at the same dose and posology in both treatment groups.
L2213: ## Analysis populations
L2214: There are three analysis populations:
L2215: - All Randomized Patients
L2216: This population includes all patients who have been randomized to treatment. Patients will be assigned to the study treatment group according to the treatment to which they have been randomized. Tables displaying patients' disposition will be based on all patients randomized.
L2217: - Full Analysis Set
L2218: The Full Analysis Set (FAS) includes all patients who have been randomized to treatment and received HEC regimen and active study drug (including partial infusion). Following the intent-to-treat principle, patients will be assigned to the study treatment group according to the treatment to which they have been randomized.
L2219: FAS will be used for demography, other baseline characteristics, for primary efficacy analysis and all efficacy analyses.
L2220: - Per Protocol (PP) population
L2221: The Per-Protocol Population includes all patients from the FAS, who have completed the 0-24 hours study period and with no major protocol violations i.e. those affecting the primary efficacy endpoint. All the protocol violations (e.g., wrong inclusion, poor compliance, missing diaries, forbidden concomitant medications, mis-randomizations) will be reviewed and discussed case by case during the BDRM and decisions described in the blind data review document/minutes which will be finalized prior to database lock. Patients will be assigned to study treatment groups according to the actual treatment received.
L2222: PP Population will be used for demography, other baseline characteristics and for the primary efficacy analysis.
L2223: ## Rationale for the choice of non-inferiority margin
L2224: Palonosetron 0.25 mg given intravenously as a 30-second bolus is the active control drug proposed for this trial since it is approved for CINV in HEC setting in the acute phase. The efficacy of this therapy has been clearly established and quantified in study PALO-99-05 (conducted worldwide enrolling overall 680 patients) that served as the pivotal efficacy trial to support approval of IV palonosetron 0.25 mg bolus. In this study, the proportion of patients with CR in the acute phase in the IV palonosetron 0.25 mg group was 59.2% (95% CI: 52.4% to 65.6%).
L2225: The meta-analysis database was comprised of 78 treatment arms from published trials and included data from 7,274 patients (the above mentioned studies using placebo were part of the database as well). Using this database, a logistic regression was performed to identify which covariates were predictive of CR. Using the relevant covariates from the meta-analysis, an equation was produced to determine the predicted CR rate for any given patient population (i.e., any given combination of relevant covariates). Relevant covariates from PALO-99-05 were entered into this equation (model) to produce the predicted historical placebo CR in the acute phase for the patient population of that study.
L2226: PALO-99-05: CR in the acute phase for IV palonosetron 0.25 mg (Intention-To-Treat population) and modeled historical placebo
L2227: IV palonosetron 0.25 mg
(N=223)	Modeled historical
placebo
Proportion of patients with
CR (95% CI)	59.2% (52.4% to 65.6%)	11.8% (8.7% to 15.8%)
L2228: The entire effect of the active control drug assumed to be present in this non-inferiority trial (M1) is estimated by the difference between the lower limit of the 95% CI for the IV palonosetron 0.25 mg (i.e., 52.4%) and the upper limit of the 95% CI for the modeled historical placebo (i.e., 15.8%). As a consequence, the non-inferiority margin for the current study should not exceed 36.6% (M1).
L2229: Based also on clinical judgment, it is considered that a substantial portion of M1 (at least 50%) should be preserved, leading to a value of about 18%. In order to be as conservative as possible, a further additional adjustment is applied to reflect uncertainties and the non-inferiority margin is
L2230: reduced to 15% (M2, the largest clinically acceptable difference of the test drug compared to the control drug). The proposed non-inferiority margin of 15% is the same as that used for HEC pivotal efficacy trial PALO-99-05. A 15% non-inferiority margin was also considered adequate for pivotal CINV studies for other 5-HT3 antagonists. Studies which formed the basis for approval of dolasetron used a non-inferiority margin of 15% versus active control drug treatments (study MCPRO031 versus ondansetron and study 73147-3-S-093 versus granisetron).
L2231: ## Efficacy analysis
L2232: Since the only difference between test and control groups is the duration of infusion (30 minutes versus 30 seconds), it is expected that, a single dose of IV PALO 0.25 mg administered as a 30-minute infusion will not decrease the percentage of patients with CR in the acute phase (0-24h after the start of chemotherapy).
L2233: The null hypothesis can be written as: H_0: \pi_T - \pi_C \le -\Delta
L2234: H_0 will be tested against the alternative hypothesis: H_1: \pi_T - \pi_C &gt; -\Delta
L2235: Where \pi_T is the proportion of patients with CR in the test group (PALO IV 30-minute infusion), \pi_C is the proportion of patients with CR in the control group (PALO IV 30-second bolus) and \Delta is the non-inferiority margin which is fixed at 15%.
L2236: All efficacy analyses will be performed on the FAS. The primary efficacy endpoint, i.e. CR in the acute phase will also be summarized on PP Population. The primary efficacy analysis will be performed on both the FAS and the PP Populations. All missing data will be considered as failures. For the non-inferiority of IV 30-minute palonosetron 0.25 mg infusion vs. I.V. 30-second palonosetron 0.25 mg bolus, both populations will have equal importance and the analyses will conclude on the basis of the coherence between the results obtained in both populations. To test the null hypothesis, the lower limit of the two-sided 99% CI for the difference in proportion of patients with CR in the acute phase between palonosetron IV 30-minute infusion and palonosetron IV 30-second bolus will be compared to the pre-set non-inferiority margin set at -15%.
L2237: Calculations will be based on stratum-adjusted Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) for the risk difference, 99% CI for the risk difference will be calculated. The model will include gender and country as strata. P-value for non-inferiority testing will be computed.
L2238: To calculate confidence intervals for CMH statistics the below quantities are needed. The calculations and the following notation are listed below.
L2239: Let K be the number of strata.
L2240: For the kth stratum we denote with:
L2241: n_k the number of patients in the test group
L2242: m_k the number of patients in the control group
L2243: x_k the number of patients with CR in the acute phase in the test group
L2244: y_k the number of patients with CR in the acute phase in the control group
L2245: N_k = n_k + m_k the total number of patients.
L2246: With these quantities the CMH estimator for risk difference is given by:
L2247: d_{CMH} = \frac{\sum W_{CMH,k} d_k}{\sum W_{CMH,k}}
L2248: Where
L2249: W_{CMH,k} = n_k m_k / N_k
L2250: and
L2251: d_k = \frac{x_k}{n_k} - \frac{y_k}{m_k}
L2252: The variance of d_{CMH} is \frac{\sum L_k}{(\sum W_{CMH,k})^2} with
L2253: L_k = \left[ x_k(n_k - x_k)m_k^3 - y_k(m_k - y_k)n_k^3 \right] / (n_k m_k N_k^2)
L2254: Finally the 100(1-\alpha)\% level confidence interval for the risk difference is
L2255: d_{CMH} \pm z_{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}} (\sum L_k)^{1/2} / \sum W_{CMH,k}
L2256: where z_{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}} is the 1 - \frac{\alpha}{2} quantile of standard normal distribution.
L2257: ## Multiplicity
L2258: No adjustment for multiplicity is foreseen in the study since there is only one single primary efficacy endpoint, which is the CR in the acute phase. All secondary efficacy endpoints are intended to provide supportive evidence related to the primary objective or additional clinical characterization of treatment effect, the results on these will be interpreted in only a descriptive manner.
L2259: ## Sample Size
L2260: The sample size and power estimation were based upon CR (complete response) in the acute phase (0-24h after the start of chemotherapy), the primary efficacy endpoint of the study. CR rate in the acute phase was estimated to be 80%. The non-inferiority margin is set at -15%. For a two-sided test of difference using Type I error equal to 0.01, a sample size of 212 evaluable patients per group is needed to ensure 90% power (nQuery + nTerim 3.0, module PTEO-1). The number of patients was increased up to 220 per group for a total of 440 to ensure an adequate number of evaluable patients.
L2261: Subgroups that will be explored include:
L2262: - Gender (Male, Female)
- Country (Belarus, Bosnia and Herzegovina, Bulgaria, Georgia, Greece, Hungary, Lithuania, Romania, Russia)
L2263: ## Interaction Analysis:
L2264: Treatment by factor (gender and country) interactions will be explored but not included in the primary analysis model. In case significant interactions (i.e. p\text{-value} \le 0.100) are found, additional investigational analyses may be run in order to understand the reason for the heterogeneity. As mentioned in the protocol, as exploratory analysis, the following full model will be fitted with logistic regression using the available data to investigate the interactions between treatment (Trt) and factors (Gender or Country)
L2265: Interim Analysis: Not applicable
L2266: ## Missing Values and Imputation Methods
L2267: Due to the short observation period (24 hours for the primary efficacy endpoint and a maximum of 120 hours for all the other efficacy assessments) and based on a previous the applicant's studies with a very similar design and in the same setting (HEC), a very low number of drop-outs and minimal missing data is expected. Minimizing the withdrawal and missing data is particularly important in the context of a non-inferiority trial. The procedure for handling missing data will be applied to the FAS and PP Population.
L2268: Any patients who do not provide data about occurrence of emetic episodes and rescue medication intake or do not provide a full set of these data throughout the 24 hours after HEC administration will be considered as treatment failures (i.e., non-responders) for the primary efficacy analysis. For consistency, the same approach for handling missing data as described for the primary efficacy analysis will be also applied to emetic episodes and rescue medication intake. The same approach described above for the primary efficacy analysis will be applied also to CR in the delayed and overall phases.
L2269: ## **3.2.1.3 Patient Disposition, Demographic and Baseline Characteristics**
L2270: A total of 441 patients were randomized, and 440 (99.8%) received study treatment. The number of patients randomized into each treatment group, patient disposition, and the reasons for early withdrawal from the study are summarized in Table 2, at 88 sites in Russian Federation (30.4%), Hungary (22.2%), Romania (20.6%), and the Republic of Georgia (9.5%). Table 2 displays the number of patients randomized into each treatment group, patient disposition and the reasons for early discontinuation.
L2271: Patient retention was excellent; 96.1% of the randomized patients and 96.4% of the treated patients completed the study. Of the 17 (3.9%) patients who withdrew from the study prematurely, 13 (2.9%) patients died while on study (6 [2.7%] patients in the infusion group, and 7 [3.2%] in the bolus group); 2 (0.5%) patients were lost to follow-up (one patient in each treatment group); and 2 (0.5%) patients chose to withdraw (both patients in the bolus group).
