L2286: ## Secondary efficacy analyses
L2287: Results of secondary efficacy endpoints supported well the conclusion of non-inferiority of the 30-min IV infusion versus the 30-sec IV bolus. These secondary endpoints included CR in the delayed and overall phases; the proportion of patients with no emetic episodes in the acute, delayed, and overall phases; and the proportion of patients with no use of rescue medication in the acute, delayed, and overall phases.
L2288: ## 3.2.2 Statistical Reviewer's Findings and Comments
L2289: - **(Efficacy Findings for Study PALO-15-17)** The statistical reviewer confirmed the applicant's analysis results for the primary endpoint. We concluded that the study data support non-inferiority of palonosetron 0.25 mg administered as a 30-min IV infusion compared with administration as a 30-sec IV bolus, in terms of the proportion of patients reporting CR (defined as no emetic episodes and no rescue medication intake) in the acute phase.
L2290: 2. **(Efficacy Results for Study NEPA-15-18)** The rate of complete response (CR) in the delayed phase for IV NEPA FDC arm was observed to be 9% more lower than that for the concurrent oral Akynzeo arm in the safety Study NEPA-15-18. In addition, the observed CR rate in the delayed phase was 12% more lower than that for oral Akynzeo in Study NETU-07-07. Whether the data supported the efficacy of delayed phase for IV fosnetupitant-palonosetron is deferred to the clinical pharmacology reviewer's evaluation.
L2291: The statistical reviewer found that the study was not powered to demonstrate the efficacy of IV NEPA FDC in delayed phase by comparing with oral NEPA FDC. As shown on the Tables 8 and 9 in the appendix, we noted that when NI margin for NETU 300mg is very small (1%), the sample size will be huge. The size of NEPA-15-18 study can only provide 4% power. Note that for Study NEPA-15-18, as the briefing package indicated, since efficacy assessment was a secondary study objective, only descriptive statistics were planned and performed. The descriptive analysis results for the efficacy endpoints cannot be claimed in the labeling.
L2292: 3. **(Efficacy Results for Study NETU-07-07)** In order to address previous issues (#2 above), the efficacy of the oral 100 mg netupitant/0.5 mg palonestron group by comparing with 0.5 mg palonestron group in delayed phase was assessed. According to the FDA's statistical review for the oral Akynzeo, it was noted that Study NETU-07-07 had the needed information; 100 mg netupitant/0.5 mg palonestron is indeed superior to 0.5 mg palonestron group in delayed phase based on the CR rate difference (see Appendix Table 9).
L2293: ## 3.3 Evaluation of Safety
L2294: The safety evaluation is not conducted in this review.

L2311: ## 5 SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L2312: ## 5.1. Statistical Issues and Collective Evidence
L2313: The applicant submitted one phase 3 efficacy study with IV palonosetron in cancer patients (PALO-15-17) and one phase 3 safety study with fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC (NEPA-15-18) to support the use of IV fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC in preventing acute nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy.
L2314: In addition to these two phase 3 studies, the applicant also conducted three phase 1 studies (two PK and safety studies in healthy volunteers, PNET-12-23 and PNET-13-63, with fosnetupitant administration, one PK and safety study in cancer patients, NEPA-15-19, with fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC administration). Based on the FDA's advice after negotiation, the ultimate requirements for the IV NEPA FDC clinical development program should include a bioequivalence trial (PNET-12-23), a non-inferiority efficacy trial on the palonosetron component (PALO-15-17), a clinical safety trial (NEPA-15-18) and two clinical pharmacology trials (i.e., PNET-13-63, drug-drug interaction in healthy volunteers; NEPA-15-19, PK in cancer patients).
L2315: Regarding the efficacy study with IV palonosetron, although data support the use of IV fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC in preventing acute nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy (HEC); we noted that the observed CR rate (delayed phase) for IV NEPA FDC arm was 9% lower than the CR rate in the delayed phase for the concurrent oral Akynzeo arm in Study NEPA-15-18, and was 12% lower than the CR in the delayed phase for oral Akynzeo in Study NETU-07-07. Whether the data supported the efficacy of delayed phase for IV fosnetupitant-palonosetron is deferred to the clinical pharmacology reviewer's evaluation.
L2316: ## 5.2. Conclusions and Recommendations
L2317: Study PALO-15-17 is positive and support the indication of preventing acute nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy (HEC).
L2318: The statistical reviewer had carefully evaluated this application and successfully confirmed the applicant's efficacy findings in Study PALO-15-17. Even though the applicant had successfully demonstrated the efficacy of IV palonosetron component, efficacy in preventing delayed nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of HEC has not yet been demonstrated; the rate of complete response (CR) in the delayed phase for IV NEPA FDC arm was observed to be 9% more lower than that for the concurrent oral Akynzeo arm in the safety Study NEPA-15-18. In addition, the observed CR rate in the delayed phase was 12% more lower than that for oral Akynzeo in Study NETU-07-07. Whether the data supported the efficacy of delayed phase for IV fosnetupitant-palonosetron is deferred to the clinical pharmacology reviewer's evaluation.
L2319: In conclusion, data of Study PALO-15-17 indeed supported the efficacy of IV Akynzeo in preventing the nausea and vomiting associated with initial and repeat courses of highly emetogenic cancer chemotherapy (HEC) only for the acute phase.

L2338: ## 1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
L2339: The oral Akynzeo capsule, a fixed-dose combination (FDC) product containing 300 mg netupitant and 0.5 mg palonosetron, has been approved since 2014 for acute and delayed chemotherapy induced nausea and vomiting (CINV) associated with i) highly emetogenic chemotherapy (HEC) including cisplatin based chemotherapy and ii) anthracyclines and cyclophosphamide based chemotherapy and chemotherapy not considered highly emetogenic.
L2340: In this submission, the sponsor proposes a new FDC (Akynzeo, 235 mg fosnetupitant/0.25 mg palonosetron) injectable product for intravenous infusion over 30 minutes for acute and delayed CINV associated with HEC. While fosnetupitant itself does not have pharmacological activities, its hydrolytic metabolite netupitant, an NK-1 receptor antagonist, is the active drug. Netupitant is not registered as a single agent. Palonosetron (5-HT3 receptor antagonist) injectable (Aloxi injectable 0.25 mg) is approved for the prevention of acute nausea and vomiting after HEC and acute and delayed nausea and vomiting after moderately emetogenic chemotherapy (MEC). Oral palonosetron (0.5 mg) had been approved for acute nausea and vomiting associated with MEC only. The oral palonosetron product has been discontinued not due to efficacy or safety.
L2341: The sponsor conducted three clinical trials to support the efficacy and safety of Akynzeo IV: a relative BA study between IV fosnetupitant and Akynzeo capsule, a non-inferiority efficacy trial between IV palonosetron infused over 30 min and Aloxi (palonosetron 0.25 mg) injection, and a safety study between Akynzeo injectable and Akynzeo oral capsules. The sponsor proposes to support the efficacy of fosnetupitant as a pro-drug following Akynzeo IV administration by relying on the Agency's findings on the efficacy of netupitant from the approved Akynzeo oral capsule (NDA 205718) owned by the same sponsor. A relative BA study was conducted to compare systemic exposures (Cmax and AUC) of netupitant following single dose of 235 mg fosnetupitant IV and Akynzeo oral capsule. The results showed that the systemic exposure (AUC0-120h) to netupitant was comparable. A phase 3 safety study was conducted to provide safety information for fosnetupitant following Akynzeo IV infusion because i) there is a 1.5-fold increase in Cmax of netupitant and ii) fosnetupitant, a new molecular entity, had not been used in humans. This phase 3 safety study also provided safety data for administering fosnetupitant/palonosetron as an FDC product in patients.
L2342: For the palonosetron component of the product, the sponsor also relied on the Agency's findings on the safety of palonosetron IV (Aloxi; NDA 21372) owned by the same sponsor. Because Akynzeo IV is administered over 30 minutes while Aloxi is administered via IV push over 30 seconds, the Cmax of Aloxi will be higher than that from Akynzeo IV while AUC will be comparable because the dose is the same. Therefore, safety of the 30-minute infusion of palonosetron is at least comparable if not better than Aloxi administered by IV push. On the other hand, the sponsor conducted a pivotal phase 3 study to evaluate the potential difference in efficacy due to the lower Cmax and demonstrated that the efficacy of palonosetron IV administered over 30 minutes is not inferior to Aloxi injectable.
L2343: The effect of fosnetupitant IV on the systemic exposures to oral dexamethasone was evaluated and found to be comparable to the effect of Akynzeo oral capsules on dexamethasone both in terms of magnitude and duration. This supports the same reduction in the dosage of dexamethasone as that when given with Akynzeo oral capsules.
L2344: ## Recommendations
L2345: The Division of Clinical Pharmacology 3 has reviewed this application and found this NDA acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective.
L2346: The Division of New Drug Bioequivalence Evaluation (DNDBE) within the Office of Study Integrity and Surveillance (OSIS) recommends accepting data from the pivotal BA/BE study (PNET-12-23) without on-site inspection as OSIS recently inspected the study sites with the inspectional outcome from the inspections classified as No Action Indicated (NAI).
L2347: ## Post-Marketing Requirements and Commitments
L2348: The following PMCs are recommended. The potential interaction between fosnetupitant, a new molecular entity, and the transporters has not been fully characterized. The in vitro study showed that palonosetron is an inhibitor of MATE1 and MATE2-K. Thus, the sponsor needs to address the in vivo drug interaction potential via these two transporters.
L2349: - In vitro assessment of inhibition potential of fosnetupitant towards MATE2-K
- In vitro assessment of fosnetupitant being a substrate of P-gp/MDR1, BCRP, BSEP, MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OCT1, and OCT2.
L2350: ## 2. SUMMARY OF CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY ASSESSMENT
L2351: ## 2.1 Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L2352: Netupitant is a substance P/neurokinin 1 (NK1) receptor antagonist. Fosnetupitant, a pro-drug, is hydrolyzed to netupitant following the IV infusion. Palonosetron is a 5-HT3 receptor antagonist. Chemotherapeutic agents exert their emetic stimulus via processes that involve the release of serotonin and substance P and subsequent activation of the 5-HT3 and NK1 receptors. The release of substance P is considered to be associated with delayed emesis induced by chemotherapy while serotonin is considered to be associated with acute emesis induced by chemotherapy.
L2353: Following a single intravenous dose administration of 235 mg fosnetupitant (free base) to healthy subjects, the median time to Cmax of fosnetupitant was at the end of infusion (i.e., 0.5 hour) and mean Cmax was 6431 ng/mL (%CV:14%). The mean terminal half-life was about one hour. The median time to Cmax of netupitant was 0.6 hours and the mean Cmax was 812 ng/mL (%CV: 21%). The mean terminal half-life of netupitant following IV infusion of fosnetupitant in healthy subjects was not well estimated due to short sampling time (120 hours) in this NDA.
L2354: Following a single intravenous dose administration of the proposed product Akynzeo to cancer patients, the median time to Cmax of fosnetupitant was at the end of infusion (i.e., 0.5 hour) and mean Cmax was 3478 ng/mL (%CV: 45%). The mean terminal half-life was about 0.75 hours. The median time to Cmax of netupitant was 0.56 hours and the mean Cmax was 590 ng/mL (%CV: 28%). The mean terminal half-life of netupitant was 144 hours with sampling time up to 192 hours. The median time to Cmax of palonosetron was also close to the end of infusion (i.e., 0.58 hour) and mean Cmax was 823 ng/mL (%CV: 35%). The mean terminal half-life was about 58 hours.
L2355: ## 2.1.1. Comparison of systemic exposures (Cmax and AUC) comparison to netupitant and its major metabolites following 235 mg fosnetupitant IV and Akynzeo capsule
L2356: A relative BA study between single dose of 235 mg fosnetupitant injectable intravenously infused over 30 minutes and single dose of oral Akynzeo capsule (300 mg netupitant/0.5 mg palonosetron) showed that the systemic exposure (AUC0-120h) of netupitant met the bioequivalence criteria. The Cmax of netupitant from fosnetupitant IV administration was 1.5-fold that of oral Akynzeo.
L2357: The AUC0-120h of major metabolites, M1, M2, and M3 after IV administration of fosnetupitant were 29%, 25%, and 16% lower than those from Akynzeo oral administration.
L2358: The Cmax of the major metabolites M1, M2, and M3 after IV administration of fosnetupitant were 39%, 34%, and 25% lower than those from Akynzeo oral administration.
L2359: ## 2.2 Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L2360: ## 2.2.1 General dosing
L2361: The proposed dosing regimen with Akynzeo IV is shown below. The proposed dose for fosnetupitant is presented as fosnetupitant free base. The dose of 235 mg fosnetupitant as free base is equivalent to 260 mg fosnetupitant chloride hydrochloride. The rationale of using reduced dexamethasone dose is discussed in Section 3.3.4 of the review.

In addition, the sponsor had used pro-netupitant (or pronetupitant) early on during its drug development. Hereafter, fosnetupitant and pro-netupitant are used interchangeably in the review document.
L2366: ## 2.2.2 *Therapeutic individualization*
L2367: Similar to the approved oral Akynzeo, avoid use of Akynzeo IV in patients with severe hepatic impairment, severe renal impairment or end-stage renal disease.

L2374: ## 3. COMPREHENSIVE CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY REVIEW
L2375: ## 3.1 Overview of the Product and Regulatory Background
L2376: ## Proposed product
L2377: AKYNZEO (235 mg fosnetupitant (free base)/0.25 mg palonosetron) for injection is a (b) (4) combination product of fosnetupitant, a prodrug of netupitant which is a substance P/neurokinin 1 (NK1) receptor antagonist, and palonosetron hydrochloride, a serotonin-3 (5-HT3) receptor antagonist.
L2378: AKYNZEO is supplied as a sterile, lyophilized powder for injection in single-use vials of 235 mg fosnetupitant (free base)/0.25 mg palonosetron. The inactive ingredients are edetate disodium (6.4 mg), mannitol (760 mg), sodium hydroxide and/or hydrochloric acid (for pH adjustment).
L2379: Each vial must be reconstituted in 50 mL of 5% Dextrose Injection, USP or 0.9% Sodium Chloride Injection, USP.
L2380: ## Clinical development program and relevant regulatory background
L2381: The clinical program consisted of five studies including three phase 1 clinical pharmacology studies. No PK data were collected from the two phase 3 studies.
L2382: - Three phase 1 trials (two PK and safety studies in healthy volunteers, PNET-12-23 and PNET-13-63, with fosnetupitant administration, one PK and safety study in cancer patients, NEPA-15-19, with fosnetupitant-palonosetron fixed dose combination (FDC) administration);
- One phase 3 efficacy study with IV palonosetron in cancer patients (PALO-15-17): palonosetron IV push (30 seconds infusion) using Aloxi vs. palonosetron 30-minute IV infusion;
- One phase 3 safety study with fosnetupitant-palonosetron FDC (NEPA-15-18) using oral Akynzeo as a comparator.
L2383: The objectives and designs of these clinical studies are shown below.

L2387: F=female; FDC=fixed dose combination; HEC= highly emetogenic chemotherapy; IV=intravenous; M=males; MEC=moderately emetogenic chemotherapy; PD=pharmacodynamic; PK=pharmacokinetic; PO=oral.
L2388: No pivotal phase 3 clinical trial with efficacy as a primary endpoint was conducted. Fosnetupitant is a prodrug of netupitant. The sponsor is relying on the Agency's findings on netupitant from the approved Akynzeo oral capsule (NDA 205718), a fixed-dose combination product containing netupitant and palonosetron, owned by the same sponsor. Netupitant is not registered as a single agent. A relative BA study was conducted to show systemic exposures (Cmax and AUC) of netupitant following single dose of 260 mg fosnetupitant IV and Akynzeo oral capsule. The relative BA study results were summarized in Section 2.1. A phase 3 safety study was conducted to provide safety information on fosnetupitant following Akynzeo IV infusion (Study NEPA-15-18).
L2389: Since Akynzeo IV is an FDC product containing palonosetron, the sponsor also relied on the Agency's findings on palonosetron IV (Aloxi; NDA 21372). Aloxi is also owned by the same sponsor. Because Akynzeo IV is administered over 30 minutes while Aloxi is administered via IV push, the Cmax of Aloxi will be higher than that from Akynzeo IV. Therefore, safety is not an issue. The sponsor conducted a pivotal phase 3 study demonstrating that the efficacy (acute phase CINV associated with HEC) contribution of palonosetron from Akynzeo IV is not inferior to Aloxi (Study PALO-15-17).
L2390: ## 3.2 General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L2391: The mechanism of action, absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion information of netupitant can be found in the product label of approved Akynzeo oral capsule.
L2392: The mechanism of action, absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion information of palonosetron can be found in the product label of approved Akynzeo oral capsule and Aloxi IV.
L2393: Fosnetupitant is a new molecular entity (NME). Below is a brief summary of the PK characteristics and ADME of fosnetupitant.
L2394: ## Absorption
L2395: The mean (CV%) Cmax and AUC0-inf of fosnetupitant following single IV dose of 260 mg fosnetupitant in healthy subjects reached 6431 ng/mL (14%) and 2938 h\timesng/mL (12%), respectively.
L2396: The median [min to max] time to Cmax (Tmax) is 0.5 [0.25 -0.52] hour. The median Tmax is at the end of the 30-minute infusion.
L2397: Fosnetupitant exhibited dose-proportional linear pharmacokinetics over the dose ranging from 19.5 to 390 mg.
L2398: ## Distribution
L2399: The mean (%CV) apparent volume of distribution (Vd) following single IV dose of 260 mg fosnetupitant in healthy subjects was 124 L (61%).
L2400: The human plasma protein binding of fosnetupitant measured by equilibrium dialysis was 92.1% at 1 \muM and 94.9% at 10 \muM. The free fraction was in the range of 5 - 8%.
L2401: The mean blood to plasma concentration ratio in humans was in the range of 0.4 - 0.6 with fosnetupitant concentrations of 0.1, 1, and 10 \muM.
L2402: ## Metabolism
L2403: Fosnetupitant is hydrolyzed to netupitant by non-CYP450 enzymes. The proposed metabolic pathway is shown in Figure 1 in Section 3.3.4.
L2404: In vitro fosnetupitant is converted to netupitant in human hepatocytes and S9 preparations from human livers, kidney, lung, and intestine indicating that the conversion of fosnetupitant to netupitant occurs in liver and extrahepatic tissues.
L2405: Following a single intravenous dose administration of 260 mg fosnetupitant to healthy subjects, the median time to Cmax of fosnetupitant was at the end of infusion (i.e., 0.5 hour) while the median time to Cmax of netupitant was 0.6 hours indicating a rapid conversion of fosnetupitant to netupitant upon administration. The mean Cmax was 812 ng/mL (%CV: 21%). The mean terminal half-life of netupitant following IV infusion of fosnetupitant in healthy subjects was not well estimated due to short sampling time (120 hours) in this NDA.
L2406: Similarly, following a single intravenous dose administration of the proposed product Akynzeo to cancer patients, the median time to Cmax of fosnetupitant was at the end of infusion (i.e., 0.5 hour) while the median time to Cmax of netupitant was 0.56 hours indicating a rapid conversion of
L2407: fosnetupitant to netupitant. The mean Cmax was 590 ng/mL (%CV: 28%). The mean terminal half-life of netupitant was 144 hours with sampling time up to 192 hours. The median time to Cmax of palonosetron was also close to the end of infusion (i.e., 0.58 hour) and mean Cmax was 823 ng/mL (%CV: 35%). The mean terminal half-life was about 58 hours.
L2408: ## Elimination
L2409: After IV administration of fosnetupitant, its plasma concentrations declined biexponentially. The mean terminal t1/2 following single IV dose of 260 mg fosnetupitant in healthy subjects was about an hour. Except for the lower doses where many PK samples of the first 24 hours were below BLOQ, the mean t1/2 did not change with the increase in dose.
L2410: ## Cross-study comparison of systemic exposure to fosnetupitant and its metabolites in cancer patients and healthy subjects
L2411: The values for Cmax of fosnetupitant were about 53% to 58% lower in the cancer patient population. The values for AUC0-inf were also 56% to 58% lower in the cancer patient population.

L2415: The AUC_{0-120h} of netupitant in patients was 26% lower than that in the healthy subjects (Table 3). The AUC_{0-120h} of netupitant, M1, M2, and M3 are shown in the table below. The reason for choosing AUC_{0-120h} as a key PK parameter for comparison is provided in Section 3.3.1. As this is a cross-study comparison, the differences in systemic exposures to fosnetupitant, netupitant, and its metabolites may not have clinical significance.

L2423: ## 3.3 Clinical Pharmacology Review Questions
L2424: ## 3.3.1 *To what extent does the available clinical pharmacology information provide pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness?*
L2425: ## Adequacy of the bridging
L2426: The sponsor relied on the efficacy and safety findings of oral Akynzeo for CINV. The efficacy of Akynzeo IV formulation was supported by the results from the pivotal BA/BE study (PNET-12-23) in which BE criteria were met for AUC of netupitant between fosnetupitant IV and oral Akynzeo.
L2427: A relative BA study between the IV formulation of 260 mg fosnetupitant and approved oral Akynzeo capsules showed that the systemic exposures (AUC0-120h) to netupitant met bioequivalence criteria. The Agency focused on AUC0-120h instead of AUC0-inf for the following two reasons:
L2428: - The sampling time for netupitant was only up to 120 hours while its mean terminal half-life in patients is 80 hours. The reported AUC0-inf in the study could not be reliably estimated.
- For the indication of CINV, the ideal duration of a drug effect is 5 days (120 hours). Thus, comparison of AUC0-120h is clinically relevant.
L2429: The Cmax of netupitant from fosnetupitant 260 mg IV administration was 1.5-fold the oral Akynzeo capsules. In addition, the AUC0-120h of netupitant from fosnetupitant IV administration were comparable to those from oral Akynzeo administration in the pivotal BA/BE study. For details, see Section 4.2.1.
L2430: The AUC0-120h of metabolites M1, M2, and M3 from fosnetupitant IV were 29%, 25%, and 16% lower than that from oral Akynzeo, respectively. Similarly, the Cmax of metabolites M1, M2, and M3 from fosnetupitant IV administration were 39%, 34%, 25% lower than that from oral Akynzeo administration, respectively.
L2431: Whether metabolites M1, M2, and M3 carry functional pharmacological activity is not clear at the time of this review. All three bind to NK1 receptor with equal affinity based upon pKi values. Netupitant inhibited NK-1 receptor agonist-induced foot-tapping behavior in gerbils in a dose-dependent manner. The metabolites M1, M2, and M3, also blocked the foot tapping activity in this study. However, the relevance of the effect on foot-tapping activity to the pharmacodynamic activity for the proposed indication is unknown. There is no data in animals to evaluate whether the metabolites have anti-emetic activity unlike what was done for netupitant. Refer to Pharm/Tox review of Akynzeo oral capsules (NDA 205718). The potential therapeutic contribution of these metabolites could not be evaluated. Therefore, for the review of pivotal BA study (PNET-12-23), focus was made on the comparison of systemic exposures (Cmax and AUC) of netupitant.
L2432: The safety and tolerability profile of Akynzeo IV is supported by a phase 1 study in cancer patients (NEPA-15-19) and a phase 3 safety study in cancer patients (NEPA-15-18). Refer to Dr. Charles Line's Clinical Review for the safety of Akynzeo IV.
L2433: Although the final proposed product is a combination product of fosnetupitant and palonosetron while fosnetupitant as a single agent was used in the pivotal BA study, the presence of palonosetron does not affect the PK of fosnetupitant. This determination was made based upon the following facts:
L2434: - No chemical interaction has occurred between fosnetupitant and palonosetron following reconstitution from the lyophilized vial.
- The infusion time was fixed at 30 minutes. Thus, the difference in concentrations between fosnetupitant used in the pivotal BA study (2.6 mg/mL) and the final proposed product (5.2 mg/mL) is not an issue.
- Effect of palonosetron on fosnetupitant: Fosnetupitant is not metabolized by CYP450 enzymes. Fosnetupitant is a substrate of OATP transporters. However, palonosetron is not an inhibitor of OATP transporter.
- Effect of palonosetron on netupitant (Clinical Pharmacology Review of oral Akynzeo NDA 205718 and approved label of Akynzeo oral capsules):Netupitant and is mainly metabolized by CYP3A4. In vitro studies showed that palonosetron does not inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, CYP2D6, CYP2E1 and CYP3A4/5 or induce CYP1A2, CYP2D6 or CYP3A4/5 although CYP2C19 was not investigated.
- An in vivo study showed that oral dose of palonosetron 0.75 mg did not affect the systemic exposure to netupitant (450 mg PO). In addition, in vivo interaction with netupitant being a substrate for BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT1 is unlikely.
- The human plasma protein binding of fosnetupitant phosphate is 93.5% while the binding of palonosetron to human plasma protein is 62%. Neither drug is highly protein bound. Thus, no competition for plasma protein binding sites with potential displacement of bound drugs is expected.
L2435: Effect of fosnetupitant on palonosetron was minimal based upon the following facts:
L2436: - Palonosetron is metabolized by CYP2D6 and, to a minor extent, by CYP3A4 and CYP1A2. In vivo inhibition of CYP2D6, CYP3A and CYP1A2 by fosnetupitant is unlikely given their in vitro IC_{50} values being at least 20-fold the in vivo values of Cmax following 260 mg fosnetupitant IV (CYP2D6: IC_{50} = 217.2 \muM; CYP3A4: IC_{50} = 196.8 - 299.2 \muM; and CYP1A2: IC_{50} = 208.4 \muM). The R values (R = 1+[I]/Ki where [I]=Cmax=9.2 \mu M; Ki=IC_{50}/2) were all &lt; 1.1.
- Palonosetron was a MDR1/P-gp substrate based upon in vitro studies. However, in vivo drug interaction between a P-gp substrate and fosnetupitant in liver is unlikely based upon basic model estimation. P-gp is also located on the blood-brain barrier (BBB). Theoretically, fosnetupitant may increase the uptake of palonosetron by inhibiting P-gp on BBB. The safety of Akynzeo IV is evaluated in a phase 3 study and is acceptable.
- Interaction at plasma protein level would be minimal as indicated in Bullet 4 of the "Effect of palonosetron on fosnetupitant."
L2437: ## Evidence supporting effectiveness
L2438: It is noteworthy that for the delayed phase nausea and vomiting, the proportion of patients with Complete Response rate at Cycle 1 was 78.3% in the IV Akynzeo (fosnetupitant 260 mg/palonosetron 0.25 mg) group and 87.6% in the oral Akynzeo (netupitant 300 mg/palonosetron 0.5 mg) group (Study NEPA-15-18). The caveat is that efficacy assessment in this study was the secondary objective (i.e., no statistical power to detect difference). However, the dose-response relationship for efficacy and magnitude of systemic exposure achieved following Akynzeo IV provided sufficient evidence to support effectiveness.
L2439: The dose-response curve was flat in the dose range of 100, 200, and 300 mg oral netupitant in combination with 0.5 mg oral palonosetron (Clinical Pharmacology Review of NDA 205718 oral Akynzeo). In a dose-ranging phase 2 study (Study NETU-07-07, NDA 205718), both 100 mg and 300 mg oral netupitant in combination with 0.5 mg oral palonosetron showed statistical superiority in the primary endpoint of Complete Response Rate in the delayed phase compared to 0.5 mg oral palonosetron alone (Biometrics Review of NDA 205718 Akynzeo oral capsules). Even though systemic exposures to netupitant was not measured in this phase 2 study, the AUCs of netupitant, M1, M2, and M3 following a single oral dose of 100 mg netupitant in healthy subjects from a separate study were much lower than those from Akynzeo IV (Table 5). In addition, there is dose-proportional increase in systemic exposures (Cmax and AUC) to fosnetupitant with fosnetupitant IV dose increase from 19.5 mg to 390 mg and greater than dose-proportional increase in systemic exposure to netupitant with oral netupitant dose increase from 100 mg to 300 mg. Taken together, the totality of the data supports the effectiveness of Akynzeo IV for the proposed indication of
L2440: delayed phase CINV. The effectiveness of Akynzeo IV for acute phase CINV was supported by a pivotal phase 3 efficacy study. Refer to Dr. Charles Lines' Clinical Review of this NDA.

L2450: ## *3.3.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions and what is the appropriate management strategy?*
L2451: ## *Food-drug Interactions*
L2452: Since Akynzeo is administered by IV infusion, food-effect study was not conducted as food-drug interactions are not anticipated or applicable.
L2453: ## Drug-drug interactions
L2454: ## In vivo evaluation
L2455: ## Inhibition potential of dexamethasone (CYP3A4 substrate)
L2456: The effects of single dose fosnetupitant IV on dexamethasone, a CYP3A4 substrate was evaluated since Akynzeo is given in combination of with a corticosteroid, dexamethasone.
L2457: The results are summarized below. For details of the study results, refer to Section 4.2.3.

L2464: As fosnetupitant is converted to netupitant quickly and with similar magnitude of inhibition between oral netupitant and IV fosnetupitant, it is likely that the inhibition of CYP3A4 is caused by netupitant and metabolite M1, both previously shown as CYP3A4 inhibitors.
L2465: In a post-marketing commitment study conducted for oral Akyzneo NDA program, the sponsor has shown that the inhibition of CYP3A decreased on Day 8 and ended from Day 8 to Day 10. The current label for oral Akynzeo states, “A single oral dose of netupitant 300 mg significantly inhibits CYP3A4 for 6 days.” Given that the inhibition of CYP3A on Day 4 was similar between 300 mg oral netupitant and 260 mg IV fosnetupitant, it is reasonable to assume that fosnetupitant single dose will inhibit CYP3A for 6 days as well. It is also reasonable to assume that the inhibition of CYP3A by fosnetupitant will decrease on Day 8 and end from Day 8 to Day 10.
L2466: The effect of dexamethasone on the systemic exposures to fosnetupitant, netupitant and its metabolites was not evaluated. However, the systemic exposures to fosnetupitant and netupitant following co-administration of fosnetupitant 260 mg IV and oral dexamethasone (Study PNET-13-63) are comparable to those from Study PNET-12-23 where fosnetupitant 260 mg IV were administered in healthy subjects. See Table 3 and Table 4 in Section 3.2 for the descriptive statistics of Cmax and AUCs from these studies.
L2467: ## In vitro and in silico evaluation
L2468: ## Fosnetupitant metabolism
L2469: Fosnetupitant is hydrolyzed to netupitant by non-CYP450 enzymes. Therefore, it is unlikely to be affected by CYP450 inhibitors and inducers.
L2470: The proposed metabolism pathway is shown in Figure 1. The metabolism pathway of netupitant is reviewed in the original NDA 205718 for Akynzeo oral capsules.
L2471: Figure 1. Proposed metabolic Pathway of fosnetupitant in humans
L2472: Source data: Section 2.7.2 Summary of Clinical Pharmacology, Figure 22
L2473: Fosnetupitant is a substrate for both OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 uptake transporters. However, because fosnetupitant is quickly hydrolyzed to netupitant following the single dose administration, the effect of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 inhibitors upon systemic exposures to fosnetupitant will be small.
L2474: ## Fosnetupitant as a CYP450 inhibitor
L2475: In the range of concentrations tested (0.3-1000 \muM), fosnetupitant showed inhibition towards CYP2C9 (IC_{50} = 27.9 \muM), CYP2C19 (IC_{50} = 46.1 \muM), CYP2C8 (IC_{50} = 56.7 \muM), CYP2B6 (IC_{50} = 106.8 \muM), CYP1A2 (IC_{50} = 208.4 \muM), CYP2D6 (IC_{50} = 217.2 \muM) and CYP3A4 (IC_{50} = 196.8 \muM and 299.2 \muM with midazolam and testosterone substrates, respectively). The *in vitro* study was conducted using human liver microsomes.
L2476: ## *Time-dependent inhibition (TDI)*
L2477: In the concentration range from 0.3 to 300 \mu\text{M} with up to 30 minutes of pre-incubation time, fosnetupitant did not show TDI potential towards CYP3A4 and CYP2C9.
L2478: At the concentration of 1000 \mu\text{M}, fosnetupitant showed a significant TDI effect, with an activity loss of 63% for CYP3A4 and 98% for CYP2C9. The concentration of 1000 \mu\text{M} is about 100-fold the Cmax (9.2 \mu\text{M}) following 260 mg single IV dose.
L2479: ## In silico evaluation of in vivo inhibition potential of CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 enzymes
L2480: The PBPK modeling approach suggested no significant effects of fosnetupitant on the systemic exposure to S-warfarin (CYP2C9 substrate) as well as omeprazole (CYP2C19 substrate) at the recommended dose level (260 mg IV infusion for 30 min). Refer to Section 4.3 “Review of Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Modeling (PBPK): PNET-16-29”.
L2481: No significant in vivo inhibition of other CYP450 enzymes is expected because no significant in vivo inhibition potential of CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 is expected. Fosnetupitant has the lowest \text{IC}_{50} values towards CYP2C9 and CYP2C19.
L2482: ## Fosnetupitant as a transporter inhibitor
L2483: ## *Vesicular transporters*
L2484: Fosnetupitant is an inhibitor of the MDR1-mediated NMQ transport in vesicular assay with a maximum inhibition of 47 % at 50 \mu\text{M}.
L2485: Fosnetupitant did **not** inhibit the BSEP-mediated taurocholate and BCRP-mediated E3S accumulation in vesicular transport inhibition assays up to 20 and 50 \mu\text{M}, respectively.
L2486: ## *Hepatic uptake transporters*
L2487: Fosnetupitant is an inhibitor of the OATP1B1-mediated E217\betaG transport (\text{IC}_{50}: 19.01 \mu\text{M}) and the OATP1B3-mediated CCK-8 transport (\text{IC}_{50}: 17.37 \mu\text{M}).
L2488: ## *Renal uptake transporters*
L2489: Fosnetupitant is an inhibitor of the MATE1-mediated metformin transport (\text{IC}_{50}: 29.48 \mu\text{M}).
L2490: Fosnetupitant is an inhibitor of OCT1-mediated metformin transport, with a maximum inhibition of 44 % at 300 \mu\text{M}.
L2491: Fosnetupitant did **not** inhibit the OAT1-mediated tenofovir transport, the OAT3-mediated methotrexate transport and the OCT2-mediated metformin transport up to 300 \mu\text{M}.
L2492: ## In silico evaluation of in vivo inhibition potential of transporters
L2493: For OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 inhibition potential estimation, the calculated R values were slightly below the cutoff value of 1.11.
L2494: The DDI potential between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates in the GI tract is minimal because fosnetupitant is administered intravenously and eliminated quickly from the systemic circulation. The ratio of the estimated [I_{u,in,max}] and reported IC_{50} for P-gp was less than 0.1, where I_{u,in,max} is the estimated maximum unbound concentration of fosnetupitant at the inlet to the liver, suggesting no DDI potential between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates in the liver.

L2511: Carryover of pro-netupitant was observed for blank samples injected after the ULOQ sample for both detectors (API4000 and API5000). On the API5000 system the acceptance criteria for carry over were slightly exceeded, but since the API5000 system was not used for routine analysis, this was not investigated any further. It was concluded that for sample measurement, five blank samples need to be injected after a high concentrated sample and that study samples need to be sorted in an ascending order of pro-netupitant concentration.
L2512: No carryover was observed for the internal standard (IS) on both devices.

L2531: ## 4.2. Review of Individual Studies
L2532: ## 4.2.1. *Pivotal BA/BE Study: PNET-12-23*
L2533: **Title:** A Phase I, double-blind, controlled, parallel groups, unbalanced single ascending dose study to assess the safety of intravenously administered pro-netupitant combined with crossover study extensions to estimate the dose of intravenous pro-netupitant yielding equivalent drug exposure as oral netupitant 300 mg/palonosetron 0.5 mg fixed dose combination in healthy male and female volunteers.
L2534: **Study Design:** This is a randomized, single-dose, single-center, parallel group single ascending dose (SAD) part with a crossover extension (SAD-CO), a pilot crossover part (P-CO), and a final crossover part (F-CO) study (Figure 2). Single ascending dose administration on Day 1 of each period separated by a washout phase of at least 4 weeks. Crossover administrations in the same subject were separated by a washout phase of at least 4 weeks.
L2535: ## Figure 2. Schematic drawing of the study design
L2536: DSMB: Drug Safety Monitoring Board, F-CO: final crossover, IV: intravenous, N: number of subjects, P-CO: pilot crossover, PK: pharmacokinetics, PO: per os, SAD: single ascending dose, SAD-CO: single ascending dose crossover
L2537: Source data: Study PNET-12-3 CSR In-text Figure 9.1-1

L2581: ## Dose proportionality assessment

L2583: Figure 5. Dose-normalized Cmax and AUC of fosnetupitant vs Dose
L2584: Reviewer's comment: Overall, systemic exposures (Cmax and AUCinf) to fosnetupitant increased in a dose-proportional fashion with the increase of fosnetupitant dose.

L2654: ## Sex effect
L2655: Reviewer's comment: The study is not powered to evaluate effect of sex on PK of fosnetupitant. The data are exploratory in nature.
L2656: The descriptive statistics of key PK parameters in males and females following single dose 260 mg IV of fosnetupitant are the followings:
L2657: ## Fosnetupitant
L2658: Cmax and AUC0-inf of pro-netupitant averaged (mean \pm SD) 6456\pm862.5 \mug/L and 2955\pm299.2 h\cdot\mug/L in females, 6416\pm961.3 \mug/L and 2928 \pm401.5 h\cdot\mug/L in males, respectively.
L2659: The elimination efficiency and the volume of distribution, expressed by CL and Vd, were 88.85\pm9.459 L/h and 155.8\pm111.8 L in females, 90.74\pm15.25 L/h and 106.0\pm36.38 L in males, respectively. The terminal half-life was 1.198\pm0.801 h in females and 0.816\pm0.281 h in males.
L2660: Reviewer's comment: PK parameters are comparable between males and females.
L2661: ## Netupitant
L2662: Cmax and AUC0-inf were 922.4\pm167.6 \mug/L and 14216\pm2831 h\cdot\mug/L in females, 793.5\pm161.1 \mug/L and 13643\pm3083 h\cdot\mug/L in males, respectively.
L2663: The terminal half-life was 34.86\pm6.652 h in females, and 36.74\pm6.987 h in males.
L2664: Reviewer's comment: Cmax in males was \sim14% lower than females while the AUC was comparable.
L2665: ## Netupitant metabolites
L2666: Cmax of M1, M2, and M3 averaged 28.91\pm4.337 \mug/L, 186.3\pm56.12 \mug/L and 60.48\pm12.64 \mug/L in females, 24.51\pm4.048 \mug/L, 163.8\pm89.67 \mug/L and 50.33\pm10.27 \mug/L in males, respectively.
L2667: AUC0-inf of M1, M2, and M3 averaged 3802\pm643.3 h\cdot\mug/L, 2920\pm1183 h\cdot\mug/L and 4878\pm1064 h\cdot\mug/L in females, 4226\pm2043 h\cdot\mug/L, 2944\pm1624 h\cdot\mug/L and 3986\pm1149 h\cdot\mug/L in males, respectively.
L2668: Terminal half-lives of M1, M2 and M3 were 77.78\pm21.56 h, 31.75\pm9.975 h and 58.89\pm31.65 h in females, 96.03\pm56.86 h, 38.61\pm30.25 h and 40.62\pm12.12 h in males.

L2689: ## 4.2.2. PK of IV Akynzeo in cancer patients: NEPA-15-19
L2690: **Title:** A Phase 1, open label study to evaluate the pharmacokinetic profile and safety of IV pronetupitant*/palonosetron (260 mg/0.25 mg) fixed-dose combination administered for the prevention of chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting in cancer patients receiving a single cycle of highly emetogenic chemotherapy (* “Pronetupitant” is the new INN “fosnetupitant”)
L2691: **Study Design:** Open label, multicenter study for evaluation of PK profile of a single IV administration of fosnetupitant/ palonosetron (260 mg/0.25 mg) fixed-dose combination (IV NEPA FDC) infused in 30 minutes, in association with oral dexamethasone administered prior to HEC.
L2692: **Treatment product:** IV fosnetupitant 260 mg/palonosetron 0.25 mg fixed-dose combination for infusion. Batch number 238021-01.
L2693: **PK sampling:** Blood samples for the determination of fosnetupitant, netupitant and its metabolites (M1, M2 and M3), and palonosetron concentrations were collected at predose, 30 min (tend), 45 min, 1h, 2h, 3h, 4h, 5h, 6h, 12h, 24h, 48h, 72h, 96h, 144h and 192h after the start of the IV NEPA FDC infusion.
L2694: **Results:**

L2753: ## Sex effect
L2754: Reviewer's comment: The study is not powered to evaluate effect of sex on PK of fosnetupitant. The data are exploratory in nature.
L2755: Mean (SD) Cmax values were 3154.55 (1402.46) ng/mL in males and 3752.31 (1684.43) ng/mL in females, whereas mean (SD) AUC\infty values were 1246.95 (612.00) h*ng/mL in males and 1519.47 (675.57) h*ng/mL in females. The systemic clearance was similar between genders with values of 233.17 (114.3) L/h in males and 261.32 (351.03) L/h in females. The mean (SD) apparent terminal half-life was slightly shorter in males [0.57 (0.29) h] than in females [0.89 (0.43) h] likely due to inter-patient variability, which may also explain differences in the volume of distribution [mean Vss (SD) was 133.51 (64.22) L in males and 198.04 (360.94) L in females].
L2756: Netupitant PK after IV fosnetupitant/palonosetron (260 mg/0.25 mg) fixed-dose combination administration was similar in males and females. Mean (SD) Cmax values were 567.09 (174.25) ng/mL in males and 608.54 (161.16) ng/mL in females, whereas mean (SD) AUC\infty values were 15672.01 (5495.62) h*ng/mL in males and 15517.83 (4813.95) h*ng/mL in females. The apparent terminal half-life was 147.18 (96.64) h in males and 140.80 (47.88) h in females.
L2757: ## 4.2.3. *In vivo drug interaction study : PNET-13-63*
L2758: Title: Evaluation of pharmacokinetic interaction between three doses of intravenous pro-netupitant and oral dexamethasone regimen: a randomized three period crossover study in healthy male and female volunteers
L2759: Study Design: This was a 3-period, 4-treatment, open-label, crossover study to evaluate the effect of single dose of IV fosnetupitant on the PK of oral dexamethasone given for 4 days. Each treatment period consists of 7 days and separated by a wash-out phase of at 28 days.
L2760: Period 1	Period 2	Period 3
Sequence 1	A	B	C
Sequence 2	B	D	A
Sequence 3	C	A	D
A: oral dexamethasone plus 5% glucose solution infusion
B: oral dexamethasone plus IV pro-netupitant 130 mg (2.6 mg/mL)
C: oral dexamethasone plus IV pro-netupitant 195 mg (3.9 mg/mL)
D: oral dexamethasone plus IV pro-netupitant 260 mg (5.2 mg/mL)
L2761: ## Treatment groups:
L2762: Dexamethasone: 20 mg PO x1 on day 1 → 8 mg PO BID on Days 2, 3, and 4.
L2763: Fosnetupitant: 130 mg, 195 mg, 260 mg single dose on Day 1.
L2764: Treatment	Study drug	Mode of administration	Dose per treatment day	Duration of treatment	Number of tablets per day (Posology†) or Volume for infusion (Duration of infusion)	Total Dose

L2767: fosnetupitant, netupitant and netupitant metabolites: blood samples were taken at pre-dose, 30 min, 45 min, then 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48, 60, 72, 84, 96 and 120 hours after start of infusion (not applicable for Treatment A).

L2797: ## Conclusions:
L2798: - On Day 1, the point estimates of treatment comparisons for dose-normalized dexamethasone AUC and Cmin increased remarkably with increasing fosnetupitant doses. At the dose of 260 mg fosnetupitant, dexamethasone AUC0-24h and Cmin increased approximately of 1.5- and 5.2-folds, respectively, as compared to the parameter values observed with dexamethasone given alone. On Day 1, dose normalized dexamethasone Cmax did not change appreciably with fosnetupitant co-administration. The 90% confidence intervals for dexamethasone Cmax fell within the standard bioequivalence interval 80.00%-125.00%.
L2799: - On Day 4, the point estimates of treatment comparisons for dose-normalized dexamethasone AUC, Cmax and Cmin indicated that dexamethasone PK was still altered by the metabolic interaction with fosnetupitant. At the dose of 260 mg fosnetupitant, dose-normalized dexamethasone AUC0-24h, Cmax and Cmin increased approximately of 2.5, 1.7 and 3.2 folds, respectively, as compared to the parameter values observed with dexamethasone given alone.

L2807: ## Vesicular transporter inhibition
L2808: **Test system for vesicular (VT) transport inhibition:** Vesicular transport assays were inside-out membrane vesicles prepared from cells overexpressing human ABC transporters. The transporters have been expressed by SOLVO Biotechnology in insect (Trichoplusia ni [Hi5] ovarian cells) and mammalian (K and M) cells. Hi5 cells were infected with recombinant baculovirus as a means of achieving the overexpression of BSEP ABC transporter. The mammalian cells were chemically selected for BCRP or MDR1 ABC transporters.

L2835: ## Conclusion - inhibition:
L2836: - Fosnetupitant is an inhibitor of the MATE1-mediated metformin transport (IC50: 29.48 \muM), the OATP1B1-mediated E217\betaG transport (IC50: 19.01 \muM) and the OATP1B3-mediated CCK-8 transport (IC50: 17.37 \muM).
- Fosnetupitant is a weak inhibitor of OCT1-mediated metformin transport, with the maximum inhibition of 44 % at 300 \muM.
- Fosnetupitant did **not** inhibit the OAT1-mediated tenofovir transport, the OAT3-mediated methotrexate transport and the OCT2-mediated metformin transport up to 300 \muM.

L2841: ## *In vitro CYP P450 inhibition potential of fosnetupitant: PNET-15-49*
L2842: **Title:** Pronetupitant Phosphate: Determination of the Potential Inhibition (IC_{50}) of the Main Isoforms of CYP450, Determination of Microsomes Binding and Determination of Microsomes Stability.
L2843: ## Study Design
L2844: The concentrations of fosnetupitant were 0.3, 1, 3, 10, 30, 100, 300, and 1000 \muM.
L2845: The test system was pooled human liver microsome (HLM). A pool of microsomes prepared from 50 individual donors were used.
L2846: The quantity of CYP specific substrate metabolites produced during the incubation with and without the compound was evaluated in triplicate. In addition, positive controls with CYP specific isoform inhibitors, were included into each experiment. All incubations were performed under linear conditions with respect to time, protein concentration and amount of product formed.
L2847: Individual CYP enzyme testing conditions are provided below.
L2848: **CYP1A2 incubation:** the substrate was Tacrine 6 \muM, the positive control was \alpha-Naphthoflavone (0.01, 0.1, 0.3 \muM), the protein concentration was 0.1 mg/mL. The incubation was carried out at 37°C, shaking, for 10 minutes.
L2849: **CYP2B6 incubation:** Bupropion 43 \muM was used as probe substrate solution and Ticlopidine (0.1, 1, 3 \muM) as positive control with the protein concentration of 0.1 mg/mL. The incubation was carried out, at 37°C, shaking, for 20 minutes.
L2850: **CYP2C8 incubation:** the substrate was Paclitaxel 14 \muM, the positive control Quercetine (0.6, 6, 18 \muM), the protein concentration was 0.1 mg/mL. The incubation was carried out at 37°C, shaking for 20 minutes.
L2851: **CYP2C9 incubation:** the substrate was Diclofenac 7 \muM, the positive control was Sulfaphenazole (0.1, 1, 3 \muM), the protein concentration was 0.1 mg/mL and the incubation was performed at 37°C, shaking, for 10 minutes.
L2852: **CYP2C19 incubation:** S-mephentyoin 71 \muM was used as probe substrate and Ticlopidine (0.1, 1, 3 \muM) as positive control with protein concentration of 0.2 mg/mL. The incubation was carried out at 37°C, shaking for 40 minutes.
L2853: **CYP2D6 incubation:** the substrate was Dextromethorphan 5 \muM, the positive control was Quinidine (0.01, 0.1, 0.3 \muM), the protein concentration was 0.1 mg/mL and the incubation was performed at 37°C, shaking, for 10 minutes.
L2854: CYP3A4 (Midazolam) incubation: the probe substrate was Midazolam 2.1 \muM, the positive control was Ketoconazole (0.01, 0.1, 0.3 \muM). The protein concentration was 0.1 mg/mL. The incubation was carried out at 37°C, shaking, for 10 minutes.
L2855: CYP3A4 (testosterone) incubation: the probe substrate was Testosterone 43 \muM, the positive control Ketoconazole (0.01, 0.1, 0.3 \muM) and the protein concentration was 0.1 mg/mL. The incubation was carried out at 37°C, shaking for 20 minutes.
L2856: The reaction was started by addition of 1 mM NADPH. After the reported incubation time, reactions were stopped by adding ice-cold acetonitrile.

L2862: ## Conclusions
L2863: In the range of concentrations tested (0.3-1000 \muM), pronetupitant showed moderate to weak inhibition potential towards CYP2C9 (IC50 = 27.9 \muM), CYP2C19 (IC50 = 46.1 \muM), CYP2C8 (IC50 = 56.7 \muM), CYP2B6 (IC50 = 106.8 \muM), CYP1A2 (IC50 = 208.4 \muM), CYP2D6 (IC50 = 217.2 \muM) and CYP3A4 (IC50 = 196.8 \muM and 299.2 with midazolam and testosterone substrates, respectively).
L2864: *Reviewer's comment: Refer to Section 4.3 for evaluation of in vivo DDI potential using PBPK.*
L2865: ## *In vitro time-dependent inhibition potential of CYP3A and CYP2C9: PNET-15-81*
L2866: **Title:** Pronetupitant Phosphate: Determination of the potential Time-Dependent Inhibition of CYP3A4 and CYP2C9
L2867: ## Study Design
L2868: Pooled human liver microsomes (HLM) used in this study. A pool of microsomes prepared from 50 individual donors was used.
L2869: Verapamil (CYP3A4) and tienilic acid (CYP2C9) were used as positive controls in the assay.
L2870: In the first step, the pre-incubation step, pronetupitant (0.3, 1, 3, 10, 30, 100 and 300 \muM), verapamil (0.25, 2.5, 5, 15, 50 and 150 \muM), tienilic acid (0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, \muM) and the solvent control (i.e. absence of compound) were incubated up to 30 minutes in buffer at the microsomal protein concentration of 1 mg/mL.
L2871: In the second step, at different times (0, 5, 10, 20 and 30 minutes) an aliquot of the preincubation mixtures was diluted 10-fold in a buffer mixture containing specific substrates of CYP3A4 and CYP2C9 (midazolam, 8.4 \muM and diclofenac, 28 \muM, respectively). These incubation mixtures, with a final microsomes concentration of 0.1 mg/mL, were added with NADPH (2 mM).
L2872: After 10 minutes shaking at 37°C the incubation reactions were stopped by adding ice-cold acetonitrile.
L2873: The quantity of metabolites produced during incubation (1-HO-midazolam or 4-HO-diclofenac) was evaluated in triplicate.
L2874: Fosnetupitant of 1000 \muM dose: Time dependent inhibition potential of CYP3A and was also determined.
L2875: **Results:** An IC50 shift (ratio of the IC50s at t=0 and t=30 min) of &gt; 1.5 with a 30 minutes of pre-incubation time is considered as time-dependent inhibitors. IC50 shift was determined for concentration up to 300 \muM.
L2876: **Table 44. CYP3A4 IC50s of pronetupitant and verapamil after a pre-incubation time of 0, 5, 10, 20 and 30 minutes and the IC50 shift ratios estimated after 30 minutes of pre-incubation.**

L2886: Reviewer's comment: This concentration (Fosnetupitant of 1000 \muM) data point was studied together with other concentration time points (fonetupitant 0 – 300 \muM) with the same positive controls for CYP3A and CYP2C9. However, data generated from 1000 \muM was not used to calculate IC50 values and IC50 shift.
L2887: The sponsor stated that including the inhibition data obtained with all the fosnetupitant concentrations tested (0.3 - 1000 \muM), the calculation of the IC50s using Graph Pad Prism software was unreliable (CYP3A4) or even impossible (CYP2C9). Refer to Response to IR (SDN28). Given that a) no TDI observed from 0-300 \muM range and b) 1000 \muM is 100X the Cmax obtained from proposed therapeutic dose, the sponsor's justification is reasonable.
L2888: ## Conclusions
L2889: In the concentrations range from 0.3 to 300 \muM, up to 30 minutes of pre-incubation time, pronetupitant did not show potential TDI effect towards CYP3A4 and CYP2C9. At 1000 \muM, pronetupitant showed time-dependent inhibition.
L2890: Reviewer's comment: 1000 \muM is about 100 times the Cmax (9.2 \muM) achieved in humans following single dose of IV infusion. Thus, time-dependent inhibition of CYP3A and CYP2C9 is minimal at the therapeutic dose.

L2906: **Test system for MDCKII monolayer assay for BCRP or MDR1:** The monolayer assays were performed using parental and BCRP or MDR1 transfected MDCKII cell monolayers. MDCKII, MDCKII-BCRP and MDCKII-MDR1 cells were cultured in Dulbecco's Modified Eagle's Medium with 4.5 g/L glucose (DMEM) supplemented with 10% (v/v) fetal bovine serum (FBS) at 37 ± 1 °C in an atmosphere of 95:5 air: CO2 in cell culture flasks prior to seeding into 24-transwell inserts. Transfected and parental MDCKII cells were cultured on the inserts with 400 µL medium per well on the apical side and 25 mL in a single-well receiver tray for all 24 wells on the basolateral side, for 96 hours. Medium was changed 24 hours before the experiment.
L2907: Experimental method for bidirectional transport (substrate assessment): Bidirectional transport of palonosetron was determined through parental, MDCKII-BCRP and MDCKII-MDR1 cell monolayers. Cells were preincubated in assay buffer for 10 minutes to allow cells adjusting to the medium. Assay buffer containing palonosetron at four concentrations (0.05, 0.5, 5 and 50 µM) was then added to the
L2908: appropriate apical (400 \muL) or basolateral chamber (800 \muL). The prazosin or digoxin efflux ratio was determined as a positive control for BCRP or MDR1 function.

L2915: ## Controls:
L2916: - TEER values were determined for each well prior to the experiment with a Volt Ohmmeter (Millicell, Millipore, Billerica MA, or equivalent). Values above 120 \Omegacm^2/plate were accepted.
- Monolayer integrity markers included the measurement of LY as low permeability compound. LY values were accepted below 2 \times 10^{-6} cm/s. Antipyrine served as a high permeability marker. The antipyrine values were expected to be around 50 \times 10^{-6} cm/s.
- The efflux ratio of digoxin (positive control for MDR1-mediated active efflux) was accepted when above 3 for the digoxin control. The efflux ratio of digoxin in the presence of the reference inhibitor PSC833 (10 \muM) or verapamil (100 \muM) was reduced by &gt; 80 %.
- The efflux ratio of prazosin (positive control for BCRP-mediated active efflux) accepted when above 3 for the prazosin control. The efflux ratio of prazosin in the presence of the reference inhibitor Ko134 (1 \muM) was reduced by &gt; 80 %.

L2920: ## **Conclusions – Palonosetron as an inhibitor**
L2921: - Palonosetron is an inhibitor of MATE1, MATE2-K, OCT1, OCT2, OAT3 in a dose-dependent manner.
- Palonosetron (100 µM) inhibited 30% the OATP1B3-mediated CCK-8 transport.
- Palonosetron up to 100 µM is **not** an inhibitor of BCRP, BSEP, MDR1, OAT1, OATP1B1.
L2922: Reviewer's comment: In vivo inhibition of MATE1 and MATE2-K by palonosetron is possible as the ratio of \frac{C_{max,u}}{IC_{50}} for MATE1 and MATE2-K is 0.35 and 0.076, respectively. Both ratios are greater than 0.02. The Cmax of 0.78 ng/mL (2.3 nM) following IV infusion of Akyzneo in cancer patients and fu of 0.38 is used for the calculation.
L2923: In vivo inhibition of OCT1, OCT2, and OAT3 is unlikely as the ratios of \frac{C_{max,u}}{IC_{50}} for these transporters are &lt; 0.1. In vivo inhibition of OATP1B3 is also unlikely as \frac{C_{max}}{IC_{50}} &lt; 0.1 assuming IC50 is 100 µM, the worst-case scenario.
L2924: Since palonosetron has been on the market quite for a while without any major safety concerns, an in vivo study to evaluate the inhibition potential of palonosetron towards MATE1 and MATE2-K is unnecessary. Therefore, a PMC is not issued regarding this.
L2925: ## Conclusions – Palonosetron as a substrate
L2926: - Palonosetron is a substrate of MDR1 (p-gp).
- Palonosetron is **not** a substrate of BCRP.
- Palonosetron is **not** a substrate of MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 uptake transporters.
L2927: Reviewer's comment: Because of IV administration, systemic exposure to palonosetron will not be impacted due to inhibition of MDR1 transporters. The DDI potential between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates in the GI tract is minimal because fosnetupitant is administered intravenously and eliminated quickly from the systemic circulation. The ratio of the estimated [Iu,in,max] and reported IC_{50} for P-gp was less than 0.1, where Iu,in,max is the estimated maximum unbound concentration of fosnetupitant at the inlet to the liver, suggesting no DDI potential between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates in the liver.

L2931: ## 4.3. Review of Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Modeling (PBPK):
## PNET-16-29
L2932: ## Objectives
L2933: The applicant submitted study report PNET-16-29 entitled ‘quantitative prediction of the potential for pro-netupitant as a perpetrator to cause CYP2C and transporter-mediated drug-drug interactions’ to support the following labeling claim.
L2934: (b) (4)
L2935: Specifically, the applicant applied physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modelling approach to predict in vivo drug-drug interactions (DDIs) between fosnetupitant (also known as pro-netupitant, the perpetrator, IV 260 mg over 30 min) and the following substrates (victims).
L2936: - CYP2C19 substrate: omeprazole
- CYP2C9 substrate: S-warfarin
- OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 substrate: rosuvastatin
- P-gp substrate: digoxin
L2937: On 9/27/2017, the applicant submitted supporting files for the physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) model development and model performance assessment for CYP2C9 substrate (S-warfarin), OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 substrate (rosuvastatin), and P-gp substrate (digoxin) (Sequence # 0020).
L2938: On 11/9/2017, the applicant submitted re-evaluation of the in vivo drug-drug interaction (DDI) potential between fosnetupitant and CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 isoforms, and P-gp, OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 transporters using basic models recommended in the draft guidance published in October 20172 (Sequence # 0028).
L2939: This review aims to evaluate the adequacy of the applicant’s modelling approaches.
L2940: ## Recommendation based on PBPK modeling review
L2941: Based on the prediction using basic model approach, the in vivo DDI potential could not be ruled out. Nevertheless, PBPK modeling approach predicted no significant in vivo DDI potential between fosnetupitant and CYP2C9 or CYP2C19 substrates. For transporters, DDIs between fosnetupitant and OATP1B1, OATP1B3, or P-gp substrates are not expected based on both basic model approach and PBPK modeling approach.
L2942: - Based on the basic model recommended in the draft guidance3, the calculated R1 values were 1.04 for CYP2C9 and 1.03 for CYP2C19. Both were slightly higher than the predefined cutoff value of 1.024. The PBPK modeling approach suggested no in vivo pharmacokinetic interaction between fosnetupitant and S-warfarin (CYP2C9 substrate) as well as between fosnetupitant and
L2943: 2 Draft guidance for industry (2017) In Vitro Metabolism- and Transporter- Mediated Drug-Drug Interaction Studies Guidance for Industry

L2948: omeprazole (CYP2C19 substrate) at the recommended dose level (260 mg IV infusion for 30 min).
L2949: - For OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 inhibition potential estimation, the calculated R values5 were 1.087 for OATP1B1 and 1.095 for OATP1B3. Both were slightly below the cutoff value of 1.16. PBPK modeling approach with sensitivity analysis also suggested negligible in vivo DDIs.
- The DDI potential between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates in the GI tract is minimal because fosnetupitant is administered intravenously and eliminated quickly from the systemic circulation. The ratio of the estimated [I_{u,in,max}] and reported IC50 for P-gp was less than 0.1, where I_{u,in,max} is the estimated maximum unbound concentration of fosnetupitant at the inlet to the liver7, suggesting no DDI potential between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates in the liver.
L2950: ## Background
L2951: The investigational product is an intravenous fixed dose combination of fosnetupitant and palonosetron HCl (IV NEPA FDC). Fosnetupitant is a water-soluble phosphorylated pro-drug of netupitant rapidly converted to netupitant in vivo following IV administration with mean half-life of approximately 1 hour. The proposed dose of fosnetupitant is 235 mg (equivalent to 260 mg fosnetupitant chloride) IV infusion over 30 min.
L2952: In this submission, the applicant applied physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modeling approach to predict in vivo DDIs between fosnetupitant (also known as pro-netupitant, the perpetrator, IV 260 mg over 30 min) and CYP2C9, CYP2C19, OATP1B1, OATP1B3 and P-gp substrates.
L2953: ## Model Development
L2954: The models were developed in Simcyp (version 15). A flow chart of modeling strategy is summarized below by the reviewer. The applicant's modeling strategy is appropriate.
L2955: Model structures for each compound are summarized below.

L2961: ## Question based review
L2962: When using PBPK modeling to predict the interaction potential of fosnetupitant as an enzyme and transporter perpetrator, the following questions were considered13.
L2963: - Can the PBPK model of fosnetupitant (the investigational perpetrator) describe the available clinical PK data using different dosing regimens?
L2964: Yes, the proposed fosnetupitant model (a minimal PBPK with a single adjusting compartment (SAC)) can describe the plasma concentration of fosnetupitant at dose levels ranging from 19.5 mg to 390 mg. The applicant verified the simulated PK of fosnetupitant against observed data at a dose level of 260 mg (Figure 20). It was noted that 260 mg was used in the simulation while the proposed recommended dose was 235 mg fosnetupitant (equivalent to 260 mg fosnetupitant chloride), and the model input should be using the corresponding base dose. Nevertheless, the simulated mean Cmax (6643 ng/mL) and AUCt (3526 ng*hr/mL) using a dose of 235 mg were closer to the observed Cmax (mean values ranging from 6414 – 6464 ng/mL, PNET-12-23), and AUCt (ranging from 2922-2939 ng*hr/mL, PNET-12-23) compared to the simulated values using a dose of 260 mg (Figure 20). The observed fosnetupitant Cmax and AUCt were 7367 ng/mL, and 2855 ng*hr/mL, respectively in Study PNET-13-63 (fosnetupitant drug-drug interaction with oral dexamethasone).
L2965: Figure 20: Observed (circle) vs. simulated (solid line) fosnetupitant PK profiles after 260 mg iv infusion over 30 min (source: Figures 3 and 5 in study report PNET-16-29). Left: simulated mean concentration-time profiles (solid lines) vs. mean observed data (solid circles; n=17; Clinical Study PNET-13-63); right: simulated mean concentration-time profile (solid lines) and 5th and 95th percentiles (dashed lines) for the simulated population vs. observed individual concentrations (symbols; n=17; Clinical Study PNET-13-63)

L2977: ## 2. Are index substrate models (CYP 2C9 and CYP2C19) verified with regard to the effect of altered enzyme activity on its PK in humans?
L2978: Yes. S-warfarin and omeprazole are in vivo index substrates of CYP 2C9 and CYP2C19, respectively14. The applicant verified the index substrate drugs by 1) comparison of simulated vs. observed PK profiles, and 2) comparison of the simulated vs. observed fold change in PK parameters in the absence and presence of other index modulators: fluconazole (a moderate CYP2C9 index inhibitor), and fluvoxamine (a strong CYP2C19 index inhibitor). It was noted that the dose used for fosneupitant simulation input was 260 mg and the proposed recommended dose was 235 mg fosnetupitant. The model input should be using the corresponding base dose. Nevertheless, the difference will not affect the DDIs conclusions, as the doses used in simulation was higher than the corresponding base dose and simulated mean PK values tend to be higher than observed mean PK values.
L2979: S-warfarin clearance was modeled by CYP2C9 allelic variants and verified against published in vivo PK data. The DDI model predicted warfarin AUC changes in the absence and presence of fluconazole (a moderate CYP2C9 index inhibitor) were 69%-120% higher than observed AUC changes15. The uncertainty associated with the Ki (inhibition constant) values were addressed by sensitivity analysis (Figure 23).
L2980: Figure 23: Sensitivity analysis to assess the impact of hepatic uptake and CYP2C9 Ki values for fosnetupitant on the predicted DDI with S-warfarin after coadministration of a single oral dose of 10 mg S-warfarin and a single 30-min IV infusion of 260 mg fosnetupitant (source: Figure 9 in study report PNET-16-29)
L2981: The contribution of CYP2C19 (fmCYP2C19) to the overall clearance of omeprazole in CYP2C19 extensive metabolizer (EM) in vivo was assigned based on literature report (0.80-0.87). In order to describe the observed DDI between fluvoxamine (a strong CYP2C19 index inhibitor) and omeprazole, a Ki values 5-fold lower than the in vitro value was used in the simulation. Nevertheless, sensitivity

L2986: ## 3. Can the proposed PBPK model be used to predict the DDIs between fosnetupitant and OATP1B1, and OATP1B3 substrates?
L2987: The basic model approach (Sequence # 0028) suggested no in vivo inhibition potential for OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 substrates. In vivo DDI prediction between fosnetupitant and rosuvastatin (OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 substrate) suggested that there is negligible in vivo inhibition potential.
L2988: The clearance (Cl_{int,T}) assigned to each transporter (49% for OATP1B1, 35% for NTCP, and the remaining 16% for OATP1B3)16 as well as their K_i values may impact the prediction of the DDI between fosnetupitant and rosuvastatin. The applicant evaluated the impact of K_i values on DDI prediction but not Cl_{int,T}. The reviewer performed additional simulation to mimic a worst-case scenario where only OATP1B3 (100%) was responsible for uptake as it has lower K_i value, and K_i value was 100-fold lower than the in vitro value (0.087 \mu M). The predicted ratios of rosuvastatin C_{max} and AUC were about 10% and 1% higher, respectively, when it was coadministered with fosnetupitant, suggesting negligible DDI between fosnetupitant and
L2989: 16 Page 8 in Application 210493 - Sequence 0020 - Performance Verification Rosuvastatin - V15
L2990: OATP1B1, and OATP1B3 substrates. As both OATP1B1, and OATP1B3 are influx transporters, inhibition effect does not raise the concern of potentially more liver accumulation.
L2991: ## 4. Can the proposed PBPK model be used to predict the DDIs between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates?
L2992: In order to simulate the DDI between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates in the intestine, a physiologically based model is needed to estimate the fosnetupitant concentration in enterocytes coming from the blood flow. The current fosnetupitant model does not include a physiologically based gastrointestinal tract. Nevertheless, the impact of fosnetupitant on digoxin intestinal efflux should be minimal because fosnetupitant is administered intravenously and eliminated from systemic circulation quickly.
L2993: The ratio of the estimated [I_{u,in,max}] and reported IC_{50} for P-gp was less than 0.1, where I_{u,in,max} is the estimated maximum unbound concentration of fosnetupitant at the inlet to the liver, suggesting no DDI potential between fosnetupitant and P-gp substrates in the liver.
L2994: ## Conclusions
L2995: - The PBPK modeling approach is appropriate to address the DDIs between fosnetupitant and CYP2C9, CYP2C19 substrates. Therefore, in vivo pharmacokinetic DDIs between fosnetupitant and CYP2C9, CYP2C19 substrates are not expected.
- In vivo DDIs between fosnetupitant and OATP1B1, OATP1B3, or P-gp substrates are not expected based on basic model approach and PBPK modeling approach.

L3008: Figure 3 is a schematic diagram illustrating the study design (PNET-12-23) across three phases: SAD / SAD-CO, P-CO, and F-CO. The design is structured around dose levels and administration routes (IV or PO).
SAD / SAD-CO Phase (Ascending Dose):
Dose levels: 19.5 mg, 32.5 mg, 65.0 mg, 130.0 mg, 195.0 mg, 260.0 mg, 325.0 mg, 390.0 mg.
For 19.5 mg, 32.5 mg, and 65.0 mg: N=8 (IV) and N=2 (PO).
For 130.0 mg, 195.0 mg, 260.0 mg, 325.0 mg, and 390.0 mg: N=8 (IV), N=2 (PO), and N=2 (PO/IV crossover, marked with X).
P-CO Phase (Pilot Crossover):
Dose levels: 195.0 mg and 260.0 mg.
For both 195.0 mg and 260.0 mg: N=10 (IV), N=10 (PO), and N=10 (PO/IV crossover, marked with X).
F-CO Phase (Final Crossover):
Dose levels: 234.0 mg and 286.0 mg.
For both 234.0 mg and 286.0 mg: N=10 (IV), N=10 (PO), and N=10 (PO/IV crossover, marked with X).
Monitoring and Analysis Points:
DSMB (Drug Safety Monitoring Board) is associated with the SAD / SAD-CO phase.
PK analyses for F_{rel,IV} estimation are associated with the P-CO and F-CO phases.

L3010: Chemical structure of methylene phosphate netupitant (top).
Hydrolysis converts methylene phosphate netupitant to RO0673189.
RO0673189 undergoes three metabolic pathways:
Demethylation yields RO0681133 (M1).
Oxidation yields RO0713001 (M2).
Hydroxylation yields RO0731519 (M3).
L3011: Figure 1 illustrates the proposed metabolic pathway of Fosnetupitant chloride hydrochloride in humans. The pathway shows the conversion of Fosnetupitant chloride hydrochloride into various metabolites (M1, M2, M3, M4, M5, M6, M7, M8) via enzymatic reactions.
Fosnetupitant chloride hydrochloride undergoes Complete non-CYP450 enzymatic hydrolysis (e.g., phosphatases) to yield M1 (major metabolite).
M1 (major metabolite) undergoes Oxidation to yield M5 & M6 (traces).
Fosnetupitant chloride hydrochloride undergoes N-demethylation (CYP3A4) to yield Netupitant.
Netupitant undergoes Oxidation to yield M4 (minor metabolite).
Netupitant undergoes N-Oxidation (CYP3A4) to yield M2 (major metabolite).
Netupitant undergoes Hydroxylation (CYP3A4) to yield M3 (major metabolite).
M2 (major metabolite) undergoes Oxidation & de-hydrogenation to yield M7 & M8 (traces).

L3034: Flow chart illustrating the modeling strategy:
Model development and verification for fosnetupitant (the perpetrator) leads to Model development and verification for the victim drugs (omeprazole/CYP2C19, s-warfarin/CYP2C9, rosuvastatin/OATP1B1 and OATP1B3, and digoxin/p-gp).
Model development and verification for the victim drugs leads to DDI simulation based on in vitro data.
DDI simulation based on in vitro data leads to Sensitivity analysis on Ki (10-folder lower) and transporter-mediated hepatic uptake of fosnetupitant.
L3035: Figure 21 displays two graphs comparing observed and simulated systemic concentration profiles of fosnetupitant over time (h) for various doses.
The left graph shows Systemic Concentration (ng/mL) versus Time (h). Multiple curves illustrate the concentration profile over 2.0 hours, peaking around 0.5 hours. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 10,000 ng/mL.
The right graph shows Systemic Concentration (ng/mL) versus Time (h) over 3 hours. It compares observed data points (colored dots) with simulated profiles (solid and dotted lines). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 12,000 ng/mL.
L3036: Figure 22 displays two scatter plots showing the relationship between fosnetupitant chloride dose (mg) on the X-axis (0 to 400 mg) and mean PK values on the Y-axis.
The left plot shows Cmax (ng/mL) versus dose. Observed Cmax (blue dots) and Simulated Cmax (red dots) both increase with dose, ranging from 0 to 10,000 ng/mL.
The right plot shows AUCt (ng*hr/mL) versus dose. Observed AUCt (blue dots) and Simulated AUCt (red dots) both increase with dose, ranging from 0 to 6,000 ng*hr/mL.
L3037: The figure displays the ratios of simulated fosnetupitant mean PK values divided by observed fosnetupitant mean PK values (Predicted / observed) versus fosnetupitant chloride dose (mg) (0 to 400 mg).
The Y-axis ranges from 0.80 to 1.40. The ratios for AUCt (blue dots) and Cmax (red dots) generally cluster between 0.90 and 1.30, supporting the text's claim of a ratio range of ~0.9-1.3.
